# AISEF v2.3 — W0 CODE AUDIT

Source audit on `release/v2.3-big` (local only — security findings, owner ruling D): the W0 parallel audit (`wi04fhzj6`, 15 subsystem lanes, defects #1–#45) and the W0-closure gap-fill (`wtyqyc77n`, 5 lanes over the surfaces the first critic named unread: plan/admission, quality/anti-gaming, authority checkers, lineage+cohort, secondary overlaps — defects #46–#104). Every defect has a verified source location, impact, adjudication (PRODUCT_DEFECT / TEST_DEFECT / ENVIRONMENT / STALE_RECORD / EQUIVALENT / ACCEPTED_LIMITATION / UNKNOWN), duplicate-of, wave + lane (`DEPENDENCY-DAG.md` §4 ownership) and a testable acceptance criterion. Every gap-fill P1 was adversarially verified (default: refute). None is fixed yet.

**Totals:** 104 defects (106 rows: #6 and #39 are split a/b, one wave each; severity counts are per row) — P1: 3, P2: 43, P3: 60. Adjudication: ACCEPTED_LIMITATION 7, ENVIRONMENT 1, EQUIVALENT 2, PRODUCT_DEFECT 64, STALE_RECORD 3, TEST_DEFECT 25, UNKNOWN 4. Waves: W1 30, W1b 2, W2 21, W3 30, W4 8, W5 12, W6 3.

## Impact and acceptance criterion per defect

**#1 [P1] ci/release** — `.github/workflows/release.yml:73-97 (environment: pypi at :76); live GET repos/nghinh/aisef/environments/pypi -> protection_rules:[], deployment_branch_policy:null (re-read 2026-11-21)`  
*Impact:* Irreversible production publish with no interactive owner gate — the charter §19/§2 (RELEASE-CHARTER.md:44-45) intended stop does not exist. In-flight 2.2.1 (ledger seq 13 AUTHORIZED) will auto-publish [W0-freeze: historical; 2.2.1 abandoned, its seq 13 withdrawn; ledger holds seq 1–12; the v2.3 ROUND-AUTH seq 13 is a draft only, docs/v2.3/drafts/LEDGER-SEQ-13-DRAFT.md]; a future v2.3.0 auto-publishes the moment V2.3.0-STATUS.json=PASS and a ledger AUTHORIZED 2.3.0 entry exist. Remediation is a repo-settings change (owner/admin), not YAML; held per directive §D.  
*Acceptance:* Owner/admin adds a Required reviewer, and optionally restricts deployments to v*.*.* tags. Then `gh api repos/nghinh/aisef/environments/pypi` must show a required_reviewers rule naming the owner. A W1 read-only governance-guard job runs in release.yml before `publish` and fails when protection_rules has no required_reviewers. Its negative test feeds a mocked `[]` response and expects FAIL; a mocked rule with a reviewer expects PASS. Before W6 publication, a dry-run must show the `publish` job in status:waiting, and the owner rejects it. Also add a REL-GOV-01 row to MASTER-CHECKLIST.md: RELEASE-GOVERNANCE-FINDING.md:49 says the row exists, but grep finds 0 rows.

**#2 [P1] orchestrate** — `aisef2/orchestrate/story_runner.py:183-370 (the try at :197 covers only resource acquisition at :198-203; the pipeline at :204-350 is unguarded; STORY_DISPOSE + scope.dispose() + STORY_END at :364-36…`  
*Impact:* A single story's unexpected fault escalates to the whole run: the StoryScope is not disposed here (worktrees/scratch leak unless the run-level interrupt later disposes them), STORY_DISPOSE/STORY_END are never journaled so the story stays logically open, and a clean shutdown() is then refused (run_scope.py:184-187 ShutdownRefused) — the run can only end via interrupt(), leaving the sentinel OPEN so the next preflight reports TORN. Fault isolation (the stage order encodes invariant IX) is defeated. This is exactly the hole roadmap E5 is scoped to close; E5 is still OPEN (Đợt 4, unchecked) so the defect ships in the v2.3 W0 baseline.  
*Acceptance:* Fault-injection unit tests drive run_story with each of these raising mid-attempt: Scanner.read() raising FileNotFoundError, prove()/reprove() raising InvariantError, quality.assess raising OSError, and merger.revert raising Residual. For every case: the journal holds STORY_DISPOSE followed by STORY_END for that attempt; scope.dispose() was called (no leftover worktree or scratch); a FAILURE_OBSERVED is journaled with a typed code (UNKNOWN or INTEGRATION) and the original type kept as data; run_scope.open_stories() is empty; RunScope.shutdown() does not raise ShutdownRefused. Run targeted mutation on the story_runner.py targets and the existing E5 typed Stage sequence; both must be green. Land this before G6, C5/G9 and C1. **[W0-freeze correction: the acceptance is the W1-DESIGN §4.5 fault matrix (F1–F25, G1–G2, R1). Contained: Exception faults before the decision while the repository state is clean/landed. Kept on the interrupt path by design (declared limits §4.8): faults while the repository state is unknown (H3), at/after DECIDE (H5), and every BaseException incl. InvariantError raised by prove()/reprove() (H8) — there open_stories() is non-empty until RunScope.interrupt.]**

**#3 [P1] qualification/release** — `validation/qualification/p5_acceptance.py:896-898; root cause validation/qualification/release_calibration.py:57 (RECORD_REL = newest_rel(_version())[0])`  
*Impact:* release_calibration.RECORD_REL = newest_rel(_version())[0] is derived from pyproject (now 2.2.1), so it resolves to closure-evidence/v2/release/V2.2.1-PROBE-CALIBRATIONS.json, which does not exist (only V2.2.0-PROBE-CALIBRATIONS.json does). reverify() (both rounds) then emits a spurious 'V2.2.1-PROBE-CALIBRATIONS.json is missing' and v21_conformance_ruling.derive() (:402) yields 'stale: identity.behaviour_evidence.probe_calibrations'. The committed v2.2 reverification record points at V2.2.0 and can no longer re-derive. aisef2/app/calibrations.py bytes are unchanged since the v2.2 records (sha 14285dcf…), so this is purely the version-pointer coupling, not a real calibration change. tests.v2.test_c2_p5_acceptance.V21Records has 2 non-gated failures (sealing_gate: a red non-gated test is a real finding); the `unittest discover` job in tests.yml:59 runs on every commit, so the suite is red on the release branch; p5_acceptance.py --check-v22 and v21_conformance_ruling.py --check-v22 both FAIL. Fix: write the V2.2.1 calibration record (identical bytes to V2.2.0's) or pin the pointer to CURRENT_ROUND rather than the pyproject version.  
*Acceptance:* Observed now: at 1bf5dd2f pyproject.toml:12 says 2.2.0, and rc.RECORD_REL resolves to an existing closure-evidence/v2/release/V2.2.0-PROBE-CALIBRATIONS.json. So the audit's RED does not reproduce, but the version-pointer coupling at :57 is still there. Fixed means the following holds in a scratch COPY tree. Bump pyproject to a code-identical patch version. Do not write a new calibration file and leave aisef2/app/calibrations.py bytes unchanged. Then `python3 -m unittest tests.v2.test_c2_p5_acceptance`, `p5_acceptance.py --check-v22` and `v21_conformance_ruling.py --check-v22` all pass, through a typed VERIFIED_REUSE re-point of the calibration record (kernel digest unchanged, never shown as MEASURED). Negative cases: a tampered calibration record, or a changed calibrations.py, gives INVALIDATED / FAIL.

**#4 [P2] adapters/grade** — `aisef2/app/review_verify.py:300 (keys exclude within_s); docstring :48-51 still calls this an open owner decision`  
*Impact:* False-blocking finding: the agent-independent gate can reject a correct implementation on a timing window the reviewer, not the clause, chose. Self-acknowledged as an OPEN owner decision (docstring 48-51: LedgerLock R-9/3 blocks at within_s:0.001, not at 20) — undecided, not yet closed.  
*Acceptance:* Closed by the owner's decision D1 'keep A' of 2026-10-18 (V21-81 names review_verify.py:300; V21-69 follows from it; OWNER-ANSWERS-2026-10-18.md:11). Add a pinning test: a finding with observable {exit_code: <approved>, within_s: 0.001} on an approved clause gives anchor()==ANCHORED and verify()==CONFIRMED, which is the decided behaviour. When review_verify.py is next edited for another reason (a kernel edit re-opens mutation evidence), change the docstring at :48-51 from 'open owner decision' to cite V21-81 D1.

**#5 [P2] authoring** — `aisef2/app/bundle.py:124-136 (order() reads only stories[s]['depends_on']; run.py:674 executes in that order) vs aisef2/plan/static_admission.py:168-175 (story_graph induced from criterion depends_on…`  
*Impact:* A bundle can be sealed AND admitted while the author under-declares a story dependency that a cross-story criterion depends_on implies. order() then schedules the dependent story before the story that produces its dependency, so StoryAdmission measures it at a parent SHA missing that work. §12 check_contradictions (static_admission.py:191) only enforces ordering for PRESERVE-after-INTRODUCE on the SAME spec, so a VERIFY/INTRODUCE cross-story dependency on a different spec is silently unenforced at runtime → spurious NOT_REACHED/PRECONDITION_BROKEN or a misleading verdict, not caught at authoring time.  
*Acceptance:* Test: a bundle where criterion cB (story B) depends_on cA (story A) while both stories declare depends_on=[]. Today it seals, admits and orders B before A, because ties go by name. After the fix, Project.order() places A before B, honouring story_graph(plan) ∪ declared edges. Alternatively, lint/admission names the missing edge B->A. Add a property test over generated plans: every induced story_graph edge is ordered dependency-first. The committed example bundles (calc-1-story, calc-3-stories) give byte-identical orders, so existing 2.2 bundles stay compatible. Land this with C5, before waves are derived from depends_on.

**#6a [P2] ci/release** — `docs/v2.3/RELEASE-CHARTER.md:38-41 vs .github/workflows/tests.yml:145-146 and staging.yml:105-110 (tests/installed_product_smoke.py:86,106 covers only run --check/run/--verify); no workflow contains …`  
*Impact:* The release gate does not yet enforce the full v2.3 claim surface (demo usability B4/B6/G4; signed/authentic evidence A2/E1/E2/E4/E3; compatibility read of 2.2 bundles). A v2.3 tag could pass CI without these being proven. This is a W1 'still needs', not a regression.  
*Acceptance:* W1-DESIGN §7 (#6 non-A2 part): installed_product_smoke runs `aisef demo` and asserts its three rows; a run written by the 2.2.0 wheel verifies with the candidate and RUN.json kernel_digest = c2d356b1…; a meta-test pins both steps in staging.yml (red-before).

**#6b [P2] ci/release** — `docs/v2.3/RELEASE-CHARTER.md:38-41 vs .github/workflows/tests.yml:145-146 and staging.yml:105-110 (tests/installed_product_smoke.py:86,106 covers only run --check/run/--verify); no workflow contains …`  
*Impact:* The release gate does not yet enforce the full v2.3 claim surface (demo usability B4/B6/G4; signed/authentic evidence A2/E1/E2/E4/E3; compatibility read of 2.2 bundles). A v2.3 tag could pass CI without these being proven. This is a W1 'still needs', not a regression.  
*Acceptance:* The release-path workflows (staging and release) run these steps from the installed wheel, offline. `aisef demo` exits 0. `aisef run --verify --expect-pubkey <escrowed pub>` exits 0 on a signed run, and exits non-zero on expected-bad controls (tampered journal, wrong pubkey, stripped signature). `--verify` of a committed historical 2.2 run directory returns consistency-only VERIFIED and is never reported as signed. A workflow meta-test fails if any of these steps is removed from the release path. (part b: lands with A2 in W2c)

**#7 [P2] ci/release** — `validation/qualification/repro_dist.py:41 (gzip.GzipFile(..., compresslevel=9): bytes depend on the host zlib/interpreter) + .github/workflows/tests.yml:114 (3.11) vs staging.yml:30 (3.12)`  
*Impact:* The advertised 'reproducible build / two independent rebuilds byte-identical' holds only on a pinned interpreter. A frozen-from-non-CI sdist digest would make release.yml `publish` fail at release_rc --verify-dist AFTER (future) approval. Worked around today by freezing digests only from a CI staging build; W1/F5 should pin the build interpreter or make repro_dist emit a host-independent gzip + add a two-minor rebuild-equality test.  
*Acceptance:* Write an explicit, tested reproducibility contract, and do not silently narrow the published claim. Option (a): the sdist gzip stream becomes host-independent. A test builds one commit with the same SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH on two CPython minors (CI legs 3.11 and 3.12) and asserts equal sdist sha256. Option (b): the claim is published as 'byte-reproducible on pinned CPython 3.12 + recorded zlib version'. Both build legs (tests.yml:114 and staging.yml:30) use the same pin, and a meta-test asserts the pins are equal and that the zlib version is recorded in the RC freeze. Either way, `release_rc --verify-dist` passes on a CI-frozen digest, and an injected 1-byte sdist change fails it. **[W0-freeze correction: root cause is preserved file-mode bits (RISK-REGISTER BUILD-REPRO-01); acceptance = W1-DESIGN §6 tests.]**

**#8 [P2] client/preflight** — `aisef2/app/client.py:105-127 (read_session parses text/error/turns/tokens only, with no served-model branch) + aisef2/app/run.py:340-343 (attested once from the preflight observation)`  
*Impact:* A dishonest or misconfigured router can serve the declared model for the two preflight probes, then silently bait-and-switch to a cheaper/different model for every billed turn with zero detection. Undermines the whole model-identity guarantee for exactly the spend that matters; cost ceilings catch gross overspend but not model substitution.  
*Acceptance:* Fake-client run tests. When a session stream's steps report a served model that differs from the attested route model, the run journals a provider/attestation mismatch and stops (IdentityStop) before any further billed turn. When it matches, each paid session journals a provider/attestation event, and the drift check over the whole run passes. read_session exposes the per-step served model. Interleaved attestation probe cost is accounted in the ledger.

**#9 [P2] client/preflight** — `aisef2/app/client.py:233-234 (XDG_CONFIG_HOME kept in the session env) + aisef2/app/run.py:616 (key resolved) and :653 (key only in `secrets` for post-run redaction) + aisef2/app/confine.py:18-20,130…`  
*Impact:* A tool the model executes during a session can read the provider key (from env or opencode.json) and POST it to any host over the unconfined egress; the run's own redaction would not prevent exfiltration, only hide it from the stored log. No loopback key-broker / egress allowlist / post-run assert-env-has-no-key, which is exactly what D3 specifies.  
*Acceptance:* Linux bwrap canary run inside a confined session against the fake provider. The session env and the client config visible in the session contain 0 occurrences of the provider key. The key is held by a loopback broker on 127.0.0.1 that injects Authorization server-side, with the client baseURL rewritten to it. An egress attempt to a non-attested host fails. A post-run scan of the session env dump, logs and artifacts finds 0 key occurrences. The run refuses when the broker cannot start (fail-closed).

**#10 [P2] evidence/journal** — `aisef2/journal/event.py:12-15 (unkeyed sha256 chain; the docstring states the anchor ceiling) + aisef2/app/lineage.py:833-851 (write_anchor/read_anchor: bare 64-hex, no MAC/signature); grep expect_pu…`  
*Impact:* Tamper on the trusted-directory boundary is detectable only as a head mismatch against an out-of-band anchor, and the anchor is not signed. Closing it is roadmap A2 (CHỐT QĐ#1: per-run ephemeral keypair + escrowed pubkey).  
*Acceptance:* `aisef run --verify --expect-pubkey PUB` returns VERIFIED for an untampered signed run (per-run ephemeral key, pubkey escrowed outside the run dir, per QĐ#1). It FAILS non-zero for each of: a tail rewrite with recomputed links and a recomputed unsigned anchor; a wrong pubkey; a removed or altered signature. Historical 2.2 runs still verify read-only as consistency-only, with no retroactive signed claim.

**#11 [P2] evidence/journal** — `aisef2/journal/format3.py:173-177 (_story_active: any open attempt, BEGIN included) used for PROOF_VERIFIED/TESTS_ADEQUACY at :218-220, vs aisef2/journal/projections/story_state.py:13-14 (_WITHIN: AC…`  
*Impact:* Because `reconstruct` runs only the writer-validator, a chain-consistent journal containing proof/verified in a non-ACTIVE attempt reconstructs cleanly and is refused only later when a gate folds StoryState — a reconstruct/fold asymmetry. As the two hand-written tables evolve independently they can diverge unnoticed. E6 (single pure state×event→next\|refuse table shared by writer and projection) is unimplemented.  
*Acceptance:* One pure state×event->(next\|refuse) table module is imported by both the format3 writer-validator and the StoryState projection, and TerminalState is derived from it. Tests: a journal carrying proof/verified or tests/adequacy inside a BEGIN (un-admitted) attempt is refused by reconstruct, i.e. by the writer-validator. Today it reconstructs cleanly and is refused only at fold. An exhaustive (state, event) equivalence test compares writer and projection with zero divergences, and existing committed journals of formats 1–3 still reconstruct and fold unchanged.

**#12 [P2] evidence/journal** — `aisef2/journal/format2.py:130-132 (PROVIDER_RESULT schema {story_id, outcome, synthetic, detail}: no stream digest; format3 does not override it) + aisef2/app/lineage.py:538-548 (in-flight spend infe…`  
*Impact:* Spend reconciliation trusts unchained on-disk session streams + the preflight ledger; a tampered/truncated stream is caught only as 'unread/unknown', never cryptographically. E4 (segment digest in provider/result, verify per segment) and E2 (owner-typed reservation at request, reconcile at result, repair charges max(reservation,stream)) are both unimplemented.  
*Acceptance:* Under a new journal format, with old formats still read, provider/result carries the sha256 of its session-stream segment, and --verify recomputes it. A truncated or edited segment then FAILS with a specific integrity problem, not an 'unknown' fallback, and the torn-tail segment is defined and tested. A provider/request writes an owner-typed spend reservation before dispatch. A SIGKILL between request and result (fault-injection test) charges max(reservation, stream), never 0 and never falsely paid.

**#13 [P2] invariants** — `aisef2/invariants/evidence.py:31,47,60 (harness_records/admit/EvidenceField); the only non-self references are aisef2/invariants/registry.py:172,174 (grep across aisef2/)`  
*Impact:* VIII is attested only by its own unit tests; the 'memory is context, never evidence' boundary gates no real production path. register() still passes because the mechanism merely resolves. This is the residual of IMPROVEMENT-ROADMAP §23 (the invariants package was dead at the production layer); A1 wired the arming but VIII's admission remains unwired — arming guarantees the code is present+unmodified, not that any evidence actually flows through admit().  
*Acceptance:* Option (a): at least one production evidence-carrying path (for example the proof records consumed by verify/fold) builds EvidenceField or calls admit(). Then grep shows a non-test, non-registry caller, and a test pushing a Context or plain dict through that production path raises InvariantError(VIII). Option (b): the owner re-points VIII as test-tier-only, and registry.py:171-176 is updated accordingly. Either way, register() must not pass on a mechanism that gates nothing.

**#14 [P2] mutation/efficiency lane** — `validation/v2/mutation.py:1642-1643 (only `.is_file()` on the ruling); VERIFIED_REUSE_RULING (:1570) and VERIFIED_REUSE_PLATFORM (:1575) are each referenced only at their definition (grep); the commi…`  
*Impact:* The one-off whole-module-invalidation override for the 42 bundle_cli targets is gated on owner authority that is only presence-verified. The docstring (mutation.py:1628) claims 'the committed ruling must be present' but any file at closure-evidence/v2/v2.2/B1-MUTATION-EVIDENCE-REUSE-RULING.json satisfies it. The digest pins (source endpoints + measurement-commit provenance) still fail-closed, so verdicts cannot be silently changed; but the authority artifact that MAKES the reuse 'owner-authorized' is not identity-bound — weakening of an INV-MUTATION-AUTHORITY-style independent-authority gate.  
*Acceptance:* Negative tests, each on a COPY tree with a green baseline. Replacing the ruling with `{}`, giving it a different id, giving it a non-approved status, or giving a platform other than linux each makes verified_reuse_current return False, and `mutation.py --check` then reports the 42 bundle_cli targets as changed, not current. The authentic committed ruling (id == VERIFIED_REUSE_RULING, blob digest pinned) returns True. In W1 F5, this one-off is re-expressed as a typed VERIFIED_REUSE record whose authority is bound by the ruling's identity and digest, not by file presence.

**#15 [P2] mutation/efficiency lane** — `validation/v2/mutation.py:1623-1645 (verified_reuse_current); grep VERIFIED_REUSE\|verified_reuse\|REUSE-RULING across tests/ and validation/ -> only validation/v2/mutation.py`  
*Impact:* A security-sensitive, fail-closed, owner-authority reuse path added in the latest commit (abbd88b5) has zero direct unit coverage. A regression that drops any of the 5 fail-closed conditions (identity, either digest, the Linux worker tree binding, measurement-commit provenance) would not be caught by the suite, while that path can flip a 'target changed → re-run' into 'current' for 42 release-gate targets.  
*Acceptance:* tests/v2 cases: a happy path where all conditions hold returns True. Each condition broken alone returns False: target not among the 42; source_sha256 mismatch; tree_sha256 mismatch; live-file digest mismatch; ruling missing or inauthentic; measurement commit unreadable; provenance digest mismatch. verified_reuse_current is registered as a mutation TARGET, and its mutants are killed by these tests.

**#16 [P2] product/probe** — `aisef2/probe/process_effect.py:907-913 (RESULT await = monotonic()+window only; TIMEOUT+members() -> SUBJECT_DEADLINE before any RESULT re-read; :918 re-read only on the no-members branch). Sibling g…`  
*Impact:* False REFUTED on product evidence: a scenario that actually completed (or whose delay was the controller/host, not the subject) is recorded as an expired-window REFUTED → a MUST_HOLD contract spuriously UNSATISFIED. Systematic: every process_effect observation lacks the guard.  
*Acceptance:* New units in tests/v2/test_probe_process_effect_units.py: (a) a Running fake with members() non-empty whose protocol file holds a valid RESULT at TIMEOUT -> the RESULT conclusion (_concluded), never SUBJECT_DEADLINE; (b) controller alive and silent past the window with no deadline record of its own -> HARNESS_FAILED (the _silent rule, after window+env.timeout_s), never SUBJECT_DEADLINE; (c) control: a subject that really hangs past W -> SUBJECT_DEADLINE/REFUTED via the controller's own deadline record (a Timer, as in the siblings). The existing pin at :542-555 is updated on purpose and named in the change. The new process_effect probe digest gets a fresh v2.3 calibration/EXECUTION_DIGEST row. v2.2 calibration records stay byte-identical (git diff empty). The tie-at-W sub-case keeps the owner D3 (V21-83) behaviour: the answer wins when the line is already in the file.

**#17 [P2] qualification/release** — `validation/qualification/c2_closure_audit.py:721-726 (history_freeze: entry_count=len(working-tree entries), digest over them) vs :947-950 (check slices entries[:entry_count]); rule :750-751. Record …`  
*Impact:* history_freeze() records entry_count=len(working-tree entries)=43 and a digest over them, but the record's own rule (:750-751) says the carrying commit's gate attempts 'cannot be in the record it carries'; the committed C2-FINAL-RUN-HISTORY.json held 27 entries at write time and 35 now, never 43. check() slices entries[:43] (yielding ≤35) and compares to the 43-entry digest, which can never match. All 12 other frozen histories MATCH; only C2-FINAL is broken. Dormant: the audit is not wired into any CI/test (only docs reference it) and the record is a superseded STOPPED-before-acceptance artifact, but it misleads anyone re-verifying cycle-2 closure evidence.  
*Acceptance:* Measured today: a re-derivation of every frozen row gives 12/13 MATCH and C2-FINAL MISMATCH (43 frozen vs 35 committed). Closed when: the freeze record stays byte-identical (immutable v2.2-era evidence; git diff empty), AND EITHER seq 36-43 are restored append-only from primary evidence so that `python3 -P validation/qualification/c2_closure_audit.py --check` exits 0 with all 13 rows MATCH, OR an append-only v2.3 erratum record binds the freeze-file sha256, the 35-entry committed history and the timing evidence above, and the W1 incremental-evidence engine classifies the C2-FINAL freeze row as INVALIDATED (never VERIFIED_REUSE). A negative test proves that an INVALIDATED row cannot be cited as current evidence.

**#18 [P2] run/execute** — `aisef2/app/run.py:705 (_journal_info -> path.read_bytes() at :757) and :710 (git(out/'repo','rev-parse','main') -> workspace.git subprocess.run at aisef2/orchestrate/workspace.py:55,71), both unguard…`  
*Impact:* B3 (the record is written whatever happens — exception, invariant failure, refused shutdown, interruption) is the whole reason the finally exists, yet L705 `_journal_info` does path.read_bytes() and L710 `git(out/'repo','rev-parse','main')` runs a subprocess, both BEFORE the RUN.json write at L727 and both unguarded. Every other I/O in this block is defensively wrapped (usage try/except L721-726, manifest try/except L728-732, _close_run try/finally L702-704) — the asymmetry shows L705/L710 are an oversight. On resource exhaustion during a ~33-min heavy run (EMFILE/ENFILE makes subprocess.run raise OSError via workspace.config_problems L55 / git L71), or if git leaves PATH, the exception propagates out of execute(), RUN.json is never written, AND it masks the original `failure` being re-raised. Fix (lazy/root): wrap L705 and L710 best-effort (default to None) like the sibling usage/manifest blocks so the record write at L727 is always reached.  
*Acceptance:* Test in tests/v2 (app run). Monkeypatch aisef2.app.run.git, and separately _journal_info's read, to raise OSError during the finally. With the execute() body (a) succeeding and (b) raising RunRefused: out/'RUN.json' exists in both cases, final_main is None and the journal field records the error. In case (b) the ORIGINAL exception type propagates (not masked by OSError) and rec.failure.stage is set. #42 is fixed in the same edit: with usage.usage raising, 'no_progress' is still present in RUN.json. The RUN.json bytes of an unfaulted run are unchanged (E7 byte-identity check).

**#19 [P2] security/confine** — `aisef2/app/tripwire.py:110-117 (scan_repo walks the checked-out tree, skips any path with '.git' in parts) vs aisef2/app/confine.py:196 (ro bind of git_dir(workspace) = the common .git: every object …`  
*Impact:* Proof independence (DEVELOPER-CONFINEMENT-GAP-001) can be defeated for git-object-hidden content: a false CLEAN verdict while the developer can retrieve the sealed stimulus, undermining the whole tripwire guarantee for that class of leak.  
*Acceptance:* Negative test. Fixture repo: commit the project bundle (and separately one contract's stimulus) in commit X, delete it in a later commit on main, set the baseline after the deletion. Today this gives scan verdict CLEAN and the run proceeds. After the fix: either the run is refused before the first session (LEAK naming the git object/commit, never the value), or the served clone holds no history beyond the baseline. Proven by `git -C <worktree> cat-file --batch-all-objects --batch` (run inside the confined session on Linux, and in the run clone on every OS) yielding no tripwire needle. The control (clean history) still runs.

**#20 [P2] security/confine** — `aisef2/app/confine.py:18-20 (network host, egress NOT confined, declared), :130-132 (none refused for runs), :164 (--share-net), :195-199 (client config dir bound RO, operator env kept inside)`  
*Impact:* A prompt-injected or misbehaving model session can exfiltrate the provider key and run artifacts to an arbitrary host. Disclosed (V21-02) but still a live exfiltration surface the sandbox does not close.  
*Acceptance:* D3 acceptance. confine.measure (Linux) asserts that the session env and every RO-bound path carry no provider key (canary key planted, grep of env + bound config = 0 hits). The session reaches the provider only through a loopback key-broker (baseURL 127.0.0.1). An egress canary to a non-attested host from inside the session fails, and a canary to the attested endpoint via the broker succeeds. A journaled key-boundary event exists. The run is refused when a key is detected in the session env.

**#21 [P2] security/confine** — `aisef2/app/confine.py:29-31 (declared: kernel test execution and probe execution of the candidate not confined); only wrap call sites are aisef2/app/client.py:350 and aisef2/app/diagnostics.py:103; t…`  
*Impact:* Arbitrary candidate code runs outside any sandbox at test/probe time; a malicious or buggy candidate can read/write the host, reach the network, and touch run evidence. This is the core D1 confinement gap for the subject-under-test.  
*Acceptance:* D1 acceptance on Linux. The developer-test runner and the probe subject run under confine.wrap (worktree writable, rest RO). Escape canaries from candidate code are denied, with evidence in the confinement record: (a) a read of a file outside the worktree (e.g. <out>/journal), (b) a write outside the worktree, (c) a network connect, (d) a setsid escapee surviving. The same canaries pass when unconfined (control). Calibration rows are re-measured as new v2.3 records. On unsupported OS with confinement requested, fail closed.

**#22 [P2] verify/authenticity** — `aisef2/app/verify.py:167-172 (expect_anchor compare + note), docstring :10-13 (consistency not authenticity without anchor); aisef2/app/run.py:446-448 anchor_of = unkeyed sha256 of manifest`  
*Impact:* The headline trust guarantee is bounded; honestly declared in the module docstring, the expect_anchor note, and README Known-limitations, but still the open gap. This is roadmap A2 / V21-30 / D5, scheduled for Wave 4, not yet implemented.  
*Acceptance:* A2 acceptance. A red/green adversarial replay: a whole-directory consistent rewrite (RUN.json + journal + sessions + regenerated EVIDENCE.json) VERIFIES today and must be REFUTED by `aisef run --verify --expect-pubkey <escrowed>` on a v2.3 run (signed head over run_id+head+anchor+kernel_digest, key outside the writable dir). The untampered control VERIFIES. The historical v2.2 run dirs still verify as consistency-only with an explicit note (V21-49), with no retroactive signed claim.

**#23 [P3] adapters/grade** — `aisef2/app/adapters.py:233 ((work/'home').mkdir(parents=True), no exist_ok) after the log-only guard :226-231; aisef2/orchestrate/review.py:33-39 catches only ProviderOutage/CapabilityUnrunnable. Unr…`  
*Impact:* An environment leftover escapes review.py's typed-exception handling (review.py:34-39) and surfaces as an unhandled exception instead of a typed ENVIRONMENT refusal, violating the module contract that a capability that cannot run raises CapabilityUnrunnable. Narrow (attempt# normally increments between retries, giving a fresh path), but a latent fail-open-to-crash trap.  
*Acceptance:* Unit test: pre-create <logs>/../review/<story>.<attempt>/home with no <story>.reviewer.<attempt>.jsonl, then call Reviewer.review with finder=ocr. The result must be a typed outcome: CapabilityUnrunnable -> failure/observed CAPABILITY_UNRUNNABLE (ENVIRONMENT) via review.review, or a documented reuse of the leftover dir. Never an untyped FileExistsError escaping review.review. Added to the G6 resume kill-in-window negative cases.

**#24 [P3] adapters/grade** — `aisef2/app/review_verify.py:302 and :308 (two _approved_by_clause(project) scans per anchor(); def :275-289), :294 reproducer_contract recomputed (also :193 in verify())`

*Acceptance:* No standalone change: the efficiency cost is O(obligations) per finding with no correctness effect, and any aisef2 edit moves kernel_digest and re-opens mutation evidence. Fold it only into a change that already edits anchor() (e.g. defect #4). Then a test counts exactly one _approved_by_clause call per anchor(), and anchor()/verify() outputs are byte-identical over the existing review_verify test corpus.

*Impact:* Trust-boundary limitation, honestly documented: a pinned-but-unattested reviewer binary is accepted. Closing it is exactly what C7 (cross-session attestation) and the C8 raw-API adapter are scoped to do; both are unchecked on the roadmap.  
*Acceptance:* The limit is declared in every OCR RUN.json session identity. Closed only if the owner rules V21-86 (D-OCR-1: compare against the upstream release artifact). Then the W3 pin upgrade (G3 pin/patch attest) records independent build provenance for the OCR binary, and a test proves that a binary matching the pin sha but lacking a provenance record is refused (or reported) as unattested. Otherwise it stays ACCEPTED_LIMITATION with this record. **[critic fix: V21-86 is decided (default 'do not download', OWNER-ANSWERS-2026-10-18.md:14) — no wait.]**

**#26 [P3] aisef2/control/*** — `aisef2/control/owner.py:81-84 (budget: None only for NOT_RETRYABLE), :120-122 (MISSING_CREDENTIAL = RESOLVED_BY_POLICY); no other reference to RESOLVED_BY_POLICY in aisef2; journal retryable = budget…`  
*Impact:* Not a wrong result today: with settings limits present (settings.py:234 requires every owner), a missing credential retries exactly like CONTRACT_UNSATISFIED up to the ENVIRONMENT count — arguably the intended effect. Risk is latent/maintenance: no kernel code branches on retryability is RESOLVED_BY_POLICY, so a future change that reads the enum expecting a real policy hook would be a silent no-op, and a reviewer reading the enum will believe credentials get special retry handling they do not. The distinction is purely documentary.  
*Acceptance:* Equivalence pinned rather than changed. A test asserts classify(MISSING_CREDENTIAL).budget is Owner.ENVIRONMENT, and that with settings limits.ENVIRONMENT=0 a MISSING_CREDENTIAL failure is not retried, while with limits.ENVIRONMENT=N it is retried at most N times (the policy decides, per the §22 rule text). In mutation records, the P->R mutant at owner.py:120 is recorded as EQUIVALENT with this test as justification. No product change.

**#41 [P3] run/execute** — `aisef2/app/run.py:93-100 (kernel_digest: sorted(root.rglob('*.py')) at :98)`  
*Impact:* The running sha256 is order-dependent (L98-99). sorted() compares Path objects by the OS flavour's normcase (POSIX: case-sensitive, '/'=0x2F; Windows: lowercased, '\\'=0x5C). A sibling dir `foo/` and file `foo<c>.py` with 0x2F<c<0x5C (digits/upper-case) sort in opposite order on the two platforms, so the SAME source tree can yield different kernel_digest cross-platform — while the code already LF-normalizes content (L99) precisely for cross-platform stability. Because kernel_digest is bound FULL into the runspec (L346), cross-platform `--verify` would HARD-FAIL runspec re-derivation (verify.py:72) and silently skip invariant-digest comparison (verify.py:122-123). Given Windows is a live release constraint and anchors are meant to be portable, this is a real latent gap. Verified: NO divergence in the current tree (no flipping sibling pair exists today), so latent, not currently biting. One-line fix: `sorted(root.rglob('*.py'), key=lambda p: p.relative_to(root).as_posix())`.  
*Acceptance:* Audit mechanism CORRECTED by measurement (CPython 3.12.14 and 3.14.7): Path ordering is parts-based on both flavours, so the claimed '/' vs '\\' sibling flip (zz/ vs zz2.py) does NOT occur; the real divergence is Windows case-folding (PurePosix ['B.py','a.py'] vs PureWindows ['a.py','B.py']). Current 94-file aisef2 tree orders identically on both flavours (latent). Test: kernel_digest over a synthetic root holding B.py, a.py, x.py and x/ siblings is identical whether ordered by PurePosixPath or PureWindowsPath; fix key = p.relative_to(root).parts (NOT the audit's as_posix() string key, which reorders x.py vs x/ and would change POSIX digests); kernel_digest() of HEAD tree is byte-identical before/after so v2.2 RUN.json kernel_digest values still verify (verify.py:122, bundle_cli.py:982).

**#42 [P3] run/execute** — `aisef2/app/run.py:721-726 (tuple assignment at :722 binds usage and no_progress together; except at :725 writes only usage.error); verify.py:246-253 skips the no_progress comparison whenever usage ha…`  
*Impact:* `rec['usage'], rec['no_progress'] = usage.usage(out,...), usage.no_progress(events)` evaluates the whole RHS before binding; if usage.usage() raises, the except (L725) sets only rec['usage']={'error':...}, so rec['no_progress'] is never written although usage.no_progress(events) depends solely on in-memory events and would have succeeded. Consumers reading rec['no_progress'] get a silently-absent field on a usage error path. Minor; split the two assignments so no_progress is computed independently of usage.usage().  
*Acceptance:* Offline execute() with usage.usage monkeypatched to raise: RUN.json carries usage == {'error': ...} AND no_progress == usage.no_progress(journal events) (non-empty fixture with two identical failed attempts); --verify still compares no_progress against the journal when usage errored (a tampered no_progress is REFUTED); RUN.json bytes on the non-error path unchanged (E7 byte-identity).

**#43 [P3] security/confine** — `aisef2/app/confine.py:122-123 (problem(): backend must be 'bwrap'), :181-182 (wrap() raises Unavailable unless sys.platform startswith 'linux'), :329-330 (measure() short-circuits off Linux)`  
*Impact:* No OS isolation is available on the project's own macOS/Windows developer+CI platforms; confinement is Linux-only and opt-in. Refusal is correct fail-closed behaviour, so the risk is a missing capability rather than a bypass.  
*Acceptance:* D2 delivered: on macOS a confinement spec with the Seatbelt/sandbox-exec backend passes confine.problem, wrap() emits sandbox-exec argv, and the confine.measure canary shows in-worktree write allowed and out-of-worktree read/write + key-file read denied (positive and negative cases, declared best-effort); on Windows wrap() still raises Unavailable and a run requesting confinement is refused (fail-closed, documented limitation). Until then v2.2 behaviour (Linux-only, fail-closed) is correct.

**#44 [P3] verify/authenticity** — `aisef2/app/verify.py:246-253 (usage compared to usage.usage(out, events, prices, rec)); aisef2/app/usage.py:102 (started, finished from rec) and :114 (wall_s from them)`

Same self-referential-comparison class as the B1 registry_digest gap (18/19 mutants survived), here on a non-verdict field.  
*Acceptance:* Adversarial replay: rewrite RUN.json started/finished/usage.wall_s consistently (+3600 s) with session streams, journal and manifest regenerated -> --verify REFUTES, because started is bound to RUN.started.json (written before execution, run.py:497) and finished/wall_s to journal or signed-head evidence (A2/E4); or, if not bindable, verify emits an explicit note that wall_s is record-asserted, not re-derived. Tokens/cost re-derivation unchanged; no new overclaim.

Replaying the 9a3d1377^ tree (7 unledgered sites) must produce a red unit verdict. **[W0-freeze correction: implemented as a NEW ungated test file (tests/v2/test_v23_live_tree_checks.py) calling the live-tree checks directly; GATED bodies unchanged; sealing_gate.py changes only for V22_REPLAYED (after ROUND-AUTH); test_sealing_gate.py not edited.]**

**#47 [P2] authority-checkers** — `validation/v2/destructive_authority.py:259-276,288-302 (used by validate :335-347,:399)`  
*Impact:* A false PASS for exactly the shape the authority class forbids ("never from a table or a report", :56). The owner invariant can be violated while the checker says PASS. There is no current live instance (live tree PASS), but once D1 arms the check it is unsound.  
*Acceptance:* validate() resolves the target root by scope: the innermost function first, then enclosing functions or closures, then self./cls. attributes of the same class, then the module. It ignores bindings in sibling functions. Every binding that reaches the site must be an ast.Call whose resolved callee is a creator (subprocess.Popen, ProcessRange, CreateJobObjectW, tempfile.mkdtemp/TemporaryDirectory, shutil.copytree), never a substring match of the unparsed text. Alternatively it is a call or tuple-unpack of a same-module/class helper whose return statement returns such a binding. _callee_creates requires the helper to return the created object. Fixtures that must FAIL: (a) a() binds proc = subprocess.Popen and b(report) does proc = report.process; os.kill(proc.pid, 9) with a CONTROLLER_CREATED_PID row; (b) h = report.get('Popen('); h.kill() with a CONTROLLER_CREATED_HANDLE row; (c) a helper that calls Popen but returns report.handle. All 66 live ledger rows still PASS, including test_interruption.py:422 via self.start()'s returned Popen and the lambda-closure cleanups.

A SPAWNERS call with non-constant argv, or argv[0] in sh/bash/zsh/cmd/powershell with -c or /c, is a 'shell' site that fails closed. The live tree passes after any newly found sites are ledgered.

**#60 [P2] lineage+cohort: aisef2/app/lineage.py** — `aisef2/app/lineage.py:264 (process_started: `ps -o lstart=` with inherited env) + :287 (_gone: `now != who["started"]` -> True); window run.py:549 (claim) -> run.py:630 (run.begin)`  
*Impact:* Breaks the 'one continuation per run' guarantee without operator involvement. The child stays UNBEGUN from the claim (run.py:549) through clone, admission, venv creation, confinement and paid preflight requests (run.py:618) up to run.begin() (run.py:630). A concurrent `aisef run --parent P` from a shell with another TZ/locale (cron/launchd vs interactive, CI TZ=UTC) takes CONTINUED-BY.2.json. Both runs then continue P and both inherit P's spend, so the lineage can spend past its ceiling. The append-only claim also records the false fact claimant_gone: True. Reproduced here: claim(P, child1) in the default env, then os.environ['TZ']='UTC' and claim(P, child2) -> superseded {'child_state': 'UNBEGUN', 'claimant_gone': True, 'operator_asserted': False} while the claimant pid was this live process. Further triggers I did not measure: a Linux clock step shifting btime-derived lstart; a hostname shared across PID namespaces or hosts on shared storage. Not covered by V21-41 (Windows has no ps) or CODE-AUDIT #31 (client._is_gone).  
*Acceptance:* (a) process_started records and compares the claimant's start time in one TZ/locale-independent form. Either run ps with env LC_ALL=C TZ=UTC0 (the same idea as the LC_TIME=C pin in validation/v2/cleanup_authority.py:133-138), or use an epoch or boot-relative value. _gone returns None, never True, when the recorded value is not in that form (every claim written before the fix). (b) Test with two real processes: process A takes claim(P, c1) under TZ=Asia/Ho_Chi_Minh LC_ALL=fr_FR.UTF-8 and stays alive. Process B calls claim(P, c2) under TZ=UTC LC_ALL=C. B must get LineageRefused 'is alive', and CONTINUED-BY.2.json must not exist. A same-env control stays refused, and a dead-pid control is still superseded by evidence. (c) A claim in the old format with a live pid is refused, not superseded. (d) Fence: execute() re-reads lineage.claims(predecessor)[-1] right before preflight.run (run.py:618) and before run.begin (run.py:630). It refuses with RunRefused (exit 2) when the newest claim no longer names this run. Test: inject a superseding claim after run.py:549, then expect RunRefused, 0 requests in the preflight ledger, and no journal.

After: `--parent B1` raises RunRefused with exit 2 naming the current holder B2, and no CONTINUED-BY*.json is created under B1. Implement the check in lineage.claim so every caller gets it. **#65 [P3] lineage+cohort: aisef2/app/lineage.py** — `aisef2/cohort/lifecycle.py:149-156 (record_run binds a bare string), :165-175 (observe_inputs is caller-driven), :184-197 (generalization takes no current measurement); aisef2/cohort/preregistration.…`  
*Impact:* Latent (no cohort exists). A future generalization could rest on cherry-picked runs, on runs under another execution profile or kernel, or on a cohort whose inputs changed after its results were read, and still be accepted.  
*Acceptance:* record_run(cohort, run_record) refuses unless runspec_hash, plan_hash and requirements sha match the preregistration, a new sha256 kernel_digest frozen input matches the run's kernel_digest, and run.started > seal time. generalization(cohorts, measured_inputs) applies observe_inputs and refuses changed digests. Tests (red-before: record_run accepts any string): a run record with a mismatched runspec_hash is refused; generalization with a changed 'prompts' digest after the read gives GENERALIZATION_REFUSED and reports that cohort EXPOSED.

**#66 [P3] lineage+cohort: aisef2/app/lineage.py** — `aisef2/cohort/lifecycle.py:114-130 (seal: `prior` caller-supplied; :128 reuse = whole-record hash equality)`  
*Impact:* Latent. 'An EXPOSED or DEVELOPMENT workload never seals again' (rule 4) and 'only a FRESH preregistration' (rule 2) depend on caller discipline. Reproduced: seal(T.prereg(1)) with no prior -> SEALED; a stale C-1 plus a copy that changes only cohort_id -> SEALED, with frozen_inputs equal to the invalidated cohort's.  
*Acceptance:* The reuse check compares frozen_inputs digests with cohort_id excluded. Red-before: deepcopy(T.prereg(1)) with only cohort_id changed to 'C-1-bis' and seal(same, [stale]) returns SEALED today; after, it gives PREREGISTRATION_REUSED. seal() takes a registry (or a digest-bound complete prior set) and refuses when it is absent; sealing a workload the registry records as EXPOSED with prior omitted gives WORKLOAD_DEMOTED.

> **Disposition #64–#66 (dated 2026-11-26, owner ruling "A2′ Closure, Cohort Defect Disposition and RC Preparation"
> §2): NON-RELEASE B2 DISPOSITION.** Remediation is removed from the v2.3 critical path. The three findings stay
> exactly as written above — they are **NOT FIXED, NOT PASSED, NOT QUALIFIED**; provenance and acceptance criteria
> are preserved for the later owner-led remediation. Tier-0 cohort code, tests and evidence remain in the tree and
> in normal CI; nothing is masked.
>
> **Mandated source-level dependency check (2026-11-26, HEAD `f0c230f3`, independently adversarially verified):**
> no active consumer of the affected `aisef2/cohort/lifecycle.py` surface (construction history-trust, `seal`
> prior/reuse, `advance`, `record_run`, `observe_inputs`, `generalization`) exists in the retained release
> capabilities. Evidence: (a) the import surface is closed and Q0-enforced (`cohort_static_checks.py:35`
> `ALLOWED_IMPORTERS`, rule COHORT_CONFINED, AST scan incl. importlib/`__import__` forms); (b) the product runtime
> imports zero cohort modules (runtime import-closure probe over `run`, `cli`, `bundle_cli`, `review_verify`,
> `lineage`, `orchestrate`, `journal`) and `run.py:716` hard-codes `generalization_claim="NONE"`, which
> `qualification/p10.py:214-215` refuses to see otherwise using its OWN local state machine (`p10.py:64`), not
> lifecycle.py; (c) no cohort record can exist (Q0 checker NO_COHORT_RECORD, `cohort_static_checks.py:133-149`);
> (d) `profile_freeze.py` consumes only `preregistration.execution_profile`/`runspec_of` (content-addressed
> identity re-derivation), never lifecycle; (e) the B5 gateway does not exist at HEAD (scheduled W5), and the
> current acceptance surface (`run.py` delivery, `bundle_cli.py`, `review_verify.py`) holds zero cohort references;
> (f) `lineage.py` (the findings' group label) never touches cohort. Precision caveat recorded by the reviewer:
> the C2-P10 cohort validator is LIVE in the v2.3 round (`test_v23_rounds.py` `test_P10_COHORT` → `currency_dag`
> node P10-COHORT → `version_forward.check_supersession` re-runs `c2_p10_cohort.build()` each round) — it is a
> deliberate exerciser of the module, the #64–#66 weaknesses are missing-refusal (overly permissive) so every
> probed refusal still fires and the verdict stays "machinery qualified; cohort count = 0", and node P10-COHORT
> has no dependents. **Change-impact note for the future remediation:** fixing #64–#66 changes `build()`'s record
> bytes, so P10-COHORT-MACHINERY.json must be regenerated through the version-forward chain or `test_P10_COHORT`
> goes red — a planned cost of the later fix, not a current-verdict exposure.

**#67 [P2] plan/admission** — `aisef2/plan/static_admission.py:120-129 (check_ownership); aisef2/plan/obligation.py:89-102 (no criterion uniqueness); aisef2/app/bundle_cli.py:581-601 (seal: facts[cr['id']] silently overwritten)`  
*Impact:* Repro (fixture world): plan [C1→BC-ADD INTRODUCE, C1→BC-NO-TEL INTRODUCE] in S1 is admitted with zero failed checks; admit_story returns [PRE_SATISFIED, READY] but journals {C1: READY}; continue_story emits plan-drift naming the wrong spec (b instead of a); the journal _admission_rule accepts it. In production the run then dies late with an untyped ProjectionError/InvariantError (qualification_counters.py:49-51 / event.py:173 / format3.py:146 _distinct_strs) after admission probes ran, or — all-PRE_SATISFIED path — the first spec is never proved at CANDIDATE (only at POST_MERGE via merge.py:49, where a developer regression routes to INTEGRATION, defeating V21-38). Reachable by a plain authoring copy-paste of a criterion id.  
*Acceptance:* Red-before: engine.admit(plan((ob('C1','BC-ADD','S1',INTRODUCE), ob('C1','BC-NO-TEL','S1',VERIFY))), INPUTS).admitted is True today. After: it is False and check 3 names 'criterion C1 has 2 obligations'. bundle_cli seal of an authoring document with a duplicate criteria id in one story is refused, naming $.stories.S1.criteria[1]. The engine digest change goes through the F5 impact path (p5_acceptance.py:845 identity), not a blanket requalification.

**#68 [P3] plan/admission** — `aisef2/app/run.py:681-685 (StoryInputs built without completed); aisef2/orchestrate/story_runner.py:70 (completed default {}), :111/:180/:367-368 (StoryResult.measured produced, never consumed); aise…`  
*Impact:* Real evidence: closure-evidence/v2/release/smoke-2/run-1/aisef-run.jsonl holds 55 plan-drift events, all attributed_to UNATTRIBUTED, candidates []; only the P2 fixture record (P2-PLAN-DRIFT.json) ever attributes ('S1'). 'UNATTRIBUTED' therefore conflates 'not attempted' with 'ambiguous'; unattributed_plan_drift (qualification_counters.py:20) is systematically inflated and any preregistered max_unattributed_plan_drift would FAIL spuriously. RFC §14 MUST (attribute through the DAG) unmet in the shipped path.  
*Acceptance:* Integration test through run.execute (offline fake client) with two stories: S1 holds a VERIFY on spec Y measured UNSATISFIED at its parent and SATISFIED post-merge, and S2 (depends on S1) INTRODUCEs Y. S2's journaled story/plan-drift has attributed_to 'S1' and candidates ['S1']; red-before it is UNATTRIBUTED with []. Negative: with no upstream flip it stays UNATTRIBUTED, and the record distinguishes 'no candidate measured' from 'more than one candidate'.

**#69 [P2] plan/admission** — `aisef2/plan/static_admission.py:32-40 (ENGINE_SOURCES = static_admission.py, obligation.py), :290; behaviour imported from :25-30`  
*Impact:* A change to compiler.py or calibration.py can flip an admission verdict while engine_digest stays identical; engine_digest is consumed as a qualification identity (p5_acceptance.py:845 'engine_digest_is_the_v21_proposal_s', c2_plan_correction.py:176, v21_conformance_ruling.py:406). This is exactly the W1/F5 'transitive behaviour-changing dependencies' impact input: reuse keyed on this digest would be VERIFIED_REUSE on stale evidence (EVID-COUPLING-01 family).  
*Acceptance:* Negative test on a COPY tree: mutate one byte of aisef2/product/compiler.py, and separately aisef2/probe/calibration.py. A new engine-identity field differs in both cases (red-before: StaticPlanAdmissionEngine.digest is unchanged). The identity is derived from the import closure of the nine check functions, or from an explicit closure list that a static test proves complete against the module's imports. The existing engine_digest stays as recorded, so frozen records still compare. Until then, no F5 round-v2.3 node may use engine_digest as a reuse key; the F5 audit-hook declared-input test covers it.

**#70 [P2] plan/admission** — `aisef2/plan/static_admission.py:65-79 (result fields), :298-299 (result_digest body)`  
*Impact:* Repro: two different plans with the same id (plan_hash differs) are both admitted and produce equal result_digest. Recorded admission evidence (RUN.json rec['admission'], P10 WORKLOAD.json/LEDGERLOCK-REGRESSION.json result_digest, C2-P5 reverification) cannot prove WHICH plan or inputs were admitted, so it cannot serve as source identity for W1 VERIFIED_REUSE/INVALIDATED. RFC §12 dataclass matches the code, so this is a contract gap, not a drift.  
*Acceptance:* Red-before: two plans with the same id and different plan_hash are both admitted with an equal result_digest. After, a v2.3 result version carries plan_hash and an AdmissionInputs digest (requirements, contracts, approvals, specs, catalogue, calibrations): the two plans give distinct result_digest, and a result whose plan_hash != plan.plan_hash is refused by require_admitted and by the journal rule. Historical v2 records validate unchanged under their frozen schema, with a COMPATIBILITY-MATRIX entry.

**#72 [P3] plan/admission** — `aisef2/journal/format3.py:147-148,166-170 (plan/static-admitted schema+rule, no emitter in aisef2/); aisef2/orchestrate/story_runner.py:155-158 (plan/frozen without admission binding); aisef2/plan/st…`

After: the journal holds exactly one plan/static-admitted (admitted=true, result_digest equal to the recomputed one) strictly before plan/frozen. **#74 [P3] plan/admission** — `aisef2/plan/drift.py:88-90 (already_satisfied = no READY) vs aisef2/orchestrate/story_runner.py:233-240 (no_work = all SATISFIED, K-NOWORK-001)`

**#76 [P3] plan/admission** — `aisef2/plan/story_admission.py:87-98`

**#78 [P3] plan/admission** — `aisef2/plan/story_admission.py:150-164`  
*Impact:* Evidence of a binding mismatch (a probe returning a record for another spec/revision/enforcement) is lost exactly when it matters; violates the 'journal before post-check' rule. Low reachability for admitted plans (check 5 removes row gaps).  
*Acceptance:* Use a probe whose record is bound to another revision, or classify/bound_result patched to raise. admit_story raises InvariantError AND the sink already holds the probe/evaluated for that criterion. Red-before: the sink holds no probe/evaluated for it.

**#79 [P2] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/quality/test_execution.py:273-276,296-299,345-348,377 (runner tracer) + aisef2/quality/relevance.py:210-215`  
*Impact:* Deterministic false INADEQUATE (owner DEVELOPER) for correct stories. Under the live TestsPolicy(True) (run.py:672) this blocks the attempt (story_runner.py:302-305) and burns the developer quality budget on every retry. Measured: the constant change LIMIT 1->2 gave relevance IRRELEVANT and vacuity NON_VACUOUS, assembled INADEQUATE. A CLI exercised via subprocess gave IRRELEVANT + NON_VACUOUS -> INADEQUATE. The two measurements contradict each other, and the defect precedence lets IRRELEVANT win. CLI dogfood projects (todo-cli) match shape (b).  
*Acceptance:* Add to tests/v2/p5/test_relevance.py, using the real te.UNITTEST runner and orchestrate.quality.assess with TestsPolicy(True): (1) The candidate changes module-level `LIMIT = 1` to `2` (and, separately, a default argument `n=1` to `n=2`). The story test imports pkg.m at module top and asserts f()==2. relevance must not be IRRELEVANT and assess().failure must not be TESTS_INADEQUATE. (2) The story test asserts on subprocess.run([sys.executable,'-m','pkg']).stdout. ResultSet.capabilities['line-coverage'] must be PARTIAL, or relevance UNMEASURABLE, never IRRELEVANT; the assembly is INCOMPLETE, not INADEQUATE. (3) Regression guard: a lazy-import variant of (1) still gives RELEVANT/ADEQUATE. Fix direction: - Attribute collection-time lines of a test module's import closure to the cases loaded from it, or declare line-coverage PARTIAL when a changed line executed only outside a case. - Have the audit hook set partial on subprocess.Popen, os.exec*, os.posix_spawn and os.system during a case.

**#80 [P2] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/quality/test_execution.py (docstring :21-23; _UNITTEST_RUNNER result-set write; execute)`  
*Impact:* A forged ADEQUATE reaches the tests/adequacy journal row even though no test ever ran. Reproduced: a story whose tests never passed assembled ADEQUATE (fixture withheld until fixed). The anti-gaming gate can be defeated by the party it is meant to police. The docstring claim of a harness-produced report is false for result_set. Bounded only because product proofs gate correctness independently.  
*Acceptance:* Add to tests/v2/p5/test_test_execution.py: a story test module that substitutes a schema-valid forged result set for the runner's own (fixture lands with the fix). It must never yield a TestExecution of EXECUTED/PASSED, and assess() with TestsPolicy(True) must never return ADEQUATE. It must give a typed refusal instead: UNRUNNABLE, or a distinct tamper outcome. Add a second negative test: a later regression run whose targets include that forging module plus a genuinely failing test must not read PASSED. Minimum fix: the runner proves it reached its post-suite epilogue through a harness-held channel opened before any test import, for example an inherited fd or a per-run nonce/MAC key removed from the child environment before import, as cli_invocation.py already does for probes. A missing or unauthenticated epilogue means no result set. Until it is fixed, the test_execution.py:21-23 docstring and README.md:52 declare the in-process limit, and no anti-gaming claim is made without it.

**#81 [P2] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/quality/test_execution.py:99-101,117-118,388-398,477 + aisef2/errors.py:8 + aisef2/app/run.py:691,697`  
*Impact:* One developer test file can abort every remaining story of the run. The fault is recorded as a kernel InvariantError, i.e. the subject's fault is misread as a controller invariant breach, and the attempt is left without story/end (E5 family). Measured: a malformed result set raises InvariantError, which is not an Exception.  
*Acceptance:* For each malformed result set (bad outcome value; IMPORT with no module; non-list field; missing keys; non-JSON) produced by a story test, te.execute returns a typed TestExecution (no result set gives UNRUNNABLE, or the tamper type of G4-2) and never raises. Red-before: a bad outcome value raises InvariantError, which is not an Exception. An integration test shows run.execute continues with the next story.

**#82 [P2] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/quality/vacuity.py (neutralise, evaluate) + aisef2/orchestrate/quality.py (assess)`  
*Impact:* False NON_VACUOUS, hence false ADEQUATE, for tests that check nothing about behaviour. Reproduced (fixture withheld until fixed). Vacuous tests pass the blocking gate.  
*Acceptance:* A withheld fixture (lands with the fix): a story test, with a real product change, that is credited NON_VACUOUS today without testing product behaviour. Red-before it gives NON_VACUOUS + RELEVANT = ADEQUATE; after, it never yields NON_VACUOUS. Mechanism: a control copy built by exactly the same procedure as the neutralised copy, with an empty neutralisation. Only cases that pass in the control and fail by assertion in the neutralised copy are attributed; a control failure gives INDETERMINATE. tests/v2/p5/test_vacuity.py:285-286 is updated accordingly.

**#83 [P2] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/quality/test_execution.py:307-314 (runner cause()),233-244 + aisef2/quality/adequacy.py:60,80-81 + aisef2/quality/vacuity.py:247-250`  
*Impact:* A story whose test files fail to collect for a cause the runner cannot determine is never blocked by the adequacy gate, and honest broken test files go through unnoticed. The record stays truthful (INCOMPLETE plus the reason), so this is gate evasion, not a false PASS. ADEQ_7 (tests/v2/p5/test_adequacy.py:118-125) pins this behaviour as frozen §15.0 rule 4 / §15.3.  
*Acceptance:* Owner decision needed (frozen §15.3; pinned by ADEQ_7, tests/v2/p5/test_adequacy.py:118-125). Red-before: a withheld fixture (a story test file whose collection fails with an undeterminable cause; lands with the fix), run through assess() with TestsPolicy(True), gives NOT_COLLECTABLE/INTEGRATION -> INCOMPLETE, failure None, and zero story cases executed. After the ruling, it never returns failure None with zero story cases executed: (a) an exception from a frame inside a story-owned test file is typed DEVELOPER, or (b) 'no story test executed' is a blocking mandatory-execution failure not charged to the developer. ADEQ_7 is updated to the ruling. **[owner decision registered in DEPENDENCY-DAG §5a with wave, STOP class and default; the lane builds the default.]**

**#84 [P2] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/quality/relevance.py:101-113,68-69 + aisef2/quality/vacuity.py:51-52,103-125`  
*Impact:* False INADEQUATE (blocking, DEVELOPER). Measured: a pure rename old.py->new.py with a test importing pkg.new gave IRRELEVANT + VACUOUS -> INADEQUATE. A binary change with a test that reads the changed file (artefact-access FULL should give RELEVANT) gave IRRELEVANT -> INADEQUATE.  
*Acceptance:* Red-before: a pure rename old.py->new.py with a test importing pkg.new gives VACUOUS + IRRELEVANT -> INADEQUATE, and a binary change read by the test gives IRRELEVANT -> INADEQUATE. After: (1) story_diff of a git rename patch yields an artefact (or an unmeasured entry) for the new path; (2) 'Binary files a/x and b/x differ' yields a non-line artefact measured by artefact-access, so a case that opened it is RELEVANT; (3) neutralise of a pure rename restores the old path or records a problem (INDETERMINATE); (4) the vacuity and relevance parsers produce the same artefact set over a shared corpus (rename, copy, binary, mode-only, empty new file).

**#85 [P2] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/quality/vacuity.py:204,208 (+ :184 copytree) + aisef2/orchestrate/story_runner.py:296-301`  
*Impact:* The developer can crash the quality stage with ordinary content. The story is REFUSED (run.py:691) instead of getting a typed INDETERMINATE, and the attempt is left open in the journal (E5). Measured: 'vacuity RAISED UnicodeDecodeError'.  
*Acceptance:* Red-before: neutralise() with a modified Latin-1 product text file raises UnicodeDecodeError and the story is REFUSED. After: neutralise() returns a Neutralisation with a problem entry and evaluate() returns INDETERMINATE. A simulated copytree failure (an unreadable file) also gives INDETERMINATE or a typed ResourceUnavailable. assess() always returns a Quality for any subject content.

**#86 [P3] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/quality/vacuity.py:269-273,261-263`  
*Impact:* The signal is weak: most new-feature stories end INCOMPLETE regardless of test quality, and VACUOUS is trivially evadable. Measured: `from pkg.m import g; g()` with no assertion gave INDETERMINATE -> INCOMPLETE. This is a spec-level limit of §15.1 ('exception rather than an assertion: cause not established').  
*Acceptance:* Owner decision needed (frozen §15.1: an exception rather than an assertion means the cause is not established). If kept, a test pins INDETERMINATE for an assertion-free test that does `from pkg.m import g; g()` on a story-added g, and the tests/adequacy evidence and docs state the limit. If changed, an ImportError/AttributeError naming a symbol or module the neutralisation removed (from the typed FilePatch set) is attributable, and that assertion-free test yields VACUOUS, not INCOMPLETE. **[critic fix: DECIDED — RFC §15.1 (RFC:834-835) + README.md:52-54 + OWNER-ANSWERS-2026-10-18.md:8: build the 'if kept' branch (INDETERMINATE pinned, ACCEPTED_LIMITATION stated). The 'if changed' branch is withdrawn: an ImportError attributable to the removed symbol satisfies §15.1 condition 4 and would make an assertion-free test NON_VACUOUS (a false ADEQUATE); VACUOUS needs a name-preserving neutralisation = a §15.1 amendment, owner-initiated only.]**

**#87 [P3] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/quality/test_execution.py:213-215`  
*Impact:* The owner depends on file order. INADEQUATE (blocking) turns into INCOMPLETE (non-blocking) when a file is renamed. This contradicts the comment at :212 ('never hidden').  
*Acceptance:* classify() over collection errors [IMPORT neither-resolution in tests/test_a.py, SYNTAX in tests/test_b.py] and over the reversed order returns the same TestExecution. Red-before: the first order gives INTEGRATION and the reversed gives DEVELOPER. Precedence: ENVIRONMENT, then DEVELOPER, then INTEGRATION.

**#88 [P3] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/quality/relevance.py:101 + aisef2/quality/vacuity.py:103`  
*Impact:* Wrong changed-line set: measured [4] where git truth is [3, 4], which can give a false IRRELEVANT and so a false INADEQUATE. Vacuity fails safe (opaque, INDETERMINATE). PEP 8 permits form feeds in Python source.  
*Acceptance:* Red-before: story_diff of a git patch whose context line is '# page\x0cbreak' returns changed lines [4], and file_patches marks the file opaque. After: changed lines are [3, 4] and file_patches is not opaque. Both parsers split on '\n' only, through one shared parser.

**#89 [P3] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/quality/test_execution.py:209,221-227`  
*Impact:* INADEQUATE (blocking, DEVELOPER) for environment absence, which conflicts with the spirit of INV-ABSENCE-NEVER-A-DEVELOPER-FAILURE. Measured: [all_skipped] -> INADEQUATE owner DEVELOPER.  
*Acceptance:* Owner decision needed on the typing. Red-before: story cases that are all skipped (skipUnless(shutil.which('git'))) give NO_STORY_TESTS_MATCHED, owner DEVELOPER -> INADEQUATE. After, they are typed distinctly from 'zero cases matched' (a separate selection value, or INCOMPLETE) and are never merged into NO_STORY_TESTS_MATCHED; the reason text no longer says 'none ran' for skipped cases. **[owner decision registered in DEPENDENCY-DAG §5a with wave, STOP class and default; the lane builds the default.]**

**#90 [P3] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/quality/test_execution.py:197-206 + aisef2/control/owner.py:142-144`  
*Impact:* The fault is misattributed: a developer defect is charged to the environment retry policy instead of the developer quality budget, and owner metrics are skewed. The story still does not commit, so this is not a false PASS.  
*Acceptance:* A story test that calls os._exit(0), or os.kill(os.getpid(), SIGTERM), at import produces a tests/adequacy row recording that the runner launched and died after importing story modules (the G4-2 epilogue marker), distinguishable from a real launch failure. Red-before: the row is identical to a launch failure. Repeated identical UNRUNNABLE on the same candidate is bounded, with a test counting retries.

**#91 [P3] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/orchestrate/quality.py:53-54 + aisef2/quality/test_execution.py:433-434`  
*Impact:* Any PYTEST configuration would make every story TESTS_UNRUNNABLE. Measured: PYTEST+--measure gave UNRUNNABLE 'no result set (exit 4)'; without the flag it gave EXECUTED.  
*Acceptance:* Red-before: assess() with te.PYTEST on a passing story gives UNRUNNABLE 'no result set (exit 4)'. After it gives EXECUTED: MEASURE is appended only for runners that declare a measurement capability, or PYTEST is removed from the exported runner set, with a test pinning the choice.

**#92 [P3] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/orchestrate/quality.py:60-61 + aisef2/quality/vacuity.py:184`  
*Impact:* Each attempt runs three full test executions plus a full tree copy. Wall time and disk grow with repository size, and ENOSPC there turns into the crash described in the non-UTF-8 item.  
*Acceptance:* Spy-runner test: when the story execution is UNRUNNABLE, the regression runner is invoked 0 times (red-before: once) and the assembly is unchanged. The neutralise copy is bounded by tracked files (git ls-files) or excludes untracked files; a test with an untracked 10 MB file shows it absent from the scratch tree.

**#93 [P3] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/orchestrate/quality.py:37-46`  
*Impact:* An INADEQUATE or INCOMPLETE that blocks a story cannot be audited from the journal: there is no record of why it was VACUOUS or IRRELEVANT. This is relevant to the W1 incremental-evidence rule ('do not represent reuse as measurement'), which needs the inputs.  
*Acceptance:* Format 3 stays frozen (V2-005 §4). The new fields go in FORMAT-4, or in an auxiliary evidence artefact bound from the row. Test: the journaled tests/adequacy row (or its artefact) round-trips Assembly.reason, vacuity neutralised/attributed/reason, relevance intersections and unmeasured. Red-before: those fields are absent. v2.2 format-3 journals still verify.

**#94 [P3] quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{va…** — `aisef2/quality/adequacy.py:3-4 vs aisef2/orchestrate/story_runner.py:302-305 + aisef2/app/run.py:672`  
*Impact:* Documentation overclaim. It hides that every false INADEQUATE above blocks delivery in production.  
*Acceptance:* The docstring states that blocking is policy and that the live run policy blocks INADEQUATE (TESTS_INADEQUATE stops the attempt before merge). A test asserts that run.py constructs TestsPolicy(True), and fails if the docstring and the policy disagree (grep-based meta-test).

**#95 [P2] secondary modules + critic overlaps A–E** — `aisef2/app/tripwire.py:119-124 (files counted :118 before the skip)`  
*Impact:* G1 gives a false CLEAN, which defeats DEVELOPER-CONFINEMENT-GAP-001. A stimulus or project bundle inside a large data/fixture file, a UTF-16 file or an archive is served to the developer while the record says the tree was scanned.  
*Acceptance:* Red-before: a file of 'x' * (2 MiB + 1) followed by the needle gives CLEAN with files_scanned=1 and hits=[], and the needle encoded as UTF-16 gives CLEAN. After: (a) a 3 MiB file containing a needle gives LEAK, via a chunked scan with overlap >= the longest needle or a refusal naming the skipped file; (b) the report carries skipped: [{file, reason}] and files_scanned excludes skipped files; (c) a UTF-16 file containing the needle is LEAK or listed as skipped/undecodable; (d) STATEMENT names the encoding and size scope. No path ends in a silent CLEAN.

**#96 [P3] secondary modules + critic overlaps A–E** — `aisef2/app/tripwire.py:36-51, 72-83 (STATEMENT :26-30)`  
*Impact:* The tripwire record overstates its coverage for numeric or short-token stimuli: an honest-looking CLEAN for a leak the tripwire cannot see.  
*Acceptance:* Today _fragments({'args': [123456789, 987654321]}) yields only composite needles, and scan_text('{"args": [123456789, 987654321]}', ...) == []. After: needles_of returns a format_sensitive list (contracts with no string-leaf needle >= MIN_NEEDLE), scan_repo copies it into its report, and STATEMENT says those contracts match only in canonical spelling. Test: that stimulus's contract appears in format_sensitive and not in the covered set.

**#97 [P3] secondary modules + critic overlaps A–E** — `aisef2/app/tripwire.py:97-102 (FOUND :31-33)`  
*Impact:* The operator is told something false and directed to hunt a read path that does not exist. A project that documents AISEF is refused with a misleading cause.  
*Acceptance:* Red-before: a README.md containing only a sentence naming 'aisef-v2-project/1' gives LEAK with contract '(the project bundle itself)' plus FOUND. After: it gets the label 'marker present, not parseable: cannot rule out the bundle', without FOUND appended, and the refusal remains fail-closed. A truncated real bundle that contains one of this project's contract ids keeps the bundle label.

**#98 [P3] secondary modules + critic overlaps A–E** — `aisef2/app/feedback.py:78-80 (proof lookup), cf. the attempt-bounded REVIEW branch :84-85`  
*Impact:* H-RETRY-001's claim of 'the evidence that decided it' is false for these codes: the developer is shown a previous attempt's REFUTED proof as the cause of an ENVIRONMENT mismatch. Only the prompt is affected; no verdict changes.  
*Acceptance:* 8-event journal: story/begin(1), probe/evaluated(2,3), proof/verified(4, candidate AAAA, REFUTED), failure(5, CONTRACT_UNSATISFIED), story/begin(6), probe/evaluated(7), failure(8, 'proof of c1: PROBE_MISMATCH'). Red-before: the row for seq 8 cites candidate AAAA and 'seq 2, 3 ... 4'. After: it contains no 'AAAA' and no 'seq 2, 3', and falls back to the generic row. The seq-5 CONTRACT_UNSATISFIED row is unchanged.

**#99 [P3] secondary modules + critic overlaps A–E** — `aisef2/app/feedback.py:68-105 (unbounded) vs :108-109 (PACK_MAX bound 'a Windows command line holds 32 767 characters'); app/adapters.py:79-85; app/client.py:347`  
*Impact:* On Windows (a real target platform), a fix-loop run with many blocking findings exceeds CreateProcess's 32,767-character limit. The session cannot start: RangeError at process_range.py:427. Developer.implement does not catch it, and story_runner catches only ProviderOutage/RangeEscaped (:241-251), so the error escapes untyped into #2 (no story/dispose or story/end).  
*Acceptance:* Red-before: 3 attempts with 8 failing review rows of 1500 characters make retry_feedback 36,239 characters. After: the retry section is bounded (RETRY_MAX, latest attempt first, an explicit '[bounded at N characters]' marker), and 30 failing review rows of 1500 characters give len(full developer prompt) <= 32_000 with the marker present. Second test: a session-start RangeError in Developer.implement becomes a typed CapabilityUnrunnable/ProviderOutage with story/dispose + story/end written.

**#100 [P3] secondary modules + critic overlaps A–E** — `aisef2/app/review_verify.py:306-307, 328-329 → app/adapters.py:161,165,170 → orchestrate/review.py:43-46`  
*Impact:* Sealed expected observables are written as plain text into the journal and the review-findings files under <out>/. An unconfined developer session on the next attempt can reach them (V21-02 class, and new exposure on top of #21). Any later change that feeds advisory rows to the developer would leak the oracle into the prompt. The tripwire does not catch it: the key subset is not one of the observable's needles.  
*Acceptance:* A CONTRADICTS_APPROVED finding against an approved contract whose observable is {'returns': 'SEALED-0123456789abcdef'}. Red-before: that value appears in the journal gate/check detail. After: 'SEALED-0123456789abcdef' appears in none of the journal gate/check detail, the review-findings artefact, or RUN.json['sessions'][*]['findings']; anchor reasons name contract ids and observable keys only. An ANCHORED blocking finding still gives the developer its own reproducer observable.

**#101 [P3] secondary modules + critic overlaps A–E** — `aisef2/app/diagnostics.py:41-47, 204-222 (written during the run); run.py:631-634, 660-661`  
*Impact:* Partial oracle information (outputs under the sealed stimulus) becomes available to an unconfined developer on a retry. This widens V21-02 in exchange for operator diagnostics.  
*Acceptance:* When access_of(...) != SESSIONS_CONFINED, the Sink keeps artefacts in memory (bounded by FILES_MAX x DETAIL_MAX) and run.py's finally writes them after the last model session; RUN.json records 'deferred'. Fake-client test: during the attempt-2 developer session, <out>/probe-observations does not exist or is empty (red-before: it holds attempt-1 facts); after execute() returns, the files exist and artifact_problems() is empty.

**#102 [P3] secondary modules + critic overlaps A–E** — `aisef2/orchestrate/adapters.py:76-101 (docstrings :2-4, :77-78, :92; read() :82-83)`  
*Impact:* The docstrings overclaim the guarantee. The scanner stage (security.scan, story_runner.py:314-319) relies on the review's tree check having just run and has no check of its own, so any reordering or new stage after review would lose integrity silently.  
*Acceptance:* Red-before on POSIX: after confine(), os.access(root, os.W_OK) is True and planting a file at the root succeeds. After: either the root is chmodded too (os.access(root, os.W_OK) is False), or the docstrings say 'advisory, same-user; integrity is the reviewer adapter's tree comparison'. read() is removed, or read('/etc/hosts') and read('../x') raise InvariantError. Test: a root-level file planted during review gives CapabilityUnrunnable, and security.scan is not reached.

**#103 [P3] secondary modules + critic overlaps A–E** — `aisef2/app/adapters.py:382-388 (RuffScanner.read), called unguarded at orchestrate/security.py:48`  
*Impact:* A truncated or garbled scanner report becomes an untyped crash of the attempt instead of CAPABILITY_UNRUNNABLE (owner ENVIRONMENT).  
*Acceptance:* read() returns Scanned(False, ()) for a report that is invalid JSON, non-UTF-8 bytes, a top-level value that is not a list, or rows that are not dicts. Each case is tested through security.scan(): the result is CAPABILITY_UNRUNNABLE and no exception propagates (red-before: ValueError / UnicodeDecodeError / AttributeError escape).

**#104 [P3] secondary modules + critic overlaps A–E** — `aisef2/app/calibrations.py:5-25 (rows carry no fixture digest); validation/qualification/release_calibration.py:123-132 (check()); tests/v2/test_v21_identity_bytes.py:36-42`  
*Impact:* Static admission check 9 trusts rows whose current validity is asserted, not re-checked. This matters to W1 (EVID-COUPLING-01): a calibration's impact inputs (fixture trees) cannot be compared mechanically from the row.  
*Acceptance:* (a) A fast unit test asserts that the shipped identity tuples equal the tuples derived from the catalog, in both directions. Red-before: an extra hand-added row in a COPY of calibrations.py passes test_v21_identity_bytes; after, the new test fails. (b) Either CI runs `python -P validation/qualification/release_calibration.py --check`, or the W1-DESIGN §3 resolve() binds each row to its fixture-tree sha256 and re-demonstrates only rows whose probe digest or fixture sha changed. Negative test: editing one negative fixture byte reports that row INVALIDATED, with aisef2/app/calibrations.py byte-unchanged.

## Subsystem maps (module + trust boundaries)

### run/execute

*Files inspected (8):* `aisef2/app/run.py`, `aisef2/app/verify.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/workspace.py`, `aisef2/app/lineage.py`, `aisef2/app/bundle_cli.py`, `aisef2/app/cli.py`, `docs/ROADMAP-2.2-CHECKLIST.md`, `docs/IMPROVEMENT-ROADMAP.md`

**Module map.** execute() (run.py:461) is the single run driver: one bundle + one settings + one never-reused out dir (L478). It writes RUN.started.json once before anything runs (L496-502, survives SIGKILL) and RUN.json in a `finally` (L700-733, B3).
