STAGE/FINALLY COUPLING: a single `rec["stage"]` string is advanced through the whole pipeline (repository L492 → invariants L515 → ocr identity L523 → client identity L534 → claim L547 → repository L577 → stimulus tripwire L590 → admission L595 → test interpreter L599 → confinement L604 → preflight L608 → run L628 → finished L696); on any failure the stage is captured in rec["failure"]["stage"] (L718). The `finally` (L700) closes the RunScope (_close_run L736: shutdown, else interrupt on ShutdownRefused) then re-derives the whole record FROM THE JOURNAL: delivery()/verdict (L374, L709), productproof() (L355, L714), budget.account()/budget_stops() (L451,L708), usage (L722), then writes RUN.json (L727) and the EVIDENCE manifest (L729). CURRENCY: within-run evidence currency = evidence_manifest() (L426) shas the record+journal+session streams+preflight+review-findings+probe-observations; anchor_of() (L446) = sha of that manifest's canonical JSON = the portable anchor `--verify --expect-anchor` re-derives; _content_sha() (L414) canonicalizes RUN.json so a re-serialised identical record matches. KERNEL_DIGEST (L93): sha256 over every aisef2/**/*.py, content LF-normalized (L99), sorted by Path; bound into the RunSpec at FULL enforcement (runspec_of L346) and recorded at L493; verify re-derives and compares (verify.py:72, :122). RESUME: the only continuation is V2.1 `--parent`/predecessor lineage (claim L546-576: spend inheritance, unread→refuse L557, unaccounted→refuse-unless-flag L571) — it records lineage, it does NOT reuse closed stories. Entry points: ocr_identity L178, runspec_of L336, layers_of L319, probe_factories L299, regression_tests L264, delivery L374, budget_stops L451, _close_run L736, _journal_info L752.

**Trust boundaries.** (1) MODEL↔HARNESS (untrusted sessions): developer/reviewer output is data; served tree scanned for hidden stimulus/bundle before serve (stimulus_tripwire L590-594, fail-closed), needles re-scanned per session (kw["needles"] L657, G1); client fence G2 (L639-657: run-scoped XDG data/state/cache, per-worktree OPENCODE_PERMISSION) is declared client-enforced, NOT OS confinement; real OS confinement measured not assumed when asked (confine.measure L604-607). (2) BINARY IDENTITY: OCR reviewer binary (ocr_identity L178) and client pin D4 (L532-545) are sha256 byte-identity against the operator's pin, re-checked at the moment of use — explicitly NOT build provenance (L172, L539). (3) KERNEL IDENTITY: kernel_digest bound FULL into runspec (L346) + 9 invariants armed in THIS process (A1, L515-520); verify only compares invariant digests when verifying kernel == run's (verify.py:122). (4) SPEND/LINEAGE: predecessor evidence is untrusted and append-only — unread spend refuses with no flag override (L557-570), unaccounted refuses unless --accept-unaccounted (L571-575); nothing of the predecessor is rewritten (one claim generation only). (5) SECRETS: provider key + client_env values with len≥8 are redacted from all session evidence (secrets tuple L653) and from the diagnostics sink (L660). (6) GIT CONFIG (B4): all git routed through workspace.git() under _ISOLATED_GIT; a repo whose own config holds a non-inert key is refused (REFUSED=128). (7) EVIDENCE/VERDICT: delivery verdict comes from the JOURNAL (L374-390), cross-checked against adapter `results`; plan quality NOT_CLAIMED, generalization NONE (L713). Assumption: check_git() (L578) verifies git ≥2.31 once at start; the finally assumes that git/filesystem stay available (see defect 1).

**Relevant IDs:** Implements: A1 (invariants armed in execute L515-520, verify-comparable), Implements: B3 (RUN.json in finally L700-733 — but see defect 1), Implements: B4 (check_git L578 + workspace.git isolation), Implements: B6 (probe_interpreter venv L253; probes run off it, V2.1 L256), Implements: C2 (Budget.max_cost dollar ceiling L636-638), Implements: D4 (client binary pin L532-545, re-checked at use), Implements: G1 (stimulus tripwire L590-594 + per-session needles L657), Implements: G2 (client fence XDG/OPENCODE_PERMISSION L639-657, declared-not-OS), Implements: PM-8 (diagnostics sink + probe-observations L658-661,719-720), Implements: RFC §12 (static plan admission admit() L114/L595-598), Implements: V2.1 continue/lineage `--parent` (claim L546-576; CONTINUED-BY), OCR identity (ocr_identity L178), confinement F (L603-607), evidence manifest/anchor (L426-448), Implements: V2-007 (probe EXECUTION_DIGEST binding L350), Implements: IR-02 (budget_stops = actual stop not ceiling-sitting L451-458), Implements: H-REGRESSION-001 (regression_tests L264-270), Still needs: E5 (typed story Stage sequence + per-story finally story/dispose+story/end; story loop L674-695 currently only try/except around sr.run_story — UNCHECKED in ROADMAP-2.2-CHECKLIST L115), Still needs: G6 (`aisef run --resume`: reuse closed stories only on fingerprint {bundle_hash, kernel_digest, route, base} match + per-story why_not — NOT implemented anywhere; execute() has only `--parent` lineage, a different mechanism; kernel_digest already recorded L493 is the fingerprint input G6 would consume — UNCHECKED L131), Still needs: E1 (verdict as closed projection fold run in verify; delivery() L374 is an ad-hoc computation in run.py parallel to verify's projections — UNCHECKED), Still needs: E6 (single story state-transition table shared by writer and projection — UNCHECKED), Still needs: A2 (keyed/signed head + external anchoring; anchor_of L446 is an unsigned sha256 — UNCHECKED)

**Defects:**
- **[P2]** `aisef2/app/run.py:710` — Unguarded git subprocess (and journal read at L705) inside the record-writing `finally` can raise OSError and skip the RUN.json write at L727, defeating B3.  
  *Impact:* B3 (the record is written whatever happens — exception, invariant failure, refused shutdown, interruption) is the whole reason the finally exists, yet L705 `_journal_info` does path.read_bytes() and L710 `git(out/'repo','rev-parse','main')` runs a subprocess, both BEFORE the RUN.json write at L727 and both unguarded. Every other I/O in this block is defensively wrapped (usage try/except L721-726, manifest try/except L728-732, _close_run try/finally L702-704) — the asymmetry shows L705/L710 are an oversight. On resource exhaustion during a ~33-min heavy run (EMFILE/ENFILE makes subprocess.run raise OSError via workspace.config_problems L55 / git L71), or if git leaves PATH, the exception propagates out of execute(), RUN.json is never written, AND it masks the original `failure` being re-raised. Fix (lazy/root): wrap L705 and L710 best-effort (default to None) like the sibling usage/manifest blocks so the record write at L727 is always reached.  
  *Repro/test:* In the `finally`, force git(out/'repo','rev-parse','main') (or _journal_info's read_bytes) to raise OSError (e.g. monkeypatch workspace.git to raise, or exhaust fds) and assert out/'RUN.json' still exists — today it does not.
- **[P3]** `aisef2/app/run.py:98` — kernel_digest iterates sorted(root.rglob('*.py')) by platform-flavored Path, not by normalized relative posix path; content is LF-normalized but iteration order is not.  
  *Impact:* The running sha256 is order-dependent (L98-99). sorted() compares Path objects by the OS flavour's normcase (POSIX: case-sensitive, '/'=0x2F; Windows: lowercased, '\\'=0x5C). A sibling dir `foo/` and file `foo<c>.py` with 0x2F<c<0x5C (digits/upper-case) sort in opposite order on the two platforms, so the SAME source tree can yield different kernel_digest cross-platform — while the code already LF-normalizes content (L99) precisely for cross-platform stability. Because kernel_digest is bound FULL into the runspec (L346), cross-platform `--verify` would HARD-FAIL runspec re-derivation (verify.py:72) and silently skip invariant-digest comparison (verify.py:122-123). Given Windows is a live release constraint and anchors are meant to be portable, this is a real latent gap. Verified: NO divergence in the current tree (no flipping sibling pair exists today), so latent, not currently biting. One-line fix: `sorted(root.rglob('*.py'), key=lambda p: p.relative_to(root).as_posix())`.  
  *Repro/test:* Add a dir `aisef2/zz/` with any .py plus a sibling file `aisef2/zz2.py`; compute digest with the current Path sort vs an as_posix-key sort under PureWindowsPath vs PurePosixPath flavour — the two platforms diverge. (Script in session confirmed current tree does not diverge.)
- **[P3]** `aisef2/app/run.py:722` — Tuple-assignment couples rec['no_progress'] to usage.usage() success; a usage.usage() failure drops no_progress even though it only needs `events`.  
  *Impact:* `rec['usage'], rec['no_progress'] = usage.usage(out,...), usage.no_progress(events)` evaluates the whole RHS before binding; if usage.usage() raises, the except (L725) sets only rec['usage']={'error':...}, so rec['no_progress'] is never written although usage.no_progress(events) depends solely on in-memory events and would have succeeded. Consumers reading rec['no_progress'] get a silently-absent field on a usage error path. Minor; split the two assignments so no_progress is computed independently of usage.usage().  
  *Repro/test:* Monkeypatch usage.usage to raise; assert 'no_progress' missing from RUN.json while events were non-empty.

### verify/authenticity

*Files inspected (13):* `aisef2/app/verify.py`, `aisef2/app/run.py`, `aisef2/invariants/registry.py`, `aisef2/app/usage.py`, `aisef2/app/cli.py`, `aisef2/app/review_verify.py`, `aisef2/app/diagnostics.py`, `aisef2/app/client.py`, `docs/IMPROVEMENT-ROADMAP.md`, `docs/ROADMAP-2.2-CHECKLIST.md`, `docs/v2.3/BASELINE.md`, `closure-evidence/v2/v2.2/B1-BINDING-AMENDMENT-1.json`…

**Module map.** verify.py is the `aisef run --verify` re-derivation engine: given a finished run dir + project bundle, return {verdict VERIFIED/REFUTED, problems, delivery_verdict, journal_events, evidence_anchor, notes}. Byte-identical to frozen v2.2.0 baseline da3ea33f (git diff vs HEAD empty); last change e3d8d1a4 (Lane B-A malformed-digest fail-close).
Entry point `verify(project, out, expect_anchor=None)` verify.py:131 — wired at cli.py:27/77-78 (`--verify --expect-anchor`); also called by demo.py:161, bundle_cli re-uses its helpers.
Pipeline order inside verify(): (1) load RUN.json, require format R.FORMAT (verify.py:135-139); (2) EVIDENCE.json manifest: read stored, re-derive fresh via R.evidence_manifest, diff files, compute anchor R.anchor_of (verify.py:141-166); (3) expect_anchor check (verify.py:167-172); (4) bundle_digest == project.digest (verify.py:173); (5) journal: read bytes RUN.json names, sha256+line-count match, format3.reconstruct, `_projection_refusal` over the 6 PROJECTIONS, torn_tail (verify.py:175-201); (6) big try (verify.py:205-258) re-derives review/probe artifacts, story outcomes, `_runspec_problems`, `_invariant_problems`, `_spend`, budget, delivery verdict R.delivery, productproof, usage, final_main.
Key helpers: `_projection_refusal(events)` verify.py:41 (admits only a journal the kernel's Authority.append could have written); `_runspec_problems` verify.py:56 (binds record settings to journal by re-deriving runspec_hash from capability/resolved + run/spec-resolved); `_spend` verify.py:78 (re-derives spend from session streams + preflight ledger + lineage, returns ceiling-break + un-reconstructable-lineage finding); `_invariant_problems` verify.py:102 (A1: shape-checks registry_digest/code_mechanism_digest as 64-hex, then value-compares only when kernel_digest matches). Invariant digests written by run.execute() at run.py:516-520 (arm_runtime → registry_digest + code_mechanism_digest), names consistent with registry.ArmedRuntime registry.py:399-403.

**Trust boundaries.** Operator/developer writes the run directory (RUN.json, journal, sessions, preflight, EVIDENCE.json) — all UNTRUSTED input to verify. Core discipline, correctly implemented: nothing read from the record can RAISE a verdict; the record/evidence can only LOWER it (failure→FAIL verify.py:240, refused stories verify.py:238, budget_stop verify.py:239). The delivery verdict is re-derived from the journal (R.delivery, R.outcomes_from), not read from RUN.json; record outcomes are only compared (verify.py:214-216,242-243).
Journal admissibility boundary (B-03 r3): a journal that fails `_projection_refusal` or does not reconstruct yields events=[] and nothing is re-derived from it (verify.py:191-201) — fail-closed.
Authenticity boundary (the declared ceiling, docstring verify.py:11-13): the hash chain (event.py) and anchor (R.anchor_of = unkeyed sha256 of EVIDENCE.json, run.py:446-448) are UNKEYED, and the dir is writable by the operator (OS-confinement is V21-02 OPEN). So without `expect_anchor` from an off-box store, verify proves only internal CONSISTENCY, not AUTHENTICITY: a whole-directory consistent rewrite VERIFIES. `expect_anchor` raises this to "authentic as far as the pinned value came from a store the developer cannot write" (note verify.py:169-172, threat model docs/v2.1/ANCHOR-THREAT-MODEL.md). Kernel-dependent invariant digests are only value-compared when rec.kernel_digest == this process's (verify.py:122-123); cross-kernel they are a note, not a check — correct, since the run's enforcement bytes are not in the dir.
Robustness boundary: all per-field re-derivation runs under broad try/except (verify.py:200,257) so a damaged/wrong-type field or unreadable stream is a REFUTED finding, never a traceback.

**Relevant IDs:** A1 (IMPLEMENTED here: --verify re-derives runtime invariant arming + registry/mechanism digests, verify.py:102-128,220; malformed-digest fail-close verify.py:116-119 = Lane B-A e3d8d1a4, B1-BINDING-AMENDMENT-1/3), B1 (IMPLEMENTED: malformed-digest fail-close product fix bound by tests/v2/test_b1gap2_verify__invariant_problems.py; live canary --verify VERIFIED 2026-11-06), B-03 round 3 (IMPLEMENTED: _projection_refusal journal-admissibility boundary, verify.py:41-53,191-197, commit 6086e31c), V21-30 / A2 / D5 (STILL NEEDED: keyed chain / signed head + external anchoring; --verify --expect-pubkey — authenticity beyond consistency, Wave 4), E1 (STILL NEEDED: delivery verdict as a projection/fold of the closed projection set, oracle_problems() run inside verify, shadowed over existing evidence), V21-02 (RELATED/OPEN: OS-confinement of the developer process — the writable run directory is the premise of the A2 authenticity gap), A2-key-decision QĐ#1 (CHỐT: per-run ephemeral keypair, public half escrowed off-box like today's anchor)

**Defects:**
- **[P2]** `aisef2/app/verify.py:167-172 (with run.py:446-448, event.py chain)` — --verify proves consistency, not authenticity, unless expect_anchor comes from an off-box store: anchor and link chain are unkeyed sha256 over a directory the operator can rewrite, so a whole-directory consistent rewrite VERIFIES.  
  *Impact:* The headline trust guarantee is bounded; honestly declared in the module docstring, the expect_anchor note, and README Known-limitations, but still the open gap. This is roadmap A2 / V21-30 / D5, scheduled for Wave 4, not yet implemented.  
  *Repro/test:* Run --verify on a finished dir without --expect-anchor, then rewrite RUN.json+journal+sessions+EVIDENCE.json consistently (regenerate the manifest) → VERIFIED. Covered-as-known by docs/v2.1/ANCHOR-THREAT-MODEL.md; closed only by keyed chain/external anchoring (A2).
- **[P3]** `aisef2/app/verify.py:251 (usage.usage uses rec at usage.py:102,114)` — The usage re-derivation is self-referential for wall_s/timestamps: usage.usage() computes wall_s from rec['started']/rec['finished'], then verify compares rec['usage'] against that output — so wall_s and the two timestamps are checked against themselves, not against evidence.  
  *Impact:* A record can carry a consistent (started, finished, wall_s) triple that is never bound to the journal/evidence. Benign today: the usage block can only lower the verdict (append a problem), never raise one, and tokens/cost ARE re-derived from the session streams. Same self-referential-comparison class as the B1 registry_digest gap (18/19 mutants survived), here on a non-verdict field.  
  *Repro/test:* Set rec['started']/['finished']/['usage']['wall_s'] to any internally consistent values with session streams unchanged → usage comparison passes. A binding test would re-derive wall_s from a journal/evidence timestamp instead of rec.
- **[P3]** `aisef2/app/verify.py:72` — In _runspec_problems, spec['runspec_hash'] is read OUTSIDE the try that guards the sibling re-derivation (verify.py:67-71 catches KeyError/TypeError/ValueError), so a run/spec-resolved event missing runspec_hash raises KeyError that escapes to the generic catch-all at verify.py:257.  
  *Impact:* Still fail-closed (REFUTED, no traceback leaked — contract holds), but the cause degrades from the specific 'settings not bound' finding to the generic 'the record cannot be re-derived from the evidence: KeyError'. Asymmetric with line 70 which does catch KeyError. Diagnostic-quality nit only.  
  *Repro/test:* Craft a journal whose run/spec-resolved data has 'revision' but no 'runspec_hash' → generic KeyError finding instead of a runspec-binding finding.

### client/preflight

*Files inspected (7):* `aisef2/app/client.py`, `aisef2/app/preflight.py`, `aisef2/runtime/process_range.py`, `aisef2/app/run.py`, `aisef2/app/settings.py`, `aisef2/app/confine.py`, `docs/ROADMAP-2.2-CHECKLIST.md`

**Module map.** client.py = the paid OpenCode session driver + the run's budget, all re-derived from evidence (no live counter). Entry points: session() client.py:298 (one `opencode run --format json` in a ProcessRange; refuses when model/env/budget/turn_cap unbound 308-311, when log exists 313, or budget.reached 313; applies D4 pin-at-use 319-323, G1 tripwire 324-328, G2 fence-via-env 330-346, V2.1 confine 348-352, redact 363-364). Budget class 130 (spend() 152 re-derives provider_requests/turns/tokens from session *.jsonl + preflight accounting + journal provider/request count; reached() 195 and exceeded() 208 enforce 4 counters + optional C2 cost ceiling 169-171,204-205). environment() 227 builds the session env (keeps PATH/HOME/locale + XDG_* + settings-named vars + route overlay; run-scoped XDG G2 236-238; deliberately KEEPS XDG_CONFIG_HOME, the key-bearing config). overlay() 242 pins model+small_model to the one route. configured_provider() 251 reads (endpoint,key) from opencode.json; key never printed. read_session()/events() 87-127 parse the stream for text/error/turns/tokens only. _release_session() 51 + _is_gone() 32 = PL-7 escapee-grace teardown. redact() 283 scrubs secret bytes from the log. preflight.py = one-time route attestation before any session. Entry points: run() 87 (GET /models + N chat probes via http_transport, writes LEDGER.jsonl + PREFLIGHT.json, verdict ATTESTED/OPAQUE); http_transport() 37 (forces stream:false, bearer key never recorded, _NoRedirect opener 25-34 refuses redirects so the key is never re-sent — 360-sec W8); identity_problems() 124 (listing lists the declared model+owner, every probe served by served_model, one system_fingerprint); fingerprint_fields() 144 / deployment() 151 feed the attested capability. Both driven from run.py:608-669.

**Trust boundaries.** Provider endpoint is UNTRUSTED (a router that can alias/re-route). Preflight treats every provider answer as data: refuses redirects without re-sending the key (preflight.py:29,57), demands the declared model in the listing + every probe served by served_model + a single deployment fingerprint (identity_problems 124-141); mixed exposed/NOT_EXPOSED fingerprints or 0-vs-1 => OPAQUE. KEY GAP: attestation is a one-shot gate — bound only at preflight (run.py:342-343 attested(preflight=fingerprint_fields), 618-622) and never re-checked during the paid multi-turn sessions (read_session client.py:105-127 ignores served model; no `provider/attestation` journal event exists anywhere in the tree). So the trust boundary is enforced for the 2 cheap probes, not for the expensive turns. The confined session (bwrap) is a trust boundary around the model+its tools: G1 needles scan (client.py:324), G2 fence declares filesystem deny outside the worktree (330-346, CLIENT-enforced, explicitly NOT measured), D4 pins the binary (319). But the provider key sits INSIDE that boundary: a real paid session needs network=host (confine.py:18-19,130-131 — `none` is measurement-only), and the key-bearing client CONFIG is kept reachable (client.py:234 keeps XDG_CONFIG_HOME; run.py:639-641) and/or the key env var is in client_env (run.py:653). redact() only cleans the key out of recorded evidence after the fact; it does not stop a tool from reading+exfiltrating it live. Process-liveness boundary: ownership is the OS's (process group / Job object), never PID equality; _is_gone (client.py:32) is fail-closed — only a PROVABLY absent/reused pid counts as gone, every unreadable probe reads ALIVE and refuses the session; pid_alive never signals (read-only os.kill(pid,0) / OpenProcess limited-query, FINDING-WIN-OSKILL-001). Budget boundary: fail-closed — anything unaccounted (sessions>requests, a step with no tokens, a started session with 0 turns, unread lineage) ends the run like a reached ceiling.

**Relevant IDs:** C7 (STILL NEEDS — in-session attestation, roadmap unchecked, checklist:123), D3 (STILL NEEDS — loopback key-broker / no key in session env / egress allowlist, unchecked, decision #3, checklist:127), D4 (IMPLEMENTS — client pin re-checked at use, client.py:319-323), G1 (IMPLEMENTS — tripwire needle scan per session, client.py:324-328), G2 (IMPLEMENTS — client fence declared via env + run-scoped XDG, client.py:330-346,236-238; declared-not-measured), C2 (IMPLEMENTS — dollar ceiling in Budget, client.py:140,169-171,204-205,217-218), B1/B7 (PARTIAL — paid-run four-counter budget, Budget class client.py:130-224; cost=C2), PL-7 (IMPLEMENTS — escapee grace teardown, client.py:26-29,51-75,362), V2.0 charter §6 (IMPLEMENTS — unbound session refused before lookup, client.py:308-311), V2.1 (IMPLEMENTS — redact client.py:283, inherited-lineage budget 142-150, confine wrap 348-352; 360-sec W8 no-redirect opener preflight.py:25-34,57), P4-LIFETIME-SEMANTICS §3 (IMPLEMENTS — _release_session + ProcessRange.release), FINDING-WIN-OSKILL-001 (IMPLEMENTS — read-only liveness probe, client.py:32-48, process_range.py:128-181)

**Defects:**
- **[P2]** `aisef2/app/client.py:105-127 (read_session) + run.py:342-343,618-622 (attest once)` — C7 unimplemented: the fixed route is attested only by the one-time preflight (2 cheap probes); the served model is never re-checked during the paid multi-turn developer/reviewer sessions. read_session parses text/error/turns/tokens but never inspects the per-step served model, and no `provider/attestation` journal event or in-run drift check exists anywhere in the tree.  
  *Impact:* A dishonest or misconfigured router can serve the declared model for the two preflight probes, then silently bait-and-switch to a cheaper/different model for every billed turn with zero detection. Undermines the whole model-identity guarantee for exactly the spend that matters; cost ceilings catch gross overspend but not model substitution.  
  *Repro/test:* grep the tree for served_model/system_fingerprint/attestation => only preflight.py and run.py:342-343/618 match; read_session() has no served-model branch. Roadmap C7 (docs/ROADMAP-2.2-CHECKLIST.md:123) is unchecked.
- **[P2]** `aisef2/app/client.py:234 (environment keeps XDG_CONFIG_HOME) + run.py:653 (key env var in client_env) + confine.py:18-19,130-131 (network=host)` — D3 unimplemented: the provider key is reachable from INSIDE the confined paid session. A real session runs network=host (egress unconfined by design, since `none` leaves no route to the provider), and the key reaches the client either via the kept XDG_CONFIG_HOME config (literal key) or via the api_key_env variable passed through client_env. redact() (client.py:283) only scrubs the key from the recorded evidence after the run, not from the live environment.  
  *Impact:* A tool the model executes during a session can read the provider key (from env or opencode.json) and POST it to any host over the unconfined egress; the run's own redaction would not prevent exfiltration, only hide it from the stored log. No loopback key-broker / egress allowlist / post-run assert-env-has-no-key, which is exactly what D3 specifies.  
  *Repro/test:* run.py:616 resolves key; run.py:653 adds it to `secrets`; environment() client.py:233-234 whitelists XDG_CONFIG_HOME; confine.py:130-131 forbids network=none for a model session. Roadmap D3 (checklist:127) unchecked, waiting owner decision #3.
- **[P3]** `aisef2/app/client.py:42-48 (_is_gone) via process_range.py:98-119 (_ps_table/process_table)` — On native Windows _is_gone's pid-reuse detection is dead: process_table() routes non-Linux to _ps_table() which shells out to `ps` (absent on Windows) and raises FileNotFoundError, caught at client.py:43 as present=None, so the start-time reuse check (45-47) never runs and the code falls through to pid_alive() alone. Separately, the whole native-Windows liveness path (_win_liveness, pid_alive nt-branch) is currently unreachable in production: _is_gone is only called from _release_session on RangeEscaped, and on Windows _Job.escaped() always returns [] (process_range.py:339-340, no BREAKAWAY_OK), so RangeEscaped never fires.  
  *Impact:* Low/latent: the degraded Windows path is still fail-closed (pid_alive reads a reused pid as ALIVE => session conservatively refused, the safe direction) and is not exercised in production because Job objects yield no escapees. But the carefully-written _win_liveness is effectively untested against real Windows process reuse, and process_table() would raise rather than work if ever reached on Windows. Worth a comment or a guard rather than silent reliance on the escapee path being impossible.  
  *Repro/test:* On Windows, call client._is_gone(pid, proc): process_table()->_ps_table() raises FileNotFoundError('ps'); confirm present=None and reuse-check skipped. On any OS, confirm _Job.escaped() returns [] so _release_session never calls _is_gone under a Job.

### security/confine

*Files inspected (8):* `aisef2/app/confine.py`, `aisef2/app/tripwire.py`, `aisef2/app/settings.py`, `aisef2/runtime/process_range.py`, `aisef2/runtime/run_scope.py`, `aisef2/app/client.py`, `aisef2/app/run.py`, `aisef2/app/diagnostics.py`

**Module map.** NOTE: the task named aisef2/app/runtime/process_range.py + run_scope.py; actual shipped paths are aisef2/runtime/process_range.py + run_scope.py (no aisef2/app/runtime/ dir exists). Audited the real files.

confine.py (584 LOC): V2.1 "REPORT F" bubblewrap confinement of the opencode client's model sessions (developer + model reviewer), Linux-only. Entry points: problem() (confine.py:120, settings-time validation), wrap()→argv() (confine.py:160/177, builds the bwrap command line + inside-env), measure()→_probe()→_judge() (confine.py:313/349/529, runs a PROBE (confine.py:58) inside the real wrapper against a worktree of the run's repo and refuses the run on any deviation), git_dir() (confine.py:139, resolves a worktree's common .git to bind RO), _mounts()/_owned()/_writes() (confine.py:242/466/518, writable-mount ownership judgement), _survivors() (confine.py:433, setsid-escapee liveness via runtime.process_range.process_table). Wired at run.py:603-607 (measure before first provider request, RunRefused on problems) and client.py:348-352 (confinement.wrap applied to the session argv/env).

tripwire.py (139 LOC): ROADMAP-2.2 G1 serve-surface leak tripwire (NOT confinement — STATEMENT at tripwire.py:26). needles_of() (tripwire.py:62) builds needles from each contract's stimulus/observable, dropping needles that occur in public bundle text; scan_repo() (tripwire.py:110) scans the checked-out tree; scan_text() (tripwire.py:86) reports contract-id + length, never the value; _bundle_hit() (tripwire.py:95) detects the project's own bundle. Wired at run.py:591-594 (tree scan at baseline) and client.py:324-328 / adapters.py:240 (prompt+env re-scan at every session / OCR review).

settings.py (267 LOC): run-settings schema + fail-closed loader. endpoint_problem() (settings.py:72) enforces https-or-http-to-loopback with no embedded credentials; load() (settings.py:136) validates confinement (via confine.problem), client_pin (D4), client_fence (G2), review/ocr mutual-exclusion with confinement (settings.py:180). LOOPBACK allowlist at settings.py:69.

runtime/process_range.py (598 LOC): OS-owned process range (WP-4.2). process_table()/_linux_table()/_ps_table() (process_range.py:117/78/98); pid_alive()/_win_liveness() (process_range.py:147/128, fail-closed liveness, avoids destructive signal-0 on Windows); _Posix/_Job backends (process_range.py:214/266, session+pgid vs Job KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE); ProcessRange.release()/_ladder()/_release() (process_range.py:523/563/576, single-flight graceful-first SIGINT→TERM→KILL, RangeNotEmpty/RangeEscaped residuals). descendants() (process_range.py:184) is report-only, never signalled.

runtime/run_scope.py (284 LOC): RunLease (run_scope.py:58, exclusive OS flock/msvcrt lock = outermost ownership boundary, RUN-2) + RunScope begin/shutdown/interrupt lifetime (run_scope.py:111/180/206). require_armed(InvariantId.II) gate at run_scope.py:112 (A1).

**Trust boundaries.** Confinement scope is NARROW and self-declared (confine.py:1-31): ONLY the two opencode client model sessions (developer, reviewer) are sandboxed. Everything else runs as the operator's user, unconfined — the docstring explicitly excludes "the kernel's own execution of the developer's tests and the probes' execution of the candidate" (confine.py:29-31). So candidate-authored product/test code executes outside any sandbox with full operator privilege + network = the D1 boundary that is NOT yet built.

Egress is intentionally UNconfined: network="host" → argv emits --share-net (confine.py:164); "none" is validation-only and refused in run settings (confine.py:130-132, settings.py). The session shares the host network and can reach any host, not just the attested provider endpoint. No egress allowlist exists (D3). The provider key reaches the session: wrap() binds the client config dir RO (confine.py:195-196 via config_dir) and keeps operator client_env secrets in the inside-env (confine.py:197), so a compromised session has both the key and open network. This is V21-02 ("the run directory … remains host-reachable from an unconfined session") stated in tripwire.STATEMENT (tripwire.py:26-30).

Platform boundary: confinement is bwrap/Linux-only; wrap() fail-closes (raises Unavailable) on non-Linux (confine.py:181-182) and measure() refuses the run — correct fail-closed behaviour — but there is NO macOS/Windows backend (D2). When the operator omits the setting entirely, sessions run unconfined on every OS (confinement is opt-in; settings.confinement defaults None, settings.py:110).

Fail-closed posture is otherwise strong and consistent: measure() refuses on any unmeasured/denied probe check (confine.py:529-584, "a check that could not run is a problem, never a pass"); _owned() treats any unestablished writable mount as a leak (confine.py:480); pid_alive/_win_liveness read ALIVE on any ambiguity (process_range.py:128-144); RunLease is a real OS lock released with the process (run_scope.py:58-87); tripwire refusals never echo the stimulus value (tripwire.py:86-92).

Config-provenance boundary: the tripwire scans only the checked-out working tree (.git excluded, tripwire.py:116) whereas the sessions bind the FULL common git object store RO (confine.py:196 git_dir; the run clone is a full --no-hardlinks clone, run.py:580) — a provenance asymmetry (see defect 1). settings.endpoint_problem relies on string-level loopback matching (settings.py:69,87): http to "localhost" is trusted, so /etc/hosts redirection of localhost could carry the key in cleartext — accepted design tradeoff, documented at settings.py:73-74.

**Relevant IDs:** F, G1, G2, D4, D1, D2, D3, V21-02, DEVELOPER-CONFINEMENT-GAP-001, A1, C2, INV-MUTATION-AUTHORITY, QĐ #3

**Defects:**
- **[P2]** `aisef2/app/tripwire.py:116 (vs aisef2/app/confine.py:196)` — G1 serve-surface tripwire scans only the checked-out working tree and excludes .git, but the developer/reviewer sessions bind the full common git object store read-only (git_dir), so sealed stimulus/observable or the project bundle committed to any git object/ref not present in the baseline working tree is readable by the developer yet never scanned.  
  *Impact:* Proof independence (DEVELOPER-CONFINEMENT-GAP-001) can be defeated for git-object-hidden content: a false CLEAN verdict while the developer can retrieve the sealed stimulus, undermining the whole tripwire guarantee for that class of leak.  
  *Repro/test:* Commit the project bundle (or a contract's stimulus) in one commit, delete it in a later commit on main. scan_repo walks the clean baseline tree → verdict CLEAN (tripwire.py:110-131). Inside the developer session `git cat-file --batch-all-objects` / `git log -p` reads the content from the RO-bound object store. The run clone is a full clone (run.py:580), so all source history is present.
- **[P2]** `aisef2/app/confine.py:18-20, 164` — Egress is not confined: network=host emits --share-net; a model session can reach any host, not only the attested provider endpoint, while holding the provider API key (config bound RO, confine.py:195-196) and operator client_env secrets (confine.py:197). No egress allowlist (D3) and no key-broker.  
  *Impact:* A prompt-injected or misbehaving model session can exfiltrate the provider key and run artifacts to an arbitrary host. Disclosed (V21-02) but still a live exfiltration surface the sandbox does not close.  
  *Repro/test:* Inspect argv (confine.py:160-174): --share-net is emitted for network!=none; the inside-env (confine.py:197-199) retains client_env variables; the client config holding the key is in the RO bind list. No code restricts destination hosts. Fix = D3 (loopback key-broker + egress allowlist to the attested endpoint; measure() should assert the session env carries no key).
- **[P2]** `aisef2/app/confine.py:29-31` — D1 not implemented: only the opencode client sessions are wrapped (client.py:348-352). The kernel's execution of the developer's tests and the probes' execution of the candidate run as the operator's user, unconfined — candidate-authored code executes with full host privilege and network during grading.  
  *Impact:* Arbitrary candidate code runs outside any sandbox at test/probe time; a malicious or buggy candidate can read/write the host, reach the network, and touch run evidence. This is the core D1 confinement gap for the subject-under-test.  
  *Repro/test:* Grep confine.wrap call sites: only client.session (client.py:350) and diagnostics.access_of (diagnostics.py:103) build a wrapper; the test interpreter (run.py:600 probe_interpreter) and probe/candidate execution are launched without confine.wrap. Tracked as D1 (ROADMAP-2.2-CHECKLIST.md:125, QĐ #3 pending).
- **[P3]** `aisef2/app/confine.py:124-125, 181-182` — D2 not implemented: bwrap is the only backend and wrap() raises Unavailable on any non-Linux host, so macOS/Windows have no sandbox at all. Confinement is fail-closed (a run requesting it on macOS/Windows is refused) but there is no Seatbelt/sandbox-exec equivalent, and when the operator omits confinement, every OS runs sessions unconfined.  
  *Impact:* No OS isolation is available on the project's own macOS/Windows developer+CI platforms; confinement is Linux-only and opt-in. Refusal is correct fail-closed behaviour, so the risk is a missing capability rather than a bypass.  
  *Repro/test:* confine.problem rejects any backend != 'bwrap' (confine.py:124-125); wrap() raises Unavailable for system not startswith 'linux' (confine.py:181-182); measure() short-circuits to problems on non-Linux (confine.py:329-330). Tracked as D2 (ROADMAP-2.2-CHECKLIST.md:126, QĐ #3 pending).

### authoring

*Files inspected (12):* `aisef2/app/bundle.py`, `aisef2/app/bundle_cli.py`, `aisef2/app/cli.py`, `aisef2/app/feedback.py`, `aisef2/plan/obligation.py`, `aisef2/plan/static_admission.py`, `aisef2/plan/story_admission.py`, `aisef2/plan/drift.py`, `aisef2/plan/__init__.py`, `aisef2/app/run.py`, `docs/v2.3/BASELINE.md`, `docs/ROADMAP-2.2-CHECKLIST.md`

**Module map.** The authoring lane turns a human-written authoring doc into a sealed, approved, admitted project bundle, and builds the developer/reviewer prompts — all OUTSIDE a run (no provider, no repo writes).

bundle.py — the sealed bundle format (`FORMAT="aisef-v2-project/1"`, bundle.py:37) and its loader. shape_problems(bundle.py:57) type/path-checks JSON (Windows-safe repo-confinement via PureWindowsPath, bundle.py:65-67); load(bundle.py:162) re-checks every seal, re-compiles every contract under THIS release's probe catalog (bundle.py:194-204), enforces facts==public FACT_FIELDS only (bundle.py:211-213) and stories==plan stories (bundle.py:215). Project.order(bundle.py:124) topo-sorts stories for execution. read(bundle.py:226) is the file entry.

bundle_cli.py — `aisef bundle` subcommands (main, bundle_cli.py:1080). seal(515): derive hashes/spec-ids from authoring, collect ALL problems then one raise (G7, via _refuse:485); approve(651): add one human:<name> ContractApproval, refusing the printed PLACEHOLDER (656); lint(780)/explain(804): readiness + per-criterion view; migrate(366): re-bind an earlier release's plan to this release's spec ids (_known:159, _earlier:148) and withhold approvals of contracts whose probe capability changed (_assess:219, _exposure:270, with_commands:341); continue_from(955): build a continuation bundle from a run's journal (carried stories→VERIFY, bundle_cli.py:1009-1019). _create(1066) is the single O_EXCL writer (newline="" → deterministic LF, the 2.2 Windows fix).

cli.py — `aisef run` argparse front-end (run, cli.py:60): dispatches to run/--check/--verify, maps refusals to exit 2 (cli.py:98-100).

feedback.py — developer/reviewer prompt text from PUBLIC facts + journal only: task(44), clauses(40), retry_feedback(68, H-RETRY-001), context_pack(128, V2.1-D4) with redact(138), no_progress_note(114, C3a); standing BOUNDARY block (feedback.py:28) tells the model repo content is data not instructions (G8b).

plan/ — obligation.py: PlanObligation/Plan/PlanQualityPolicy, hash-bound (plan_hash, obligation.py:99-102), EXPECTED_AT_PARENT role→parent map (76). static_admission.py: the nine §12 static checks (CHECKS, 273) + require_admitted(302); story_graph(168) induces story deps from criterion deps. story_admission.py: §13 runtime gate classify(74)+admit_story(139), routes on ContractSatisfaction never raw verdict. drift.py: §14 PRE_SATISFIED/attribution + §29 plan_quality.

**Trust boundaries.** Core boundary: the DEVELOPER agent must never see the oracle. bundle.py splits every criterion into PUBLIC facts (FACT_FIELDS = requirement/section/clause/clause_text/subject, bundle.py:38) and the private probe stimulus/observable/expectation; load() refuses any fact field outside FACT_FIELDS (bundle.py:211-213). feedback.task/retry_feedback/context_pack build prompts from facts+journal only; context_pack reads only the controller's own event records and redacts run secrets (feedback.py:138-142); BOUNDARY (feedback.py:28) is prompt hygiene, explicitly "never claimed as a guarantee".

Admission boundary (§12): engine is harness code qualified on fixtures, result is per-project and re-derived at freeze (static_admission.py docstring); reads committed objects only, invents no revision, runs no probe. Routing is on ContractSatisfaction, never a raw verdict (story_admission.py:74-101); a role/expected pair with no §13 row fails closed (static_admission check 5, :191). No developer call before story/admitted (ordering_problems:120, request_developer:132).

Approval boundary: an approval is a human statement (human:<name>) binding a contract-hash+requirement-hash; approve() refuses the placeholder (bundle_cli.py:656). migrate carries an approval forward ONLY if the contract still evaluates as it did (ARCHITECTURE-EXCEPTION-V2-007) — fail-closed: an undeclared/zero capability version, or a probe swap, selects every contract (_assess:241-245). bundle.py:185 comment pins the MIGRATE_NEVER_APPROVES shape; migrate's candidate approvals are always a subset of input (bundle_cli.py:412), never minted.

continue boundary: journal truth — a merge the journal never committed is reported and left out, never trusted; continuation proves consistency, authenticity only with --expect-anchor (bundle_cli.py:1028-1029). The run directory is operator-writable (anchor threat model), so --expect-anchor is the only authenticity check.

The tools "cannot tell who types the command" (bundle_cli.py:51): keeping the bundle + seal/approve away from the developer agent is an OPERATIONAL assumption, not enforced in code.

Known, DECLARED softness (not a regression): facts_oracle_content (bundle_cli.py:757) flags the oracle's own stimulus/observable values appearing in public facts, but it is INFORMATIONAL and never flips the lint verdict (bundle_cli.py:799 omits it) — by design (G8a), so a READY bundle can still leak oracle tokens through facts text.

**Relevant IDs:** IMPLEMENTED in this lane: G7 (seal/load/lint/admission collect every problem in one refusal — bundle.py:169/188-189, bundle_cli.py _refuse:485, seal:548/568/602/623), G8a (facts_oracle_content informational in seal+lint — bundle_cli.py:757/786), G8b (BOUNDARY block in developer prompt — feedback.py:28/65), C3a (no_progress_note — feedback.py:114), H-RETRY-001 (retry_feedback — feedback.py:68), V2.1-D4 (context_pack — feedback.py:128; docs/v2.1/CONTEXT-PACK.md), V2.1 proposal-D (aisef bundle continue — bundle_cli.py:955), ARCHITECTURE-EXCEPTION-V2-007 (aisef bundle migrate capability-aware approval carry — bundle_cli.py:54/366/RELEASES:121; owner decision 2026-10-15), RFC §11/§12/§13/§14/§29 (plan plane — plan/obligation.py, static_admission.py, story_admission.py, drift.py), STILL NEEDED (open, docs/ROADMAP-2.2-CHECKLIST.md): B4 aisef bundle draft REQ.md (line 108) — not implemented; no 'draft' subcommand in bundle_cli.main, B6a classify_or_reason sibling probe diagnostic (line 104) — absent, B6b validate-at-seal + explain shows the COMPILED assertion (subject/stimulus/expected observable/within_s) so approval is a real review (line 105) — explain() currently shows only probe/class/polarity/subject (bundle_cli.py:804-819), NOT the compiled oracle; weakens the human approval gate, B6c aisef bundle probes [--kind] / --skeleton (line 106) — absent, B6d papercuts: authoring-doc→'run seal', unapproved→name the command, not-a-git-repo vs not-exist, run --check prints client readiness (line 107) — partially present (seal:537 distinguishes not-a-repo) but not consolidated, Out of lane but gating release: REL-GOV-01 (docs/v2.3/RELEASE-GOVERNANCE-FINDING.md — pypi env has no protection rules)

**Defects:**
- **[P2]** `aisef2/app/bundle.py:124-136 (Project.order) vs aisef2/plan/static_admission.py:168-175 (story_graph); seal writes the two independently at aisef2/app/bundle_cli.py:580 and :597` — The bundle carries two independent story-ordering sources — author-declared stories[sid].depends_on (story-level) and the plan's criterion-level PlanObligation.depends_on — and nothing in seal/load/admission reconciles them. Project.order() (used verbatim for execution, run.py:674) honours only the author's story-level graph, while §12 static admission and §14 drift use the criterion-induced story_graph. load() only checks story-id set-equality (bundle.py:215) and that story deps resolve (bundle.py:100-102); grep confirms story_graph is never imported into bundle.py/bundle_cli.py.  
  *Impact:* A bundle can be sealed AND admitted while the author under-declares a story dependency that a cross-story criterion depends_on implies. order() then schedules the dependent story before the story that produces its dependency, so StoryAdmission measures it at a parent SHA missing that work. §12 check_contradictions (static_admission.py:191) only enforces ordering for PRESERVE-after-INTRODUCE on the SAME spec, so a VERIFY/INTRODUCE cross-story dependency on a different spec is silently unenforced at runtime → spurious NOT_REACHED/PRECONDITION_BROKEN or a misleading verdict, not caught at authoring time.  
  *Repro/test:* Author two stories A,B with criterion cB in B whose depends_on=[cA] (cA in A), but set stories[B].depends_on=[] and stories[A].depends_on=[]. seal+approve+lint → READY, admission PASS (criterion DAG acyclic, no PRESERVE/INTRODUCE conflict). Project.order() may emit B before A (ties by name). Add a load-time or admission check: story_graph(plan) edges must be a subset of the author's stories[].depends_on.
- **[P3]** `aisef2/app/bundle_cli.py:366-374 (migrate), reaching aisef2/app/bundle.py:69-93 (shape_problems)` — migrate() validates only data['format'] (bundle_cli.py:372-373) before calling B.shape_problems(data) (:374). Unlike load(), it omits the KEYS-completeness guard (bundle.py:166-167). shape_problems indexes top-level keys directly (data['name'] bundle.py:70, data['stories'] bundle.py:93, etc.), so a bundle with the right format but a missing top-level key raises a bare KeyError, not MigrationError/BundleError.  
  *Impact:* Breaks migrate()'s documented contract ('Refuses a bundle whose shape does not hold', bundle_cli.py:367). At the CLI the broad except (KeyError,TypeError,ValueError) at bundle_cli.py:1161 still maps it to exit 2, but any library/test caller of migrate() receives an untyped KeyError instead of the typed BundleError subclass, and the error names a Python key, not the authoring path.  
  *Repro/test:* migrate({'format':'aisef-v2-project/1'}) → KeyError('name') from shape_problems, not MigrationError. Fix: add load()'s `if sorted(data)!=sorted(B.KEYS): raise MigrationError(...)` before shape_problems (or have shape_problems use data[k] via a guarded accessor).
- **[P3]** `aisef2/app/bundle_cli.py:468-476 (_shell_word), windows branch :472-473` — On Windows, _shell_word only wraps a path in double quotes when it contains whitespace; it does not quote cmd.exe metacharacters that are legal in NTFS filenames (& ^ % ( ) ! and %VAR% expansion). The POSIX branch (:474-476) is handled via shlex.quote, but the Windows branch is not.  
  *Impact:* The approve/lint command strings printed by migrate's with_commands (bundle_cli.py:355-359) and continue_from's `next` (bundle_cli.py:1054) are advisory text the operator pastes. If the operator chose an --out path like `a&b.json` on Windows, the pasted `aisef bundle approve a&b.json ...` mis-parses or runs `b.json` as a second command. Advisory-only (no code path executes it), hence low severity.  
  *Repro/test:* _shell_word('a&b.json', windows=True) returns 'a&b.json' unquoted. Quote when any cmd metacharacter (whitespace or one of &^%()!<>|) is present.

### evidence/journal

*Files inspected (16):* `aisef2/journal/event.py`, `aisef2/journal/writer.py`, `aisef2/journal/compat.py`, `aisef2/journal/format2.py`, `aisef2/journal/format3.py`, `aisef2/journal/fold.py`, `aisef2/journal/projections/__init__.py`, `aisef2/journal/projections/story_state.py`, `aisef2/journal/projections/terminal_state.py`, `aisef2/journal/projections/budgets.py`, `aisef2/journal/projections/failure_owner.py`, `aisef2/journal/projections/retry_target.py`…

**Module map.** THE ENVELOPE & CHAIN (F1). `event.py` is the RFC §20 envelope: `Event` (frozen, seq==index, time never read by projections, event.py:52-81), content identity `event_id` = sha256(canonical envelope) (event.py:84-86), hash-chain link `chain_n = sha256(chain_{n-1} + event_id_n)` from GENESIS=0*64 (link, event.py:89-90). Format-1 payload schemas + append-site `validate` + the line codec `encode`/`decode` (event.py:195-308); `decode` re-derives the chain link and refuses a line that is not the canonical encoding of its event (event.py:288-308).

THE WRITER (§20, append-only). `writer.py` JournalWriter: public surface is append/emit/events/head/path/close, no update/delete verb; seq assigned here (`_assign`), re-validated at append, admitted on open via reconstruct; synchronous write+fsync; on OSError truncates back to pre-write size and poisons the writer (`_broken`), leaving a torn tail the next reader reports (writer.py:62-94). format2/format3 add `JournalWriter2`/`JournalWriter3` with the same discipline + a repair-only `time` param (format2.py:333-413, format3.py:337-417).

THE READERS / FORMAT COMPAT (P4, §35). `compat.py` reconstruct = the format-1 reader: decode every line, validate known types, skip unknown-ignorable (seq kept, never folded), refuse unknown-required, report torn tail; returns immutable `Journal(events, chain, skipped, torn_tail)` with `length`/`head()` (compat.py:28-72). `format2.py`/`format3.py` are format bumps through the frozen mechanism: new schemas + journal-spanning `_RULES`, `declared_format` reads journal_format from run/begin seq 0, `reconstruct` dispatches to the declaring format's own validator (format2.py:302-328, format3.py:303-332). Format 3 gives all 29 vocab types an append-site schema and carries the 9 V2-005 failure codes (format3.py:143-157).

PROJECTIONS & FOLD (F11, §21). `fold.py`: Projection protocol (id/version/initial/step, step pure over frozen state), from-scratch `fold`, incremental `Folder`, `oracle_problems` (incremental==fold-of-prefix), `CacheRow`+`_seal`+`resume` (a row is a shortcut bound to proj-id/version/length/head/state, discarded unless all four still describe the journal; never authority), and `Authority` which advances writer+projections together so an event any projection refuses never reaches the log (fold.py:91-162). `projections/` is the closed list of six control-critical read models a gate may cite; `project()` always folds from the journal, never a cache (projections/__init__.py:24-39). The six: story_state (v2 lifecycle), terminal_state (run+story terminal), budgets (v2, per-owner spend), failure_owner, retry_target, qualification_counters.

LINEAGE & ANCHOR (V2.1). `app/lineage.py`: continued-run governance decided from evidence — `claim` (exclusive CONTINUED-BY.json, child-state reconciliation BEGUN/ENDED_UNBEGUN/UNBEGUN/UNREAD), `reconcile` (journal vs git for effects not journaled), spend re-derivation `account_of`/`lineage_account`/`usage_lineage` (unread vs in-flight unknowns), and the external evidence anchor `write_anchor`/`read_anchor` (plain 64-hex head, O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW, fsync; lineage.py:830-851). The anchor is the out-of-journal binding of `head` that the chain cannot self-protect.

**Trust boundaries.** (1) WRITER-CONTROLLED DIRECTORY IS TRUSTED; the chain only proves CONSISTENCY, not AUTHENTICITY. The chain is unkeyed sha256, so an actor with write access to the run dir can rewrite any tail and recompute all later links into a self-consistent journal with a different `head`. The ONLY thing that catches a whole-tail rewrite is a `head` value held outside the journal — and that anchor (lineage.py write_anchor/read_anchor) is itself an UNSIGNED 64-hex file. event.py:12-15 states this ceiling explicitly; it is roadmap A2 (keyed chain / signed head / first-class external anchoring), not yet built.

(2) THE JOURNAL IS THE AUTHORITY; CACHES NEVER ARE. `fold.resume` serves only non-gate reads; `_seal` is a digest not a key, so a row re-sealed over a wrong state with the right head passes the seal — hence `projections.project` folds the journal and no gate reads a cache (fold.py:12-15, projections/__init__.py:3-6). Oracle (`oracle_problems`) is what catches a wrong row.

(3) VALIDATE-AT-APPEND vs FOLD-AT-READ asymmetry. On the Authority append path BOTH the format writer-validator AND every projection step run before a byte is written (fold.Authority.append, fold.py:147-158), so a bad event is refused. But `reconstruct` runs ONLY the writer-validator (compat.py:60-70, format3.py:320-331); the six projections are NOT re-run at reconstruction. A chain-consistent journal can therefore reconstruct and only fail later when a gate folds a projection. This is sound given boundary (1), but it means the writer-validator and the projection tables must stay in lockstep (see E6 defect).

(4) FORMAT ISOLATION. Each format is sealed and read by its own validator; mixed streams / second run/begin / wrong declared format are refused (the (seq==0) xor run/begin check, format3.py:275-276). A format-2 reader refuses a V2-005 failure code rather than guessing (FMT3-5).

(5) EVIDENCE vs EFFECT (lineage). A missing/torn event means the RECORD of an effect is missing, never that the effect did not happen; continuation reconciles against git + operation identities and refuses what it cannot attribute unless the operator names it (lineage.py:7-12). Unknown spend (unread evidence, in-flight requests) is listed and never shown as a known 0.

**Relevant IDs:** F1 (envelope/format1 — implemented: event.py), P3/P4 reconstruct + seq==index (implemented: compat.py, writer.py), §35 V2-005 / journal format 2 & 3 (implemented: format2.py, format3.py), F11 six control-critical projections + fold/Authority (implemented: fold.py, projections/), P-V2-02 / A2 at M4 — RUN.json-as-consistency-projection + --expect-anchor (implemented: lineage.py anchor, cli.py), A2 — keyed chain / signed head / --expect-pubkey + first-class external anchoring (NEEDED, open, QĐ#1), E1 — verdict = fold of closed projection set + oracle_problems in verify (NEEDED, open), E2 — write-ahead spend reservation through SIGKILL window (NEEDED, open), E3 — OS-observed exit/signal tier (NEEDED, open), E4 — session-stream segment digest sealed into provider/result (NEEDED, open), E5 — typed Stage pipeline with finally-always story/dispose+story/end (NEEDED, open), E6 — single shared story transition table for writer + projection (NEEDED, open, HIGH), E7 — single RunRecord/Stage enum for execute() and verify() (NEEDED, open)

**Defects:**
- **[P2]** `aisef2/journal/event.py:12` — A2 authenticity gap (by-design ceiling, roadmap-open): the hash chain is unkeyed sha256 and `head` is bound only by an UNSIGNED external anchor file (lineage.py:833 write_anchor stores plain 64-hex). An actor with write access to the run directory can rewrite the tail and recompute every later link into a self-consistent journal with a different head; nothing in the journal detects it, and the anchor that would is itself unauthenticated. The code documents this as a known ceiling; `--expect-pubkey` / signed head does not exist (grep: no expect_pubkey anywhere).  
  *Impact:* Tamper on the trusted-directory boundary is detectable only as a head mismatch against an out-of-band anchor, and the anchor is not signed. Closing it is roadmap A2 (CHỐT QĐ#1: per-run ephemeral keypair + escrowed pubkey).  
  *Repro/test:* event.py:12-15 docstring states the ceiling; lineage.py:833-851 write_anchor/read_anchor store/accept a bare 64-hex line with no MAC/signature; no `--expect-pubkey` wiring exists (only `--expect-anchor`, a plain-hex consistency check, cli.py:39 / bundle_cli.py:43).
- **[P2]** `aisef2/journal/format3.py:173` — E6 drift risk (roadmap-open, HIGH): the story lifecycle is encoded in two places that can silently diverge. The writer-validator's `_story_active` (format3.py:173-177) accepts proof/verified and tests/adequacy whenever the story merely 'has an open attempt' (began & not ended — including the un-admitted BEGIN state), while the StoryState projection restricts both to ACTIVE only (story_state.py:13 `_WITHIN`). The writer table is strictly weaker than the projection table, and only the Authority path runs both.  
  *Impact:* Because `reconstruct` runs only the writer-validator, a chain-consistent journal containing proof/verified in a non-ACTIVE attempt reconstructs cleanly and is refused only later when a gate folds StoryState — a reconstruct/fold asymmetry. As the two hand-written tables evolve independently they can diverge unnoticed. E6 (single pure state×event→next|refuse table shared by writer and projection) is unimplemented.  
  *Repro/test:* Compare format3.py:173-177 (`_story_active`: began & not story/end) + its use for PROOF_VERIFIED/TESTS_ADEQUACY in `_RULES` (format3.py:218-220) against story_state.py:13 (`_WITHIN` requires ACTIVE for both). TerminalState (terminal_state.py:10-16) is a third, separate table for the same lifecycle.
- **[P2]** `aisef2/journal/format2.py:130` — E2/E4 gap (roadmap-open): the most adopter-sensitive value — money — is not sealed into the chain. provider/result (format2.py:130-132) carries no digest of the session-stream segment it accounts for, and account_of re-derives spend from un-chained session files on disk (lineage.py:496-551). There is also no write-ahead spend reservation across the SIGKILL window: an answered-but-streamless request is 'unknown', an in-flight one is 'accepted by name'.  
  *Impact:* Spend reconciliation trusts unchained on-disk session streams + the preflight ledger; a tampered/truncated stream is caught only as 'unread/unknown', never cryptographically. E4 (segment digest in provider/result, verify per segment) and E2 (owner-typed reservation at request, reconcile at result, repair charges max(reservation,stream)) are both unimplemented.  
  *Repro/test:* format2.py:127-132 PROVIDER_RESULT/TOOL_RESULT schemas have no stream-digest field; lineage.py:538-551 derives in-flight/unknown spend by counting session *.jsonl files, not by verifying a chained digest. Roadmap: E4 (HIGH/M), E2 (MED/M), both unchecked in ĐỢT 4.

### orchestrate

*Files inspected (16):* `aisef2/orchestrate/story_runner.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/proof.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/workspace.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/adapters.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/merge.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/gate.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/review.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/security.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/quality.py`, `aisef2/runtime/story_scope.py`, `aisef2/runtime/run_scope.py`, `aisef2/control/budget.py`…

**Module map.** Area = the one post-plan execution path; no V1 consulted (seam is the only legacy door).

ENTRY POINTS:
- run_story (story_runner.py:168) — admits legacy via seam, asserts the story has an obligation (171), freezes the plan once (173/_freeze_plan:155), then loops _attempt while outcome=="RETRY" (176-180), returning StoryResult.
- _attempt (story_runner.py:183) — the straight pipeline, one journaled attempt: acquire scratch+2 checkouts (198-200) → admit_story at the frozen parent (214, no developer before admission) → continue_story (227) → no_work short-circuit when every obligation is SATISFIED at parent (233, K-NOWORK/PRESAT) → developer.implement on INTRODUCE work only (240) → move impl+verifier checkouts to candidate (261-262) → two-party proof at CANDIDATE of verify_at_candidate + affected PRESERVE of committed stories (273-283, V21-38) → engineering quality (296, skipped when no_work) → confine→review→scan→unconfine (307-320) → merge (322) → move to merged + reprove at POST_MERGE (329-348) → gate.decision (351) → COMMIT / RETRY(budget.charge→run.retry) / ROLLBACK (352-363) → STORY_DISPOSE + scope.dispose() + STORY_END (364-366).
- proof.prove (proof.py:80) — two independent ProbeRecords of one spec at one candidate (implementer checkout vs verifier checkout), each journaled; binds to Proof or one typed failure: no probe→PROBE_INVALID_SPEC (96), comparability digest mismatch→PROBE_MISMATCH (97-98), disagreement→VERIFIER_DISAGREEMENT (103-105), else route() on ContractSatisfaction by MeasurementPoint (106-111). Party.run (proof.py:65) acquires each probe's process range into the StoryScope and releases it in finally. obligations_of (114) maps cid→(spec,role).
- workspace.GitWorkspace (workspace.py:115) scratch/checkout/move/diff; GitMerger (163) base/merge(--no-ff, update-ref)/revert(reset branch to merge^1); commit_all (216) a developer capability's last step. git() (64) refuses any repo whose local/worktree config holds a non-inert key (B4) and runs every git under _ISOLATED_GIT/_NO_BACKGROUND_GIT (27-33: no hooks/fsmonitor/signing/maintenance, autocrlf off).
- Supporting typed objects: Attempt/StoryResult (99-119), _Held early-release helper (132), _EscapedRange PL-7 stand-in (39), _Sink (122), _fail (161).

STATE: all control is journal-derived — _committed (151) and attempt number (188) fold projections; gate rows (gate.py) are cited by seq; budget.charge (budget.py:34) decides retry purely from the journal prefix.

**Trust boundaries.** 1. Developer/provider is UNTRUSTED. It is called only after admission permits it and only for READY INTRODUCE work (240). Its answer is the typed Implemented (adapters.py:33): candidate must be a full SHA (post_init 45). The named candidate is MEASURED, never trusted — proof/quality/review/scan all run against it; an invalid SHA is caught at ws.move (checkout --force fails→ResourceUnavailable) or at merge (_sha verify, workspace.py:177).
2. No prose decides control (invariant VII). Every adapter returns a typed object or raises a typed refusal (ProviderOutage/CapabilityUnrunnable/ResourceUnavailable/RangeEscaped); merge outcome is typed from git exit + unmerged-path list, never stderr (workspace.py:174-198, merge.py:28 flattens any raise to UNKNOWN/INTEGRATION, never interprets).
3. Reviewer/scanner run confined: confine() (adapters.py:91) strips write bits off the story's OWN verifier checkout to build a ReadOnlyScope with no widening switch; symlinks/.git skipped (KA-09). unconfine in finally (320). A model review alone never blocks (Finding.blocks requires corroboration, adapters.py:64; D-002).
4. Deletion authority (INV-MUTATION-AUTHORITY): workspace.py removes nothing by name — a checkout is removed by git from its own worktree registry (Checkout.release 109), scratch by the TemporaryDirectory handle (Scratch.release 91); a survivor is a reported Residual, never force-rm'd. Checkout.gitfile is pinned at add and re-checked on move (145) so a developer that repoints .git is refused (B4).
5. Resource lifetime is the StoryScope stack, disposed in reverse (story_scope.py), ranks PROCESS_RANGE<SANDBOX<WORKTREE<SCRATCH so a probe range disposes before the checkouts it could write to; a range not proved empty makes every writable resource under it RESIDUAL, not released (PL-7: _EscapedRange 39, RangeEscaped handling 246-250).
6. Owner/retry boundary: a failure is charged only to its own owner's budget (budget.py, D-006); UNKNOWN and all INTEGRATION codes are not retryable (owner.py TAXONOMY).
7. Trust ASSUMPTION worth noting: _attempt's blocking-obligation selection (220) keys on decision.failure!=None while admission's `admitted` keys on disposition∈BLOCKING (story_admission.py:165); line 222 dereferences `blocking.criterion_id` and relies on the invariant that every BLOCKING disposition carries a non-None failure — true today but an implicit cross-module coupling, not an assertion.

**Relevant IDs:** E5 (STILL NEEDED, Đợt 4) — _attempt→typed stage machine + finally always emits story/dispose+story/end; roadmap marks it 'làm TRƯỚC C1/C5'; the live defect above is this item, E1 (STILL NEEDED, Đợt 4) — delivery verdict should be a fold of the closed projection set; today Attempt.outcome (story_runner.py:99,352-363) and StoryResult.committed/revision (113-119) are computed imperatively, not folded, E6 (STILL NEEDED, Đợt 4) — one pure story-state transition table shared by writer and the story_state projection read at story_runner.py:152,188,run_scope.py:178, C5 (STILL NEEDED, Đợt 5, QĐ#4) — parallel story waves by depends_on+write-scope; current path is strictly sequential (run_story per story, single journal append chain), G9 (STILL NEEDED, reference design for C5) — run-parallel/journal-sequential + closing identity check, B5 (STILL NEEDED, Đợt 5, QĐ#4) — independent acceptance gate; E5 is named its orchestration-replacement seam, WP-6.2 (IMPLEMENTED) — the single authoritative story path is this module, V21-38 (IMPLEMENTED) — affected PRESERVE of committed stories proved at candidate pre-merge (story_runner.py:266-283, merge.affected_preserve), V2-005 / ARCHITECTURE-EXCEPTION (IMPLEMENTED) — the 9 orchestration failure codes + journal format 3 (owner.py:51-71) used throughout proof/quality/review/security/merge, PL-7 / P4-LIFETIME-SEMANTICS §17.1 (IMPLEMENTED) — _EscapedRange + RangeEscaped RESIDUAL semantics (story_runner.py:39-53,246-250), B4 release charter (IMPLEMENTED) — git isolation + config inert-key allowlist (workspace.py:27-44,52-71) + gitfile pinning (105-112,145), B6/B7 (IMPLEMENTED) — git clean -ffdx between revisions (workspace.py:151); revert only when merged!=candidate (story_runner.py:334,347), C2-ORCHESTRATION-CONFORMANCE-REPAIR (IMPLEMENTED) — per-spec probe identity via _probe/by_spec (story_runner.py:373-386, proof.py:54-78), INV-MUTATION-AUTHORITY (IMPLEMENTED) — no path removed by name in workspace.py (git/TemporaryDirectory own deletion), D-002 (IMPLEMENTED) — a model review alone never blocks (review.py:43-49, adapters.Finding.blocks)

**Defects:**
- **[P1]** `aisef2/orchestrate/story_runner.py:197-366 (body 204-350 unguarded; dispose/end 364-366 outside any finally)` — The _attempt pipeline and its terminal STORY_DISPOSE + scope.dispose() + STORY_END block run outside any try/finally. Only a handful of typed exceptions are caught per-stage; any other exception a stage raises propagates out of _attempt/run_story, skipping scope.dispose() and the story-close events. Concrete raisers: InvariantError from run_probe/bound_result inside prove()/reprove() (probe.protocol.py:264,223 — e.g. a record that does not bind), an OSError from scanner.read() when the tool crashed before writing its report (security.py:48), a file error inside quality.assess() measure/evaluate, or a Residual/InvariantError from merger.revert() (story_runner.py:335,348).  
  *Impact:* A single story's unexpected fault escalates to the whole run: the StoryScope is not disposed here (worktrees/scratch leak unless the run-level interrupt later disposes them), STORY_DISPOSE/STORY_END are never journaled so the story stays logically open, and a clean shutdown() is then refused (run_scope.py:184-187 ShutdownRefused) — the run can only end via interrupt(), leaving the sentinel OPEN so the next preflight reports TORN. Fault isolation (the stage order encodes invariant IX) is defeated. This is exactly the hole roadmap E5 is scoped to close; E5 is still OPEN (Đợt 4, unchecked) so the defect ships in the v2.3 W0 baseline.  
  *Repro/test:* Drive run_story with a Scanner whose read() raises FileNotFoundError (tool exited before writing its report) → _attempt raises → run_scope.open_stories() still lists the story as not ENDED → RunScope.shutdown() raises ShutdownRefused. A finally wrapping the pipeline that always emits STORY_DISPOSE+scope.dispose()+STORY_END (classifying the escape as UNKNOWN/INTEGRATION) is the E5 fix.
- **[P3]** `aisef2/orchestrate/story_runner.py:163-164 (with aisef2/orchestrate/merge.py:29)` — For a FailureCode.UNKNOWN failure, _fail sets data['original'] = detail.rsplit(' ',1)[-1], commented 'the flattened error's type name'. But the only UNKNOWN that reaches _fail is produced by merge() (merge.py:28-35), which discards the flattened original (`code, _original = flatten(e)`, _original unused) and _attempt builds detail as f'merge: {m.failure.code.value}' (story_runner.py:325), whose last token is the literal 'UNKNOWN'. Every other detail= assignment in _attempt ends in a code.value/disposition/message, never a bare type name, so the extraction never recovers a real type.  
  *Impact:* §22 provenance ('keep the original as data') is defeated on the merge path: the FAILURE_OBSERVED.original diagnostic field is always the useless string 'UNKNOWN' instead of the real exception type (which survives only in the gate/check detail at merge.py:33). Diagnostics only — ownership/retryability are correct (UNKNOWN→INTEGRATION, not retryable).  
  *Repro/test:* Make merger.merge() raise ValueError('x') → FAILURE_OBSERVED event carries original='UNKNOWN', not 'ValueError'. Fix: thread merge.py's _original (type name) through Merged into detail, or pass original explicitly into _fail rather than re-parsing detail.

### product/probe

*Files inspected (18):* `aisef2/product/__init__.py`, `aisef2/product/contract.py`, `aisef2/product/spec.py`, `aisef2/product/compiler.py`, `aisef2/product/approval.py`, `aisef2/product/outcome.py`, `aisef2/probe/catalog.py`, `aisef2/probe/protocol.py`, `aisef2/probe/calibration.py`, `aisef2/probe/process_effect.py`, `aisef2/probe/cli_invocation.py`, `aisef2/probe/file_artifact.py`…

**Module map.** PRODUCT PLANE (aisef2/product, RFC §6–§10, content-addressed identities F2/F4/F9):
- contract.py:98 Subject / :112 BehaviorContract.create (:152) — the F4 behaviour contract; contract_hash binds every field incl. subject_absence+rationale (:179). names_test_artefact (:87) is the invariant-IX gate: a subject/stimulus/observable string naming a test artefact is rejected at construction (:108, :141). canonical/digest helpers (:60-65) give the shared content identity.
- approval.py:49 ContractApproval / :82 require_approved — §6 requirement authority; approver must be `human:<name>` (:61); binding_problems (:73) checks approval binds both current hashes.
- spec.py:28 ProductProofSpec.create (:56) — F4 spec; semantic_hash (:68) = probe id+digest+probe_input+expectation (excludes prose/ids); id = PPS-digest (:74).
- compiler.py:59 compile_spec — pure f(approved contract); require_approved first (:61), one ProbeRef per SubjectKind (:62), expectation_for polarity (:51). COMPILER_DIGEST = content hash of the 4 product sources (:29-37).
- outcome.py:36 Executed/:53 Unrunnable/:60 InvalidSpec (two-axis result); contract_satisfaction (:71) derived, never stored; on_subject_absent (:82) the only absent-subject result (§10.2).
PROBE PLANE (aisef2/probe):
- protocol.py — F5 Probe Protocol (:133), HarnessProbe base (:145), Observation (:93)/ObservationKind (:82), classify_failure (:164) the sole Observation→ProbeResult map, run_probe (:264) binds identity+enforcement+revision into a sealed ProbeRecord (:188), bound_result (:223) the only read path (PROBE-BIND), ProbeRegistry (:246) harness-owned metadata (PROBE-META-1), ProbeInterrupted (:122) §9.3.
- catalog.py:102 CATALOG / :48 CatalogEntry — B6c harness-owned registry; one active probe per SubjectKind (problems_of :125), DESIGN-CHECK-1 child_of_harness≠captured_stdout (:88); registry()/active()/catalogue() build the admission maps; nothing in control imports it (verified: no hits in aisef2/control, aisef2/orchestrate).
- calibration.py:104 calibrate — §9.1 CONTRAST calibration: positive fixture must observe SATISFIED, negative REFUTED (demonstrates_contrast :77); ProbeCapabilityCalibration binds EXECUTION_DIGEST (executing :68); SpecFalsifiabilityEvidence (:51) is Q2-only.
- Four probes, each = observation_class/spec_class (the subject-independent oracle) + verdict_of + a Probe subclass + _watch/_concluded: file_artifact.py:815 (FULL enforcement, git object store, no subject process, grep in a worker), cli_invocation.py:1108 (PARTIAL, subject in ci.AGENT child, equality DECISION-4), python_callable_v2.py:428 (PARTIAL, active python_callable probe, DECISION-6 supersede), process_effect.py:820 (PARTIAL, scenario/step vocabulary STEPS :136 + closed FAULTS :148). cli_invocation exports the shared watch primitives (_await :1092, _protocol :1072, _harness_failure :1388, _silent :1326, AGENT); python_callable exports _after_dispatch (:379, §9.3 ledger guard) reused by all subject-running probes. Probe identity split (V2-007): DIGEST=capability (:280 etc.), EXECUTION_DIGEST=hashed source bytes (_probe_digest).

**Trust boundaries.** Core trust model = HARNESS/CONTROLLER (trusted) vs SUBJECT (untrusted candidate code), RFC B1. (1) The probe process is a controller that never imports/constructs/calls the subject; the subject runs in a separate process (cli_invocation.AGENT) started before DISPATCHED. (2) Protocol authenticity: every protocol line on the marker file is HMAC-SHA256'd under a per-evaluation key the subject never sees (the key is cleared from request.json before the subject exists); _protocol (cli_invocation.py:1072) accepts only lines carrying the mark+nonce+valid MAC, so a forged protocol line the subject writes is captured bytes, not a verdict. (3) Oracle independence: the expected verdict is fixed before looking — candidate_expectation from polarity at compile time (compiler.py:51), and observation_class maps observable+stimulus→class without reading subject output; calibration (calibrate) requires the probe to demonstrate BOTH verdicts (CONTRAST) against committed fixtures, so a probe that always returns one verdict is NotQualified. (4) Invariant IX (no developer artefacts): names_test_artefact gates the contract (contract.py:108/141) and again spec.probe_input in run_probe (protocol.py:273); locators are module:name/path: forms, never developer paths/commands. (5) Enforcement honesty: run_probe refuses (UNRUNNABLE) rather than degrades when the probe's level < required (protocol.py:279); PARTIAL vs FULL records are not comparable (comparability :217). (6) Signal authority §9.3: only the controller's own ProcessRange.ledger decides an interruption (_after_dispatch python_callable.py:379); a non-controller signal is INDETERMINATE, never a verdict. (7) Isolation (not sandbox): -I, scrubbed env, fresh cwd/TMPDIR/pycache per evaluation, evaluation dir refused inside the checkout — PARTIAL probes still trust the subject's self-reports about itself (WEAKEST_PATH). file_artifact is the only FULL probe (runs no subject code; reads git's object store at the SHA). ASSUMPTION worth noting: the per-evaluation window/deadline must be measured by the controller, not inferred from the range merely being alive (B1-BLOCKS-STOP-001) — see defect.

**Relevant IDs:** F2, F4, F5, F9, RFC-§6, RFC-§7, RFC-§8, RFC-§9, RFC-§9.1, RFC-§9.2, RFC-§9.3, RFC-§10, RFC-§10.1, RFC-§10.2, RFC-§24, RFC-§35, PROBE-META-1, PROBE-BIND-1, PROBE-BIND-2, PROBE-CATALOG-CLOSURE, DESIGN-CHECK-1, B1, B6, B6c, B1-BLOCKS-STOP-001, IR-01, ARCHITECTURE-EXCEPTION-V2-002, ARCHITECTURE-EXCEPTION-V2-003, ARCHITECTURE-EXCEPTION-V2-007, C2-P2-FINDING-001, C2-P2-FINDING-002, C2-P2-FINDING-003, DECISION-1, DECISION-2, DECISION-4, DECISION-6, WP-2.0.1, WP-2.1.1, WP-2.2.1, WP-2.2.2, WP-2.3.1, WP-2.4.1, PM-5, PM-7, PM-9, PM-11, PM-13, V21-37, NEEDS: http_route probe (SubjectKind.HTTP_ROUTE declared at arch/enums.py:27 but no catalog entry — compile_spec fail-closes with CompileError for any http_route contract)

**Defects:**
- **[P2]** `aisef2/probe/process_effect.py:907` — process_effect._watch concludes SUBJECT_DEADLINE/REFUTED from `run.members()` being non-empty, but the HARNESS controller is itself a permanent range member and the RESULT await carries no watchdog grace — so a controller that lingers/starves at the window boundary (or a RESULT landing in the final-poll gap) is misreported as a subject deadline. This is the B1-BLOCKS-STOP-001 failure the two sibling subject-running probes explicitly guard against.  
  *Impact:* False REFUTED on product evidence: a scenario that actually completed (or whose delay was the controller/host, not the subject) is recorded as an expired-window REFUTED → a MUST_HOLD contract spuriously UNSATISFIED. Systematic: every process_effect observation lacks the guard.  
  *Repro/test:* Compare python_callable_v2.py:528/531 and cli_invocation.py:1301/1304: both await RESULT for `window + env.timeout_s` and, on TIMEOUT with members present, return `_silent()` → HARNESS_FAILED (because the controller arms its own window Timer and writes `deadline: true`). process_effect has no controller-side timer (grep: no "window" key passed to its HARNESS, no Timer/settle/conclude), awaits only `time.monotonic() + window` (:907), and on TIMEOUT-with-members returns SUBJECT_DEADLINE REFUTED (:909-913) WITHOUT re-reading the marker file for a RESULT that just landed. tests/v2/test_probe_process_effect_units.py:542 (`Running.members()->[object()]`) bakes in this behavior; a `Running` fake with RESULT already in the protocol file would still yield SUBJECT_DEADLINE because members() is checked before the RESULT re-read. Fix: give the controller its own deadline Timer (as the siblings do) or, at TIMEOUT, re-read the marker file for RESULT and treat members-present-with-no-RESULT as HARNESS_FAILED (_silent), not a subject deadline.

### invariants

*Files inspected (12):* `aisef2/invariants/registry.py`, `aisef2/invariants/evidence.py`, `aisef2/invariants/__init__.py`, `aisef2/errors.py`, `aisef2/arch/enums.py`, `validation/v2/kernel_static_checks.py`, `tests/v2/__init__.py`, `aisef2/app/run.py`, `aisef2/app/verify.py`, `validation/v2/mutation.py`, `aisef2/journal/event.py`, `docs/IMPROVEMENT-ROADMAP.md`

**Module map.** registry.py is the lane's core: RFC §4's nine invariants I–IX as a typed `REGISTRY` tuple (registry.py:93-189), each `Invariant` naming `Mechanism`s of three kinds — CODE `package.module:qualname`, STATIC_RULE `kernel_static_checks:RULE`, CHECKER `validation/v2/<file>.py:function` (MechanismKind 62-66). Entry points: (1) `register()` registry.py:304 — fail-closed full validation (exactly I..IX in order, frozen titles from arch.enums.INVARIANT_TITLE, every invariant has mechanisms + declared for all 9 tiers, every mechanism resolves via `resolve()` 212, InvariantError is uncontainable, every tier `__init__` calls `arm()` via AST check `_arms()` 253, no stray test dir, rendered doc matches `render()` 474). (2) `arm(tier)` 356 — the dev/test arming: each `tests/v2/*/__init__.py` calls `arm(Tier.X)` at import so all nine are armed before the tier's first test module (verified: 9/9 tier inits call arm). (3) `require_armed(inv)` 370 — the runtime guard's first act; fails closed in an unarmed process; passes if runtime-armed (`_RUNTIME_ARMED`) OR tier-armed (`_ARMED`). (4) Runtime half added by ROADMAP-2.2 A1 (be7b1700): `register_runtime()` 425 / `arm_runtime()` 462 run only the pure checks + resolve CODE mechanisms (installed wheel has no tests/validation/docs — RUNTIME_STATEMENT 384); `runtime_mechanism_digest()` 406 digests imported CODE module bytes (CRLF-normalized) so a checkout and its wheel digest identically. Wired in production: aisef2/app/run.py:516 `invariants.arm_runtime()` is the first stage of `execute()` try-block (binds registry_digest + code_mechanism_digest into RUN.json rec["invariants"] 517-520); aisef2/app/verify.py:102-128 `_invariant_problems` re-derives and compares on `--verify` (shape-checks 64-hex unconditionally, compares values only when kernel_digest matches — guards the B1 self-referential-mutation weakness). Hot guards calling require_armed: journal/fold.py:148 (VI), runtime/run_scope.py:112 (II), invariants/evidence.py:49 (VIII). evidence.py is invariant VIII ("Memory Is Context, Never Evidence"): `admit(value)` 47 accepts only the closed set `harness_records()` 31 (Event, ProbeRecord, Executed, …, Charge) and refuses Context/str/mapping/look-alike; `EvidenceField` 60 admits on construction. No-Prose-As-Control-State is invariant VII (enums.py:184) + the STATIC_RULE `NO_PROSE_CONTROL` (kernel_static_checks.py:167-176) referenced by both I.no-prose-control and VII.no-prose-control: an AST scan over all of aisef2/ flagging any attribute/getattr/hasattr named text|rationale|ownership_rationale (PROSE set, kernel_static_checks.py:55).
