Baseline verified green: register(), register_runtime(), arm_runtime() all succeed; registry_digest a5ef12d7 identical across register()/register_runtime() (the cross-context comparable identity).

**Trust boundaries.** Evidence-origin boundary (VIII): only harness-produced typed records are evidence; anything an agent/model read (Context) is refused by admit() with an uncontainable InvariantError. Enforcement-vs-claim boundary (RUNTIME_STATEMENT, registry.py:384): at runtime only CODE mechanisms are re-resolved and digested from the installed kernel; STATIC_RULE/CHECKER/tier/rendered-doc checks are the development tier's and are NOT re-run against a wheel — so "nine armed in this run" means code mechanisms resolved+unmodified, not that static/checker gates ran. Fail-closed is the posture everywhere: register*/arm* raise RegistrationError (a subclass of InvariantError, which derives from BaseException not Exception, errors.py:8 — no `except Exception` can swallow it; audited by validation/v2/except_boundaries.py). Uncontainability is itself a registered mechanism (UNCONTAINABLE, registry.py:192). Who-trusts-whom: runtime control reads ONLY the typed identifiers (InvariantId, Mechanism, digests); the rendered doc docs/implementation/v2/INVARIANTS-V2.md is generated FROM the registry and checked against it, never read for control (registry.py:15-16). Digest identity of a CODE mechanism = module-file bytes only, not qualname (registry.py:284-296), so multiple mechanisms in one module share a file hash by design. Arming state is process-global mutable (_ARMED 353, _RUNTIME_ARMED 459): trust is per-process, established at tier import or at execute()'s first stage, and leaks across tests sharing an interpreter.

**Relevant IDs:** A1 (ROADMAP-2.2, DONE be7b1700 — runtime arming in execute(), register_runtime CODE-only, bind registry_digest+code_mechanism_digest into RUN.json, --verify re-derive, require_armed hot guards), Lane M (ROADMAP-2.2 owner item 1, OPEN — register v22/invariants kill tests into mutation TARGETS + re-measure/classify/seal; require_armed P5 reseal), B3 (finally still writes the record when arm_runtime's uncontainable failure fires — run.py:514 comment), WP-5.5 / F10 (the nine-invariant typed registry + VIII evidence boundary), WP-1.1 (NO_PROSE_CONTROL static rule — invariant I/VII), V2-003 (ONE_SIGNAL_AUTHORITY static rule), Invariants I–IX (RFC §4); VII = 'No Prose As Control State'; VIII = 'Memory Is Context, Never Evidence', G8 (ROADMAP-2.2, DONE — no-oracle-words lint + prompt boundary; adjacent, declared hygiene NOT an invariant), C4 (ROADMAP-2.2, REVERTED — touched invariant IX stage ordering / ALL_CONTROL_DECISIONS_JOURNAL_BACKED; owner forbade changing the invariant), B1 (mutation self-referential-comparison weakness that verify.py:107 explicitly guards for the invariant digests)

**Defects:**
- **[P2]** `aisef2/invariants/evidence.py:47 (and 60, 31)` — Invariant VIII's admission boundary (admit/EvidenceField/Context/harness_records) has ZERO production callers. Grep of aisef2/ finds it referenced only by the registry mechanisms (registry.py:172,174) and one test (tests/v2/p5/test_invariants.py); the .admit( hits in app/run.py, cli.py, bundle_cli.py, plan/static_admission.py are a different StaticPlanAdmissionEngine.admit/calibrations admit, not this one. No kernel evidence-carrying field is an EvidenceField.  
  *Impact:* VIII is attested only by its own unit tests; the 'memory is context, never evidence' boundary gates no real production path. register() still passes because the mechanism merely resolves. This is the residual of IMPROVEMENT-ROADMAP §23 (the invariants package was dead at the production layer); A1 wired the arming but VIII's admission remains unwired — arming guarantees the code is present+unmodified, not that any evidence actually flows through admit().  
  *Repro/test:* grep -rn 'EvidenceField\|invariants.evidence' aisef2/ → only registry.py:172,174; no construction site. Add a production evidence field that routes through admit(), or document VIII as test-tier-only.
- **[P3]** `validation/v2/mutation.py:1275-1277` — require_armed's mutation record is known-stale: its P5 kill-set predates the wave-1 _RUNTIME_ARMED early-return branch (registry.py:373-374). The source comment states this explicitly ('wave 1 CHANGED it ... which makes its P5 record stale — a P5 re-measure/reseal item for the owner'). Lane M (checklist:97) is still open [ ].  
  *Impact:* Mutants of the runtime-armed branch of require_armed — the guard that lets installed-kernel hot paths (fold VI, run_scope II, evidence VIII) proceed — have no current mutation attestation, so a weakening mutant there could survive unmeasured until the owner re-measures/reseals P5.  
  *Repro/test:* Re-run mutation for aisef2/invariants/registry.py::require_armed with tests/v2/p0/test_invariants_runtime.py in the kill set and reseal the P5 record (owner gate item, Lane M).
- **[P3]** `aisef2/invariants/registry.py:299 and 284` — mechanism_digest (299) and _identity (284) are named in NO mutation TARGETS entry. registry.py targets cover {register, arm, resolve, _load_script, require_armed, _arms, test_directories} (mutation.py:316-318, P5) and {registry_digest, runtime_mechanism_digest, register_runtime, arm_runtime} (mutation.py:1278-1279, V2.1), but not mechanism_digest / _identity.  
  *Impact:* The dev-tier mechanism-identity digest (the sha256 that would detect an enforcement-mechanism file drifting) has no mutation attestation; a mutant that weakens how mechanism identity is computed could survive. Lower blast radius than the runtime digest (dev tier only), but it is the file-integrity half of 'mechanisms present and not modified'.  
  *Repro/test:* Add aisef2/invariants/registry.py::mechanism_digest and ::_identity to P5_TARGETS with _INV_TESTS and measure.
- **[P3]** `aisef2/invariants/registry.py:353,459` — Arming state is module-global mutable (_ARMED, _RUNTIME_ARMED) with no per-test reset; a test that arms the runtime leaks into later tests expecting an unarmed process. Already a documented bisect cause (ROADMAP-2.2-CHECKLIST.md:155: '2 test p5 cần clear cả _RUNTIME_ARMED').  
  *Impact:* Test-isolation fragility, not a product bug: require_armed/arm_runtime idempotence is correct, but any process-global arming is sticky across the shared pytest interpreter, so new tests asserting fail-closed behaviour must manually clear _RUNTIME_ARMED or get a false pass. A future unarmed-path test added without the clear would silently not exercise the refusal.  
  *Repro/test:* Run a p5 test that arms runtime before a test asserting require_armed raises in an unarmed process, in one interpreter, without resetting _RUNTIME_ARMED → the second test's refusal never fires.

### aisef2/control/*

*Files inspected (19):* `aisef2/control/__init__.py`, `aisef2/control/owner.py`, `aisef2/control/routing.py`, `aisef2/control/budget.py`, `aisef2/arch/enums.py`, `aisef2/product/outcome.py`, `aisef2/plan/story_admission.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/review.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/story_runner.py`, `aisef2/runtime/run_scope.py`, `aisef2/journal/event.py`, `aisef2/journal/projections/retry_target.py`…

**Module map.** The control plane decides WHO owns a failure, WHETHER it retries, and WHICH budget a retry charges — three modules, each a pure function over typed inputs, no I/O.

owner.py (the taxonomy, RFC §22, F3): `FailureCode` (22 codes: 13 base + 9 V2-005), `Retryability{RETRYABLE,NOT_RETRYABLE,RESOLVED_BY_POLICY}`, `Classification(code,owner,retryability,rule)` with `.budget` property (owner.py:81-84 → None iff NOT_RETRYABLE). `TAXONOMY` (owner.py:88-160) maps every code once to exactly one `Owner`+`Retryability`. Entry points: `classify(code)` (owner.py:163-167, fail-closed on untyped) is the single read site for owner+retryability; `flatten(value)` (owner.py:170-172) maps any non-FailureCode to UNKNOWN keeping the original as data (§22, Invariant VII). `FORMAT_2_CODES`/`V2_005_CODES` frozensets gate which codes each journal format admits.

routing.py (the owner routing table, RFC §10.3, F2, amended V2-001): `route(result,spec,point,role)` (routing.py:96-104) and `row_for(...)` (routing.py:107-130) key an owner decision on (MeasurementPoint, ContractSatisfaction, IndeterminateReason, ObligationRole) via `_EXECUTED` (routing.py:67-93). Derived satisfaction only (`contract_satisfaction`), never raw BehaviorVerdict. `UnroutableOutcome(InvariantError)` (routing.py:40-41) fails closed on any untyped/undeclared tuple. `Route(failure,decided_by,disposition,rule)`. At PARENT returns a StoryAdmission disposition (consumed by plan/story_admission.py:80-91); at CANDIDATE/POST_MERGE returns an owner Classification.

budget.py (retry budgets as journal projections, RFC §17/§22, WP-4.5): `charge(events,story,limits)` (budget.py:34-50) recomputes — never counts — the retry decision from the journal: folds RETRY_TARGET for the budget+failure_seq, folds BUDGETS["retries"] for spent, compares to the policy limit; a failure charges only its own owner's budget. `developer_spend(...)` (budget.py:53-55) reads per-criterion developer calls. `Charge` dataclass carries the full rationale.

Consumers: plan/story_admission.py (row_for at PARENT), orchestrate/{story_runner,merge,proof,review,security,quality}.py (classify/route/flatten), runtime/run_scope.py:160 + orchestrate/story_runner.py:357 (budget.charge), journal/event.py:235 (writes failure/observed owner+retryable from the taxonomy).

**Trust boundaries.** Who-trusts-whom and the enforced assumptions:

1. Single source of ownership (Invariant IV). classify() (owner.py:163) is the ONLY place owner+retryability are read; journal/event.py:228-235 REFUSES a failure/observed whose call site supplies owner/retryable and re-derives them from TAXONOMY (event.py:187-189 validates stored==taxonomy). Static check RETRYABLE_ONLY_IN_TAXONOMY (kernel_static_checks.py:181-193) forbids any module but owner.py from deciding retryability. Boundary holds.

2. No prose/raw-verdict as control (Invariant VII, II). routing routes on derived ContractSatisfaction, never BehaviorVerdict; static check NO_RAW_VERDICT_ROUTING (kernel_static_checks.py:177-180) enforces it across aisef2/control + aisef2/plan. flatten() quarantines foreign errors to UNKNOWN→INTEGRATION, never DEVELOPER (owner.py:129-131).

3. Fail-closed routing. Any untyped point/role/result or undeclared (point,satisfaction,reason,role) tuple raises UnroutableOutcome (routing.py:112-128); the table never defaults to DEVELOPER. A PARENT PRECONDITION_ABSENT with no role, or an INDETERMINATE reason with no row, is refused by design.

4. Budget isolation (D-006). A retry charges the failure's OWN owner budget only (budget.py:39-50); a credential rejection (ENVIRONMENT) can never reach DEVELOPER. Budgets are journal projections, not side counters — static check NO_SIDE_RETRY_COUNTER (kernel_static_checks.py:213) enforces it.

5. Reviewer != developer (C6). The TYPED separation exists and is correct: REVIEW/SECURITY are distinct Owners (enums.py:122-123), REVIEW_FINDING→REVIEW retryable→REVIEW budget (owner.py:148-150), the budgets projection charges review/security maps never the developer map (budgets.py:16,56-62), and a model review alone cannot block — Finding.blocks requires corroboration (adapters.py:64-65, used at review.py:43-49, D-002). BUT the runtime independence C6 actually asks for — reviewer served by a SECOND model/route so the model does not review itself — is NOT in control/*; it is a settings/orchestrate concern still OPEN (see relevant_ids). control/* is "ready for C6," not "C6 done."

Assumption that must hold upstream of budget.charge: `limits` is keyed by Owner enum (settings.py:105,235 guarantee it; budget.py:44 does Owner(budget)); a partial/empty limits map degrades safely to no-retry.

**Relevant IDs:** RFC §22 (typed failure taxonomy) — owner.py implements, RFC §10.3 / §10.1 (owner routing on measurement point + ContractSatisfaction) — routing.py implements, RFC §13 (StoryAdmission dispositions at PARENT) — routing._EXECUTED consumed by plan/story_admission.py, RFC §17 / WP-4.5 (retry budgets as projections) — budget.py implements, RFC §25 (reviewer/security findings) — REVIEW/SECURITY owners + REVIEW_FINDING/SECURITY_FINDING codes, RFC §26 (post-merge / merge conflict) — POST_MERGE_* + MERGE_CONFLICT codes, Invariant IV Typed Ownership — classify() single source, enforced RETRYABLE_ONLY_IN_TAXONOMY, Invariant VII No Prose As Control State — flatten()/NO_RAW_VERDICT_ROUTING, F2 (ProbeExecutionStatus/ContractSatisfaction/MeasurementPoint), F3 (Owner, capped), F6 (ObligationRole), F7 (StoryAdmissionDisposition), F11 (ControlProjection) — frozen vocabularies owner.py/routing.py/budget.py depend on, ARCHITECTURE-EXCEPTION-V2-001 (measurement-point-aware owner) — implemented in routing table + SUBJECT_ABSENT_AT_CANDIDATE/POST_MERGE_SUBJECT_LOST, ARCHITECTURE-EXCEPTION-V2-003 (NON_CONTROLLER_SIGNAL -> INTEGRATION, never retried) — owner.py:113-119, routing.py:79/85/91, ARCHITECTURE-EXCEPTION-V2-005 (9 orchestration codes, journal format 3) — owner.py:51-60,69-71,132-159, D-002 (model review alone never sole blocking authority) — enforced at adapters.Finding.blocks, not in control/* but gates REVIEW_FINDING, D-006 (invalid credential never retryable; credential failure never charges DEVELOPER) — owner.py:123-125 + budget isolation, Roadmap C6 (ROADMAP-2.2-CHECKLIST.md:139, IMPROVEMENT-ROADMAP.md:84) — STILL NEEDED: reviewer on a second route/model with independent attest + separate ledger line; control/* provides the typed REVIEW owner+budget but the runtime route independence is unimplemented and OPEN, Roadmap C4 (early no-op route: candidate==base -> route straight to DEVELOPER at CANDIDATE) — NEEDED; would call routing at CANDIDATE on a short-circuit path not yet present, Roadmap C9 (cost-aware repair/retry loop: fold stuck-retry spend into the money cap) — NEEDED; builds on budget.charge/developer_spend, v2.3 directive (docs/v2.3/BASELINE.md:62): no requalification of frozen evidence; control/* audited as-is on release/v2.3-big, no edits

**Defects:**
- **[P3]** `aisef2/control/owner.py:84` — The three-valued Retryability (RETRYABLE/NOT_RETRYABLE/RESOLVED_BY_POLICY) collapses to binary downstream: Classification.budget returns self.owner for both RETRYABLE and RESOLVED_BY_POLICY, so MISSING_CREDENTIAL is indistinguishable from a plainly-retryable code once it reaches the journal and budget.charge. The 'policy decides whether to retry' semantics advertised for RESOLVED_BY_POLICY (owner.py:120-122) is never honored as a distinct branch — it only manifests implicitly as the ENVIRONMENT limit count.  
  *Impact:* Not a wrong result today: with settings limits present (settings.py:234 requires every owner), a missing credential retries exactly like CONTRACT_UNSATISFIED up to the ENVIRONMENT count — arguably the intended effect. Risk is latent/maintenance: no kernel code branches on retryability is RESOLVED_BY_POLICY, so a future change that reads the enum expecting a real policy hook would be a silent no-op, and a reviewer reading the enum will believe credentials get special retry handling they do not. The distinction is purely documentary.  
  *Repro/test:* python3 -c "from aisef2.control.owner import classify,FailureCode as F; print(classify(F.MISSING_CREDENTIAL).budget, classify(F.CONTRACT_UNSATISFIED).budget)" -> both non-None (Owner.ENVIRONMENT / Owner.DEVELOPER); journal retryable = (budget is not None) = True for both (event.py:235). RESOLVED_BY_POLICY path taken by classify is equal to RETRYABLE at every downstream site.

### adapters/grade

*Files inspected (9):* `aisef2/app/adapters.py`, `aisef2/app/review_verify.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/adapters.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/review.py`, `aisef2/app/client.py`, `aisef2/app/bundle.py`, `docs/ROADMAP-2.2-CHECKLIST.md`, `docs/IMPROVEMENT-ROADMAP.md`, `docs/v2.3/BASELINE.md`

**Module map.** This lane is the kernel's develop→review→grade stage. Three adapters implement the orchestrate protocols (aisef2/orchestrate/adapters.py).

DEVELOPER — Developer.implement (adapters.py:76): builds the task prompt (feedback.task), on a prior failure appends retry feedback + context_pack (D4) + no_progress note (C3a, adapters.py:83-85), runs a client.session in the story checkout, then decides "changed" from `git status --porcelain` OR HEAD moved (adapters.py:91-93) and commits via commit_all (adapters.py:98) → Implemented(COMPLETED, full-sha). No change → FAILED; error+no-change → ProviderOutage.

REVIEWER / GRADE GATE — Reviewer.review (adapters.py:140) is the verified-review core: capture candidate HEAD + whole file tree (tree_of, adapters.py:310) BEFORE the finder; run finder = _model (opencode session, adapters.py:178) or _ocr (Open Code Review, adapters.py:198); bound each finding (review_verify.bounded:132); re-assert the checkout is unchanged after the finder AND again after verification (_unchanged:330). Per finding: _verify (adapters.py:301 → review_verify.verify:184) runs the reproducer with the calibrated probe of the subject kind → Verification{CONFIRMED|NOT_REPRODUCED|INDETERMINATE|UNVERIFIABLE}; review_verify.anchor:292 decides whether the expected value is the cited clause's approved-contract outcome; review_verify.blocks:332 = fix-loop ∧ CONFIRMED ∧ ANCHORED ∧ severity≥floor; review_verify.preserve:497 writes the full redacted finding to review-findings/<story>.<attempt>.<index>.json and review_verify.detail:423 writes the bounded (SUMMARY_MAX=1500) journal statement; emits Finding(fid, confirmed|blocking, (evidence,) iff confirmed, detail) (adapters.py:169).

SCANNER — RuffScanner/NoScanner/scanner() (adapters.py:372-404): ruff --exit-zero informational; never blocks.

VERIFY-TIME AUDIT — review_verify.artifact_problems:529 re-binds each journal review row to its artifact bytes for `aisef run --verify`.

Gate wiring (caller): orchestrate/review.py:43-49 turns each Finding into one gate/check, failing the story (REVIEW_FINDING, owner REVIEW) only when f.blocks; f.blocking alone yields the advisory D-002 message (review.py:45).

**Trust boundaries.** Finder output (model reviewer or OCR) is DATA, never control — "no prose it produces is ever read for control" (orchestrate/adapters.py:3, invariant VII). The acceptance decision rests on two independent kernel authorities, not the reviewer's word: (1) the kernel's own calibrated probe must REFUTE the stated behaviour (CONFIRMED), and (2) the bundle's APPROVED contracts must anchor the expectation (ANCHORED). Finding.blocks = blocking ∧ corroborated_by (orchestrate/adapters.py:65); adapters.py:169 fills corroborated_by only when confirmed, so a model finding with blocking:true but unconfirmed can never block (D-002/RFC §25). This is the core of B5 and it is sound.

Prompt-injection boundary: REVIEW_PROMPT/OCR_BACKGROUND tell the model repo/diff text is evidence, not instructions (adapters.py:39-40, 50-51). G1 leak-tripwire scans everything served into the OCR session — rule.json + background.md (adapters.py:238-242) — and client.session scans prompt+env; refusal never echoes the stimulus value.

Checkout-immutability boundary: candidate HEAD + full tree hashed before the finder and re-checked after the finder and after verification (_unchanged:330-338); ANY HEAD or tree change discards EVERY finding (CapabilityUnrunnable) — a review that didn't run on the frozen candidate is void. Fails closed (an unreadable file after → mismatch → discard).

Secret boundary: run secrets + OCR route token redact session streams, the findings put in RUN.json (adapters.py:172), journal statements, and the preserved artifacts (review_verify.redacted:370, depth-capped at DEPTH_MAX=64 so the walk can't hit the recursion limit).

Evidence-preservation boundary: a CONFIRMED/blocking finding whose full artifact cannot be kept REFUSES the whole review (adapters.py:167-168, NOT_PRESERVED) — a confirmed finding is never passed silently, and a block never rests on the bounded (cut) statement, only on the byte-bound artifact.

Binary-identity boundary (weak/declared): OCR exe is pinned by sha256 re-checked at moment of use (adapters.py:203-206) but its version is self-declared — "a self-declaration, not build evidence" (adapters.py:287): no independent build attestation (C7 open).

Confinement: reviewer/scanner run in a ReadOnlyScope the runner derives from the story checkout with no parameter to widen it (orchestrate/adapters.py:1-4; client.session confine, workspace_writable=False for the model reviewer).

Open structural gap: this gate runs ONLY inside the kernel's own story-runner loop (orchestrate/review.py:33). There is no standalone entrypoint that grades an arbitrary external agent's tree/diff against the approved bundle — the B5 "reposition" is unbuilt; the verify/anchor/preserve machinery is the reusable core it would call.

**Relevant IDs:** D-002 / RFC §25 (IMPLEMENTED: model review alone never blocks — Finding.blocks needs probe-corroboration; review_verify + adapters.Reviewer), V2.1 proposal C (IMPLEMENTED: verified findings, anchor, artifact — review_verify.py), PM-8 diagnostics sink (IMPLEMENTED: adapters.py:132,304; verify entries= plumbing), C3a no_progress note (IMPLEMENTED [x] c3429f6e: adapters.py:83-85), G1 leak-tripwire (IMPLEMENTED [x] fdd9c35b: adapters.py:238-242, OCR serve path), G2 fence client / D4 pin-at-use (IMPLEMENTED [x] 76fd9cd8: OCR environment() + review_ocr.pinned at adapters.py:203-206), B-03 round-3 item 5 statement+artifact (IMPLEMENTED: review_verify.detail/preserve/artifact_problems), B5 independent acceptance-gate entrypoint 'grade this tree/diff per approved bundle' for any agent's PR (STILL NEEDED — unchecked, Wave 5, QĐ #4=yes; verify/anchor/preserve core exists but gate runs only inside the story runner, no standalone entrypoint), C8 ClientAdapter 4-method protocol argv/config_endpoint/parse_stream/identity (STILL NEEDED — unchecked, Wave 4; Developer/Reviewer call client.session with opencode hard-wired), C7 cross-session model attestation (STILL NEEDED — unchecked, Wave 4; relates to the self-declared OCR identity gap adapters.py:287), E8 executor-seam attestation (REMOVED this cycle unless B5 approved — dependency note), within_s anchoring OPEN owner decision (review_verify.py:44-51 — undecided)

**Defects:**
- **[P2]** `aisef2/app/review_verify.py:300 (+ docstring 44-51)` — Anchoring excludes within_s from the value comparison (keys = sorted(k for k in c.observable if k != 'within_s')), but verify() still passes the full observable to the probe. A reviewer can pick an unmeetably small window (within_s: 0.001) that a correct product legitimately cannot keep; the class table makes it REFUTED → CONFIRMED here, and it still ANCHORS, so under fix-loop it blocks a correct candidate.  
  *Impact:* False-blocking finding: the agent-independent gate can reject a correct implementation on a timing window the reviewer, not the clause, chose. Self-acknowledged as an OPEN owner decision (docstring 48-51: LedgerLock R-9/3 blocks at within_s:0.001, not at 20) — undecided, not yet closed.  
  *Repro/test:* Feed a finding whose reproducer observable is {exit_code: 3, within_s: 0.001} citing a clause whose approved contracts expect exit_code 3; anchor() returns ANCHORED (within_s dropped from keys) and verify() returns CONFIRMED (window expired) → blocks() True under fix-loop.
- **[P3]** `aisef2/app/adapters.py:233` — (work / 'home').mkdir(parents=True) has no exist_ok=True. The log-exists guard above (adapters.py:228-231) only covers the .jsonl log, not the work dir. A leftover review/<story>.<attempt>/ from a prior OCR run that died before writing the log makes mkdir raise FileExistsError, which is neither ProviderOutage nor CapabilityUnrunnable.  
  *Impact:* An environment leftover escapes review.py's typed-exception handling (review.py:34-39) and surfaces as an unhandled exception instead of a typed ENVIRONMENT refusal, violating the module contract that a capability that cannot run raises CapabilityUnrunnable. Narrow (attempt# normally increments between retries, giving a fresh path), but a latent fail-open-to-crash trap.  
  *Repro/test:* Create <logs>/review/<story>.<attempt>/home, leave <logs>/<story>.reviewer.<attempt>.jsonl absent, call Reviewer._ocr → FileExistsError propagates past review.review().
- **[P3]** `aisef2/app/review_verify.py:302,308,294` — anchor() recomputes _approved_by_clause(project) twice (full scan of project.plan.obligations each) at lines 302 and 308, and reproducer_contract() again at 294 after adapters._verify already built it. All per finding.  
  *Impact:* Redundant O(obligations) work doubled on every finding; no correctness effect. Pure efficiency / ponytail cleanup — compute the clause map once and thread the already-built contract through.  
  *Repro/test:* Static: two calls to _approved_by_clause within one anchor() invocation; verify() and anchor() each call reproducer_contract(finding,...) independently.
- **[P3]** `aisef2/app/adapters.py:286-287` — OCR reviewer identity recorded as declared_by: 'the binary's own version output: a self-declaration, not build evidence'. The grade authority (which reviewer bytes ran) rests on sha256-of-bytes pin + self-report only; there is no independent build/provenance attestation.  
  *Impact:* Trust-boundary limitation, honestly documented: a pinned-but-unattested reviewer binary is accepted. Closing it is exactly what C7 (cross-session attestation) and the C8 raw-API adapter are scoped to do; both are unchecked on the roadmap.  
  *Repro/test:* N/A (design attestation gap) — surfaced in RUN.json s['identity'] on every OCR review.

### mutation/efficiency lane

*Files inspected (10):* `validation/v2/mutation.py`, `validation/v2/checker_calibration.py`, `validation/v2/currency_dag.py`, `validation/v2/version_forward.py`, `validation/v2/b1_bindings_check.py`, `validation/v2/execution_order.py`, `docs/v2.3/BASELINE.md`, `docs/ROADMAP-2.2-CHECKLIST.md`, `docs/IMPROVEMENT-ROADMAP.md`, `closure-evidence/v2/v2.2/B1-MUTATION-EVIDENCE-REUSE-RULING.json`

**Module map.** PURPOSE: re-derive-and-compare evidence gate for AISEF mutation testing + its cross-record currency plumbing. Nothing in a committed record is trusted; the checker recomputes digests from the live tree and git.

mutation.py (2734 lines) — the runner + verdict. Entry points: check(root) mutation.py:2616 (all phase records current+every target killed/audited) → problems_of mutation.py:2602 → target_problems mutation.py:2568 → _problems_v2 mutation.py:2519 (runner-2 verdict). run_target mutation.py:2419 measures one target; main() `run` mutation.py:2653 drives a campaign (one scratch copy mutation.py:2692, record written after every target 2714). Mutation operators: mutants() mutation.py:1720 (AST ops in _SWAP 1549 + emit/twin 1742; comparison/bool/const/enum/dict/list/if/return/sorted); null_mutant mutation.py:2369; _root mutation.py:1689 (function OR module-level table OR SCRIPT/fn child-script targets). Kill judgement: _mutant 2292 runs each kill file in order, _confirm 2268 brackets a FAIL (two null PASS + mutant re-FAIL same signature BRACKET 2265), _remeasure 2309 on a fresh tree, mutant_class 2253, _confirmed 2505. EFFICIENCY knobs: TIMEOUT=900 mutation.py:70 per kill file; one worker-time budget via release ladder (RELEASE_WAIT_S/_earlier 2002, _settle 1981); execution_order table only reorders WHICH reproduced FAIL comes first, never the verdict (kill_tests stays declared, ORD-4). Target tables P1_TARGETS..S2_TARGETS..V21_TARGETS assembled into PHASE_TARGETS mutation.py:1305 / TARGETS 1440; SUPERSEDED/V21_RETIRED 1283 keep historical entries un-rechecked (superseded() 2595). Survivor handling: NO_AUDIT 1442 (never audit-excused), AUDITED 1447 (by-description, refmodel only), positional SURVIVOR-AUDITS.json via audits_at 1497/audit_of 1535.

WHOLE-MODULE INVALIDATION: source_sha256 = source_digest(whole module text) run_target mutation.py:2439; the change-check reads the ENTIRE module file and compares (_problems_v2 mutation.py:2524, target_problems 2578). Editing ANY function in a module invalidates EVERY target in it → must re-run. This is the lane's core efficiency cost and exactly what roadmap F5 targets.

VERIFIED_REUSE (one-off 42-target escape, owner ruling 2026-11-20 Option C, commit abbd88b5) mutation.py:1557-1645: a fail-closed override of whole-module invalidation for 42 aisef2/app/bundle_cli.py targets across the single pinned source transition c15d5b10→0fdfc022 (only _create changed, open(...,newline=\"\") POSIX no-op). verified_reuse_current mutation.py:1623 requires: target in the 42; recorded source_sha256==MEASURED; recorded tree_sha256==authorized per-target Linux worker tree; live file digest==CURRENT; ruling file present; AND the measurement commit b1fb8580's own bundle_cli.py re-hashes to MEASURED (positive git provenance 1644).

checker_calibration.py — WP-0.5 Q0: 57-checker REGISTRY (line 571); each run 3 ways (clean PASS, committed known-bad FAIL for the named reason, always-PASS-mutant must make calibration FAIL) calibrate() 621/run() 641. Wires mutation (_mutation 323), b1_bindings 174, currency_dag 186, version_forward 194, execution_order 418 as calibrated checkers. --check compares committed Q0 record + history_problems() 675.

version_forward.py — owner v2.2 Option C record supersession: bind 65/body_digest 54/validate_chain 87 (base carries no block, each later record self-verifies + supersedes predecessor by LF-normalised digest, duplicate (version_context,kernel_digest) refused), select_for_tree 144 (exact (round,tree), never newest, never fallback), toposort 124 (Kahn, refuses cycle/unknown dep), resolve_upstream 221 / check_supersession 238 / write_supersession 198 (append-only). currency_dag.py — owner v2.2 #1: NODES DAG 46 (10 version-forward nodes + OWNER_GATED 85), validate 101 toposorts then resolves each node's own v2.2 supersession + each upstream edge for the current tree.

b1_bindings_check.py — owner 2026-11-12 §B fail-closed risk gate: every manifest target's source sha256 == frozen binding, aisef2 kernel tree == frozen, registry unchanged, else exit 1 (problems() 14). execution_order.py — B-03b: the mutation execution-order table is byte-for-byte what its committed T0 sweep record derives (table() 50 sorts kill files by ascending T0 seconds, stable ties; check() 81 re-derives).

**Trust boundaries.** CORE MODEL: committed mutation records/rulings are UNTRUSTED data; the checker re-derives every source digest from the live working tree and from read-only git (HEAD, blob, tree, show at a pinned commit) and compares — a record cannot assert its own currency. Trusted inputs: the live file bytes, git's answers, and (as owner authority) the presence of specific committed ruling/manifest files.

WHO TRUSTS WHOM: (1) verdict trusts live-tree digest over the record's claim (mutation.py:2524/2578). (2) VERIFIED_REUSE trusts the OWNER RULING file as the authority that permits reusing 42 Linux verdicts at a changed module; it re-pins both source endpoints and the measurement commit's provenance by digest, but trusts the human attestation 'only _create changed / AST-slice isolated' for reusing the OTHER 41 functions' verdicts (not machine-verified; both endpoints are cryptographically pinned, so the diff is fixed). (3) Q0 calibration trusts the checkers' REAL decision functions (runs them, incl. an always-PASS mutant). (4) version_forward/currency_dag trust ONLY an exact (version_context==CURRENT_ROUND 'v2.2', kernel_digest==HEAD:aisef2) match — never 'newest', never silent fallback; a missing/tampered/ambiguous/cross-version record is an explicit LookupError. (5) b1_bindings trusts the FROZEN manifest and fails closed when git can't answer (empty kernel != frozen).

ASSUMPTIONS/SEAMS: execution order is verdict-neutral by construction (kill_tests stays declared). superseded/retired targets are historical and never re-checked (phase isolation). currency_dag's stated guarantee is about EDGES only — per-node own-record currency is delegated to each node's own gated_test, not the DAG verdict (see defect). The W0 baseline (docs/v2.3/BASELINE.md:53) rests on mutation.py --check=PASS, which for the 42 bundle_cli targets rests on VERIFIED_REUSE, whose owner-authority gate is presence-only (see defect).

**Relevant IDs:** F5 (Mutation incremental in CI — NOT implemented: lane still does whole-module invalidation mutation.py:2439/2524; the only reuse is the manual one-off 42-target VERIFIED_REUSE 1557-1645, not the general 'only re-run modules/kill-test closure that changed, reuse sealed via proof()' F5 envisions; BASELINE.md:62 lists F5 as W1 cold-vs-incremental work), F2 (self-declared typed markers / release allowlist — NOT in this lane: F2 lives in validation/qualification/release_qualify.py; none of these 6 files implement marker-derived allowlists; concern mis-scopes F2 here), F4 (3 client.py survivors + 4 equivalence guards — adjacent: survivor accounting here via NO_AUDIT 1442 / AUDITED 1447 / SURVIVOR-AUDITS.json audits_at 1497, but the client.py fix/kill-tests are elsewhere), B-03a / RELEASE-DECISIONS seq 5 (Q0 checker calibration v2.1 record — checker_calibration.py:16,37), B-03b / owner 2026-10-07 step 4 (execution-order table derived from committed T0 sweep — execution_order.py), owner 2026-11-12 §B (B1 fail-closed risk-gate bindings — b1_bindings_check.py), owner v2.2 #1 (cross-record currency DAG, toposort — currency_dag.py, version_forward.toposort 124), owner v2.2 Option C / seq-8 (version-forward record supersession — version_forward.py; VERIFIED_REUSE 'Option C' owner ruling 2026-11-20 — mutation.py:1557), INV-MUTATION-AUTHORITY (destructive-authority sibling; relevant to defect #1 — reuse authority gate is presence-only), V2.2.0-STATUS gate (177/177 targets, BASELINE.md:55) — the frozen baseline these checkers must keep green

**Defects:**
- **[P2]** `validation/v2/mutation.py:1642` — verified_reuse_current only checks the owner ruling file EXISTS (`.is_file()`); it never verifies the file is the authentic ruling. The identity constant VERIFIED_REUSE_RULING (mutation.py:1570, == the ruling's own `id` field 'AISEF-V2.2-B1-MUTATION-EVIDENCE-REUSE-RULING') and VERIFIED_REUSE_PLATFORM (1575) are declared but NEVER referenced — the content/identity check they exist for was not wired in.  
  *Impact:* The one-off whole-module-invalidation override for the 42 bundle_cli targets is gated on owner authority that is only presence-verified. The docstring (mutation.py:1628) claims 'the committed ruling must be present' but any file at closure-evidence/v2/v2.2/B1-MUTATION-EVIDENCE-REUSE-RULING.json satisfies it. The digest pins (source endpoints + measurement-commit provenance) still fail-closed, so verdicts cannot be silently changed; but the authority artifact that MAKES the reuse 'owner-authorized' is not identity-bound — weakening of an INV-MUTATION-AUTHORITY-style independent-authority gate.  
  *Repro/test:* Replace the ruling JSON with `{}` (or any content) keeping the path: verified_reuse_current still returns True for all 42 targets (every other check passes) and mutation.py --check still PASSES. Fix: read the file and assert its `id`/record field == VERIFIED_REUSE_RULING (the constant already exists for this).
- **[P2]** `validation/v2/mutation.py:1623` — No test exercises the VERIFIED_REUSE mechanism / verified_reuse_current. grep over tests/ for VERIFIED_REUSE|verified_reuse|REUSE-RULING returns nothing; the calibration `_mutation` known-bad (checker_calibration.py:329) uses a synthetic target that never hits the 42-target path.  
  *Impact:* A security-sensitive, fail-closed, owner-authority reuse path added in the latest commit (abbd88b5) has zero direct unit coverage. A regression that drops any of the 5 fail-closed conditions (identity, either digest, the Linux worker tree binding, measurement-commit provenance) would not be caught by the suite, while that path can flip a 'target changed → re-run' into 'current' for 42 release-gate targets.  
  *Repro/test:* Add tests/v2 cases: (a) a happy path where all 5 conditions hold → True; (b) each condition individually broken → False. None exist today.
- **[P3]** `validation/v2/currency_dag.py:119` — In validate(), a node whose OWN v2.2 supersession is missing gets status 'missing' (LookupError text 'no record declared') and is NOT appended to `problems` (only 'error' is, line 122-123). A leaf node with a v2.2 record expected but no DAG dependents (P10-PROFILE, P10-COHORT, Q5, K-NOWORK) therefore contributes no problem and the DAG reports SOUND.  
  *Impact:* currency_dag's top-level SOUND/problems verdict does not catch a missing own-supersession for dependency-free nodes; detection relies entirely on each node's own gated_test. The validate() docstring is scoped to 'every declared EDGE resolves' (so this is arguably by-contract), but a manifest/consumer reading the SOUND verdict as 'all nodes current' would be misled. Low severity: real per-node currency is still enforced by the node's own gated test.  
  *Repro/test:* Delete a v2.2 supersession file for a leaf node (e.g. P10-PROFILE) on a tree whose kernel has no other binding: currency_dag.validate()['problems'] stays empty for that node (status 'missing'), main() prints 'currency DAG: SOUND', rc 0. Fix: append 'missing' own-supersession to problems, or document that SOUND covers edges only.

### qualification/release

*Files inspected (17):* `validation/v2_release_gate.py`, `validation/qualification/release_rc.py`, `validation/qualification/repro_dist.py`, `validation/qualification/v21_conformance_ruling.py`, `validation/qualification/p5_acceptance.py`, `validation/qualification/c2_closure_audit.py`, `validation/qualification/release_calibration.py`, `closure-evidence/v2/cycle2/RELEASE-DECISIONS.json`, `closure-evidence/v2/release/V2.2.1-STATUS.json`, `closure-evidence/v2/release/V2.2.1-RC1-FREEZE.json` (V2.2.1-STATUS/RC1-FREEZE read at audit time, before the 2.2.1 revert), `closure-evidence/v2/cycle2/C2-HISTORY-FREEZE-CANDIDATE.json`, `closure-evidence/v2/cycle2/C2-FINAL-RUN-HISTORY.json`…

**Module map.** Release/qualification lane = the last gate before production PyPI plus the evidence-record currency checks that must hold on the release tree.

v2_release_gate.py — THE production-publish gate; release.yml runs `main()` (:73) with the tag; `problems(version,...)` (:32) returns why prod PyPI must not receive VERSION: frozen RC exists + version matches + release_rc.check()==[] ; stable X.Y.Z (:51); pyproject==tag (:53); status FINAL_RELEASE_READY==PASS + OPEN_BLOCKERS==[] from V<ver>-STATUS.json (:55-60); latest RELEASE-DECISIONS entry carrying a PRODUCTION_PUBLISH key must be human:owner/AUTHORIZED/VERSION==tag (:61-69). Verified PASS for 2.2.1 on this tree at audit time, before the 2.2.1 revert.

release_rc.py — charter G2 RC freeze. `subject()` (:63) picks VERSION from pyproject and the newest frozen RC; `freeze()` (:94) writes V<ver>-RC<n>-FREEZE.json (source SHA, shipped_trees for SHIPPED=:73, kernel tree+digest via `kernel_digest_at` :81, wheel/sdist sha256 from SHA256SUMS, build epoch); `check()` (:113) re-derives the kernel digest at the RC sha and refuses if HEAD's SHIPPED trees ≠ the RC's; `verify_dist()` (:131) byte-compares dist/ to frozen digests (publish job); `epoch()` (:125). `--check`/gate PASS here.

repro_dist.py — deterministic sdist. `normalize()` (:25) re-packs the tar (members sorted, mtime=epoch, uid/gid/uname/gname cleared, USTAR, contents re-checked :37) and re-gzips with mtime=epoch (:41); staging and prod both run it so the frozen digests reproduce.

p5_acceptance.py — C2-P5 owner acceptance boundary. `approve()`:184 / `verify()`:370 / `record()`:583 / `check()`:629 are the frozen 2.0 path; `reverify(round)`:761 re-derives the boundary literally for v2.1 (B-03a) and v2.2 (Option C); `reverification_problems`:989 / `reverification_v22_problems`:1064 are the currency gates; version-forward writers at :1034/:1053 append under closure-evidence/v2/v2.2 via validation/v2/version_forward.py. `main()`:1081.

v21_conformance_ruling.py — v2.1 behaviour-change ruling vs the frozen cycle-2 record. `derive()`:327 builds one ROW per file c2_orchestration_repair.check reports under (d) that 2.1 changed; `check()`:474 re-derives and names drift; `tolerated()`:279 is what c2_orchestration_repair may accept; `write_v22`/`check_version_forward`:492/506 are the v2.2 supersession API. `main()`:527.

c2_closure_audit.py — cycle-2 final closure audit (evidence only, STOPPED-before-acceptance). `build()`:756 assembles triage/orch/freeze + a 25-item acceptance `tests` dict whose verdict STOPS on any False or release blocker; `history_freeze()`:715 freezes per-phase history digests; `gate_of()`:703; `check()`:942 re-binds the committed records. Not referenced by any CI/test (docs only).

release_calibration.py (adjacent root cause) — `RECORD_REL = newest_rel(_version())[0]` (:57) is derived from the live pyproject version; consumed by p5_acceptance.reverify (:772,896-898) and v21_conformance_ruling (:402).

**Trust boundaries.** Instruction/authority source = the committed owner decision ledger closure-evidence/v2/cycle2/RELEASE-DECISIONS.json, keyed by approver=="human:owner". The whole lane trusts that release-branch commits are owner-controlled; it does NOT verify signatures — a committer who can write the branch can forge a V<ver>-STATUS.json and a decisions entry with approver "human:owner" and pass the gate. That is the governance boundary, assumed out of scope by design.

The byte-integrity boundary is real and sound: the published artifact is pinned not by trust but by release_rc freeze (kernel digest recomputed from the RC commit's git objects, release_rc.py:116) + verify_dist byte compare (:131) + repro_dist determinism, so a moved shipped file is caught (release_rc.check 'moved', :119-121).

Revocation convention (fragile but documented): v2_release_gate only considers ledger entries containing a PRODUCTION_PUBLISH key (v2_release_gate.py:61). A later owner HOLD/revoke expressed under a different decision key is invisible, so an earlier AUTHORIZED still wins — a revocation MUST reuse the PRODUCTION_PUBLISH key.

Evidence/history records are append-only + content-addressed (full_history_digest prefix property; approvals aggregate_digest; _write_once refuses rewrites; writers refuse an uncommitted aisef2). reverify/ruling compare literally, no rebind / no engine-digest tolerance (owner B-03a).

Record-currency tests are gated by tests/v2/sealing_gate.py: @sealing skips tree-binding tests unless AISEF_SEALING=1, and its own rule (:10-11) says a RED non-gated test is a real finding (defect 1 is exactly that).

Assumption that bites: the v2.2 round pins currency to CURRENT_ROUND="v2.2" (version_forward.py:37) but the probe-calibration sub-pointer is pinned to the live pyproject version — the 2.2.1 patch bump decoupled them (defect 1). External dependency: c2_closure_audit.build() calls `gh pr view 6` and `git ls-remote origin` with check=True (:774,776), so --write hard-fails offline.

**Relevant IDs:** Charter G2 (RC freeze: release_rc.py, repro_dist.py), Charter G8/§10/§19/§22 (production-publish gate: v2_release_gate.py), Charter G9 (owner RELEASE-DECISIONS ledger), Charter §11/§18 (reproducible sdist: repro_dist.py), S4/S6/S7 (RC freeze, smoke preregistration, clean-install rebuild), RELEASE-DECISIONS seq 3 (V2.0 PRODUCTION_PUBLISH), seq 4 = B-01 ARCHITECTURE-EXCEPTION-V2-007, seq 5 = B-03a/B-03b/B-03c/B-03d requalification round (p5_acceptance, v21_conformance_ruling), seq 7 (V2.1.0 PRODUCTION_PUBLISH, Option 1 scoped gate), seq 8 = AISEF_V2_2_VERSION_FORWARD_QUALIFICATION / Option C (version_forward, write_v22, reverify round=v2.2), seq 9 (V2.2 release mutation-scope amendment), seq 10 (V2.2 final-close-blockers parallel execution), seq 11/12/13 (V2.2.0 RC/RC2 and V2.2.1 PRODUCTION_PUBLISH AUTHORIZED — seq 13 is the one v2_release_gate 2.2.1 verifies) [W0-freeze: historical; 2.2.1 abandoned, its seq 13 withdrawn; ledger holds seq 1–12; the v2.3 ROUND-AUTH seq 13 is a draft only, docs/v2.3/drafts/LEDGER-SEQ-13-DRAFT.md], Cycle-2 §2–§10 (c2_closure_audit triage/orch/freeze/acceptance), QP-2.6 (Q0–Q3 candidate) and authorized successors LANE-Q QP-2.7/2.8/2.9, LANE-X WP-2.10.1/2.10.2 (p5_acceptance.record), STILL NEEDED: a V2.2.1 (or CURRENT_ROUND-pinned) PROBE-CALIBRATIONS record to re-green the v2.2 version-forward currency gate (defect 1); for a v2.3 release, bump version_forward.CURRENT_ROUND to v2.3 with its own version-forward records and a fresh V2.3 qualification baseline + STATUS + RC freeze for v2_release_gate

**Defects:**
- **[P1]** `validation/qualification/p5_acceptance.py:896-898 (root cause validation/qualification/release_calibration.py:57)` — The 2.2.1 version bump repointed the version-derived probe-calibration evidence pointer to a file that was never written, turning the v2.1/v2.2 re-verification currency gates and their non-gated unit tests RED on release/v2.3-big.  
  *Impact:* release_calibration.RECORD_REL = newest_rel(_version())[0] is derived from pyproject (now 2.2.1), so it resolves to closure-evidence/v2/release/V2.2.1-PROBE-CALIBRATIONS.json, which does not exist (only V2.2.0-PROBE-CALIBRATIONS.json does). reverify() (both rounds) then emits a spurious 'V2.2.1-PROBE-CALIBRATIONS.json is missing' and v21_conformance_ruling.derive() (:402) yields 'stale: identity.behaviour_evidence.probe_calibrations'. The committed v2.2 reverification record points at V2.2.0 and can no longer re-derive. aisef2/app/calibrations.py bytes are unchanged since the v2.2 records (sha 14285dcf…), so this is purely the version-pointer coupling, not a real calibration change. tests.v2.test_c2_p5_acceptance.V21Records has 2 non-gated failures (sealing_gate: a red non-gated test is a real finding); the `unittest discover` job in tests.yml:59 runs on every commit, so the suite is red on the release branch; p5_acceptance.py --check-v22 and v21_conformance_ruling.py --check-v22 both FAIL. Fix: write the V2.2.1 calibration record (identical bytes to V2.2.0's) or pin the pointer to CURRENT_ROUND rather than the pyproject version.

- **[P2]** `validation/qualification/c2_closure_audit.py:721-726 (write) vs :948-950 (check), record rule :750-751` — The C2-FINAL history-freeze digest was frozen over 43 working-tree entries that were never committed, so c2_closure_audit.py --check is structurally unsatisfiable for C2-FINAL (permanent false integrity alarm).  
  *Impact:* history_freeze() records entry_count=len(working-tree entries)=43 and a digest over them, but the record's own rule (:750-751) says the carrying commit's gate attempts 'cannot be in the record it carries'; the committed C2-FINAL-RUN-HISTORY.json held 27 entries at write time and 35 now, never 43. check() slices entries[:43] (yielding ≤35) and compares to the 43-entry digest, which can never match. All 12 other frozen histories MATCH; only C2-FINAL is broken. Dormant: the audit is not wired into any CI/test (only docs reference it) and the record is a superseded STOPPED-before-acceptance artifact, but it misleads anyone re-verifying cycle-2 closure evidence.  
  *Repro/test:* python3 -P validation/qualification/c2_closure_audit.py --check  -> 'C2-FINAL: the first 43 entries are not the frozen ones' (exit 1). A per-row scan shows C2-FINAL entry_count=43 vs committed_total=35; every other phase matches.
- **[P3]** `validation/qualification/p5_acceptance.py:782,804,851` — reverify() dereferences the 2.0 history records (prop['specs'], accepted['verification']) with no None guard, so a missing OUT_REL/PROPOSAL_REL raises an opaque TypeError instead of the lane's intended clean 'problem' string.  
  *Impact:* accepted, prop = _json_or_none(OUT_REL), _json_or_none(PROPOSAL_REL) can be None, but :804 `for s in prop['specs']` and :851 `accepted['verification'][...]` assume they exist — unlike history_problems()/proposal_history_problems() which deliberately guard ('a missing record is its blob problem above, never an exception'). Inconsistent fail-closed behavior across the lane; low severity because the 2.0 records are always-committed history, so it does not trigger in practice.  
  *Repro/test:* Rename closure-evidence/v2/cycle2/C2-P5-ACCEPTANCE.json or P5-PLAN-V2.2-PROPOSAL.json and call p5_acceptance.reverify(run_guards=False) -> TypeError, vs history_problems() which returns a clean list.

### ci/release

*Files inspected (23):* `.github/workflows/tests.yml`, `.github/workflows/staging.yml`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `.github/workflows/qualification.yml`, `.github/workflows/conformance.yml`, `.github/workflows/b1-preflight.yml`, `.github/workflows/b1-linux.yml`, `.github/workflows/b1-windows.yml`, `.github/workflows/b1-rerun.yml`, `.github/workflows/win-oskill-repro.yml`, `.github/workflows/diagnostic.yml`, `.github/workflows/pins.yml`…

**Module map.** 12 workflows. THREE lanes:

(1) MANDATORY CI — tests.yml. Jobs: `unit` (tests.yml:9) matrix ubuntu 3.11-3.14 + windows-latest/3.11 + macos-latest/3.12 (tests.yml:18-26), fetch-depth:0 (tests.yml:37), runs the suite inside the OS-owned process range via owned_run.py (WP-4.2 leak discipline, tests.yml:57-59), then v1_evidence_guard --check post-step (WP-0.4, tests.yml:64). The v2.1.0-only scoped qualification branch (tests.yml:56-57) runs release_qualify.py ONLY for the exact `v2.1.0` tag; every other ref (incl. a future v2.3.0 tag) runs raw `unittest discover` (tests.yml:58-59). `sealing` job (tests.yml:75, if master||tag, tests.yml:76) runs full suite with AISEF_SEALING=1 — record-currency tier, honest-RED on unsealed trees. `lint` ruff==0.16.4 (tests.yml:93-101). `wheel` (tests.yml:108): reproducible build at frozen epoch (release_rc --epoch), repro_dist.py, packaging_check (exactly aisef+aisef2), clean-venv install, installed_smoke + installed_product_smoke + `aisef init`.

(2) RELEASE PATH — staging.yml (non-publishing) + release.yml (publishing). staging.yml `build` (staging.yml:24) builds sdist→wheel reproducibly, records SHA256SUMS, uploads `dist`+`dist-sha256`; `install` (staging.yml:59) re-verifies sums then clean-installs on ubuntu+windows, product smoke skipped on Windows (shebang fake-client POSIX-only, staging.yml:106). release.yml triggers ONLY on `v[0-9]+.[0-9]+.[0-9]+` (release.yml:29); jobs: `stable-tag` regex re-check (release.yml:45), `tests`/`staging` via workflow_call (release.yml:51-57), `v2-gate` runs validation/v2_release_gate.py (release.yml:69), `publish` needs all four, `environment: pypi` + id-token OIDC trusted publishing (release.yml:76-78), re-verifies sums + release_rc --verify-dist against the frozen RC (release.yml:88-96) then pypa/gh-action-pypi-publish. v2_release_gate.py gate = pyproject==tag ∧ frozen-RC bound to HEAD ∧ V<ver>-STATUS.json FINAL_RELEASE_READY==PASS with []  blockers ∧ latest RELEASE-DECISIONS PRODUCTION_PUBLISH ruling is human:owner/AUTHORIZED/==version (v2_release_gate.py:32-70; fail-closed, version-scoped via status_rel v2_release_gate.py:25-27).

(3) OUT-OF-BAND — qualification.yml (push to hardening/systematic-v1 or release/** with `[qualify]`, qualification.yml:8-11; Q0-Q3 on ubuntu 3.11-3.14 + win/3.11, owned-range + evidence guard), conformance.yml (weekly cron paid Claude-CLI conformance + V1 release-gate freshness, conformance.yml:9-32), b1-*.yml mutation gates (workflow_dispatch, isolation-guarded: opencode-absent + scratch XDG), win-oskill-repro.yml / diagnostic.yml / pins.yml (manual/scheduled probes).

**Trust boundaries.** - PRODUCTION PUBLISH BOUNDARY: the only path to production PyPI is release.yml `publish` (release.yml:73). Authority chain = tag-regex trigger → stable-tag re-check → tests+staging green → v2-gate (3 evidence predicates) → `environment: pypi` deployment gate. OIDC trusted publishing (id-token:write, release.yml:78) means no PyPI token in repo/secrets; the publisher binding (repo `nghinh/aisef`, workflow release.yml, env pypi) lives on PyPI, one-time human-configured (release.yml:21-24). INTENDED final human stop = a Required reviewer on the `pypi` environment — this is a GitHub repo setting, NOT expressible in YAML. Per docs/v2.3/RELEASE-GOVERNANCE-FINDING.md:9-14 the env currently has protection_rules:[] → the interactive gate does NOT exist (REL-GOV-01).
- DECISION-LEDGER vs INTERACTIVE-GATE: v2_release_gate reads RELEASE-DECISIONS.json (a data file in-tree, release.yml v2-gate). The W0 finding (RELEASE-GOVERNANCE-FINDING.md:28-30) explicitly states a ledger entry is NOT an interactive per-run approval; directive §A/§D forbid conflating them.
- FROZEN-RC BYTE BINDING: publish uploads exactly the bytes staging built; release_rc --verify-dist uses dict-equality `have==want` (release_rc.py:131-134, robust to missing/extra/changed files); kernel identity re-checked from the tag's git objects (release_rc.py:113-122). This is the supply-chain integrity boundary.
- SECRETS: only conformance.yml consumes ANTHROPIC_API_KEY (conformance.yml:17), on schedule/dispatch only — never on pull_request, so fork PRs cannot exfiltrate it. tests.yml `pull_request:` (tests.yml:5) has no secret use. pins.yml uses GITHUB_TOKEN read-only (pins.yml:28).
- PLATFORM-PARITY (intentional): Windows end-to-end `aisef run` is deliberately NOT claimed — staging skips product smoke on Windows (staging.yml:106) and charter §2 (RELEASE-CHARTER.md:48-49) forbids promoting a platform leg on a native-Windows UNIT pass alone. Windows coverage = unit suite only (tests.yml:21-22). This is governance-consistent, not a gap.
- MUTATION ISOLATION: b1-* enforce opencode-absent-on-PATH + scratch XDG before any mutant runs (b1-preflight.yml:23/64, b1-linux.yml:27, b1-windows.yml:25) — prevents mutants invoking the real paid client. GitHub default bash is `-eo pipefail`, so the `rc=$?`-after-pipe idiom in b1-preflight.yml:46-48 still fails the step on mutation failure (checked; not a bug).

**Relevant IDs:** REL-GOV-01, BUILD-REPRO-01, F1, F5, F2, G1, G2, G4, G8, WP-4.2, WP-0.4, A2, E1, E2, E3, E4, D1, D2, D3, C7, B2, B4, B6

**Defects:**
- **[P1]** `.github/workflows/release.yml:73-97 (env at :76) + docs/v2.3/RELEASE-GOVERNANCE-FINDING.md:9-30` — REL-GOV-01 (already found): the `pypi` deployment environment has protection_rules:[] (no Required reviewer), so the `publish` job never pauses. Any tag matching v[0-9]+.[0-9]+.[0-9]+ that passes tests+staging+v2-gate auto-publishes to production PyPI with no human click.  
  *Impact:* Irreversible production publish with no interactive owner gate — the charter §19/§2 (RELEASE-CHARTER.md:44-45) intended stop does not exist. In-flight 2.2.1 (ledger seq 13 AUTHORIZED) will auto-publish [W0-freeze: historical; 2.2.1 abandoned, its seq 13 withdrawn; ledger holds seq 1–12; the v2.3 ROUND-AUTH seq 13 is a draft only, docs/v2.3/drafts/LEDGER-SEQ-13-DRAFT.md]; a future v2.3.0 auto-publishes the moment V2.3.0-STATUS.json=PASS and a ledger AUTHORIZED 2.3.0 entry exist. Remediation is a repo-settings change (owner/admin), not YAML; held per directive §D.  
  *Repro/test:* GET /repos/nghinh/aisef/environments/pypi → protection_rules:[]. Dry-run gate: after adding a Required reviewer, a stable tag must leave `publish` in status:waiting.
- **[P2]** `docs/v2.3/RELEASE-CHARTER.md:38-41 vs .github/workflows/tests.yml:145-146 and staging.yml:105-110 (coverage in tests/installed_product_smoke.py:84-86)` — v2.3 mandatory release gates NOT wired into CI: charter §2 requires offline end-to-end `aisef run` AND `aisef demo` AND signed `--verify --expect-pubkey` (+ expected-bad controls) AND a versioned-evidence read of historical 2.2 runs. Current product smoke exercises only `aisef run` --check/run/--verify; there is no `aisef demo`, no --expect-pubkey/signed-head control, and no 2.2-evidence read in any gate.  
  *Impact:* The release gate does not yet enforce the full v2.3 claim surface (demo usability B4/B6/G4; signed/authentic evidence A2/E1/E2/E4/E3; compatibility read of 2.2 bundles). A v2.3 tag could pass CI without these being proven. This is a W1 'still needs', not a regression.  
  *Repro/test:* grep the five target workflows for demo/expect-pubkey/versioned → only release_rc --verify-dist and `aisef run --verify` appear; no `aisef demo` or `--expect-pubkey` step exists.
- **[P2]** `validation/qualification/repro_dist.py:41 + .github/workflows/tests.yml:114 (py3.11) vs staging.yml:30 (py3.12) + docs/v2.3/RISK-REGISTER.md:10` — BUILD-REPRO-01: the sdist .tar.gz is not byte-reproducible across the build host's Python minor (gzip/zlib stream differs); repro_dist.py normalizes tar metadata and gzip mtime but re-compresses with compresslevel=9 whose bytes depend on the interpreter's zlib. The two CI build legs use different interpreters: tests.yml `wheel` builds on 3.11, staging/release builds on 3.12.  
  *Impact:* The advertised 'reproducible build / two independent rebuilds byte-identical' holds only on a pinned interpreter. A frozen-from-non-CI sdist digest would make release.yml `publish` fail at release_rc --verify-dist AFTER (future) approval. Worked around today by freezing digests only from a CI staging build; W1/F5 should pin the build interpreter or make repro_dist emit a host-independent gzip + add a two-minor rebuild-equality test.  
  *Repro/test:* Measured at the 2.2.1 cut: local CPython-3.14 sdist b1675af0… vs CI-3.12 sdist 7a3462bc… for identical source+SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH; wheel identical 8e1d2928… both (RISK-REGISTER.md:10).
- **[P3]** `.github/workflows/conformance.yml:9-30 (cron :10, key :17, claude step :29-30)` — A scheduled PAID job: conformance.yml runs every Monday 03:00 UTC against secrets.ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, installing the real Claude Code CLI and running 5 live conformance tests, unattended, with no in-workflow spend cap or ledger (only timeout-minutes:40 bounds it).  
  *Impact:* Ongoing unmetered paid-API spend outside the v2.3 economics discipline (charter §4 / RELEASE-CHARTER.md:66: zero new paid trials without an owner-approved scoped cap + spend ledger). Pre-existing, not a v2.3 regression, but the cron fires regardless of release state and is not reflected in any spend ledger.  
  *Repro/test:* conformance.yml has `schedule: cron "0 3 * * 1"` + `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` + `npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code`; no cap/ledger step present.
- **[P3]** `.github/workflows/diagnostic.yml:7-9 vs qualification.yml:8` — diagnostic.yml triggers only on branch `hardening/systematic-v1`, while qualification.yml was updated to also run on `release/**`. So `[diagnose]` real-path reproducers cannot run on the current release/v2.3-big branch.  
  *Impact:* Minor parity gap: the targeted ORCH_9/10 diagnostic reproducer is unavailable on the v2.3 release branch; a diagnosis would require pushing to the legacy hardening branch. Low impact (legacy probe tool).  
  *Repro/test:* diagnostic.yml `on.push.branches: [hardening/systematic-v1]` only; a `[diagnose]` commit on release/v2.3-big triggers nothing.

### legacy-boundary

*Files inspected (17):* `aisef/cli/parser.py`, `aisef/cli/__init__.py`, `aisef/cli/implement.py`, `aisef/cli/__main__.py`, `aisef/__main__.py`, `aisef/_compat.py`, `aisef/control/budget.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/seam.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/story_runner.py`, `aisef2/app/run.py`, `aisef2/arch/enums.py`, `validation/v2/gen_migration_table.py`…

**Module map.** The legacy boundary is two separate mechanisms.

(1) CLI DISPATCH — the split lives entirely in aisef/cli/parser.py::main (aisef/cli/parser.py:407-449), the single entry for both the `aisef` console script (pyproject.toml:40 `aisef = "aisef.cli:main"`) and `python -m aisef` (aisef/__main__.py, aisef/cli/__main__.py). main() hand-scans a leading `--project` (parser.py:414-415) then intercepts exactly three tokens BEFORE building the V1 parser: `run`→aisef2.app.cli.run (parser.py:416-425, lazy import, prints a typed error if aisef2 missing), `bundle`→aisef2.app.bundle_cli.main (426-428), `demo`→aisef2.app.demo.main (429-431). Everything else falls to the V1 argparse tree build_parser(). V1 execution survives only behind the named `legacy` subparser (parser.py:194-229): `legacy run`→cmd_run (aisef/cli/implement.py:266 → aisef.phases.run.run_sprint/run_verify_only), `legacy improve`→cmd_improve (implement.py:348 → aisef.phases.improve.improve). Per CHANGELOG.md:80-81 (2.0.0) every OTHER V1 command (plan, mockup, gates/approve/reject/review/change, verify, tool, qa, devsecops, pre-deploy, evidence, ctx, issues, report, cost, doctor, init/setup/baseline/compile/doc/guard/skill/gate/replay, closure, status, dashboard, memory) is unchanged V1. So V2 owns only the execution verb; the whole surrounding lifecycle stays V1.

(2) V2-SIDE COMPAT SEAM — aisef2/orchestrate/seam.py is the only place aisef2 names a V1 concept. admit_legacy (seam.py:48-55) is called once in story_runner.run_story (aisef2/orchestrate/story_runner.py:170) before a story begins; resolve() (seam.py:34-45) maps each V1 proof mode to a V2 (ObligationRole, Polarity, SubjectAbsence) via a migration table and raises SeamRefusal for a row needing a human declaration — before any journal event or provider call. The table's ONLY producer is validation/v2/gen_migration_table.py (reads aisef/control/obligation.py as AST source, never imports it; gen_migration_table.py:464). In the shipped runtime the seam is DORMANT: StoryInputs is built at aisef2/app/run.py:681 without legacy/declarations/migration_table, so admit_legacy hits `if not modes: return ()` (seam.py:51-52). WP-6.2 built it; WP-6.3 removes it (seam.py:1).

COMPAT/MIGRATION proper: `aisef bundle migrate` (aisef2/app/bundle_cli.py:366,1109) is intra-V2 across-release re-binding (ARCHITECTURE-EXCEPTION-V2-007), NOT V1→V2. aisef/_compat.py is OS (Win/Unix) syscall shims (flock, killpg, atomic_replace), unrelated to the V1/V2 line.

**Trust boundaries.** ONE-WAY and clean: aisef2 imports zero aisef — verified by grep (no `from aisef`/`import aisef.` in aisef2/*.py) and enforced by tests/v2/test_p6_orchestration.py:520-521 ("the frozen V1 kernel is never imported") plus test_old_path_audit.py. story_runner.py:6 states "No V1 code is consulted; the only legacy entry is the seam."

State separation: V2 `aisef run` writes its own hash-chained journal and NEVER writes V1's `_bmad-output/` (grep for `_bmad-output` in aisef2 is empty). V1 commands (budget, report, pre-deploy, evidence, closure) read/write `_bmad-output/`. The migration-table generator treats V1 strictly as read-only AST text (gen_migration_table.py:464 "nothing under aisef/ is imported or written"; sha256-pins the V1 kernel).

Packaging boundary: pyproject ships only `aisef` + `aisef2` (pyproject.toml:47); the sole migration-table producer lives in validation/ (not shipped) — consistent with the seam being runtime-dormant. BASELINE.md:28-29 fixes the inventory (aisef2=94 files, aisef legacy V1=109 files) and declares (BASELINE.md:41) per-module trust boundaries are automated counts needing this audit.

Dispatch trust: main() trusts argv only; a leading `--project DIR` is forwarded verbatim to V2 (parser.py:425). Assumption: the first non-`--project` token decides V1-vs-V2 routing; `legacy`/all other verbs can never be shadowed by V2 because V2 claims only the exact tokens run/bundle/demo.

**Relevant IDs:** G1 (V2 is the authoritative `aisef run`; parser.py:184,219), WP-6.1 (migration table), WP-6.2 (compat seam, built), WP-6.3 (seam removal, pending), RFC-§31, RFC-§7, RFC-§10.2, RFC-§11, F4, F6, ADR-004-R3 (legacy improve), ADR-004-R13 (legacy run --verify-only), D2 (driver-led vs agent-led), ARCHITECTURE-EXCEPTION-V2-007 (bundle migrate), v2.3-RELEASE-CHARTER §4 Compatibility (keep 2.2.0 CLI/bundle/format readers or versioned migration), B4/B6/G4 (shipped-CLI-only operation), BASELINE.md:41 (per-module trust-boundary audit owed)

**Defects:**
- **[P3]** `aisef/control/budget.py:9` — Stale cross-version claim in the budget module docstring: it names `aisef run` as sharing the `_bmad-output/` BudgetLedger ceiling with `aisef legacy improve`, but since 2.0.0 `aisef run` is the V2 runtime (aisef2), which imports none of aisef.control.budget and writes no `_bmad-output/`.  
  *Impact:* Operator-facing: implies a single hard spend/time ceiling bounds concurrent `aisef run` (V2) + `aisef legacy improve` (V1); in fact each version has its own (or no shared) ceiling, so combined spend is unbounded across the split. No functional break — comment only.  
  *Repro/test:* grep confirms aisef2 imports no aisef and writes no _bmad-output; V2 run.py:675 uses its own `budget`, not aisef.control.budget.BudgetLedger.
- **[P3]** `aisef2/orchestrate/seam.py:44` — resolve() else-branch calls SubjectAbsence(row['subject_absence']) (and ObligationRole/Polarity at :45) assuming a matched row is MAPPED/UNMAPPED; a matched row whose reason is UNKNOWN_V1_MODE has None in those fields and raises a raw ValueError instead of the intended typed SeamRefusal.  
  *Impact:* Latent robustness gap on the public boundary API. Not reachable via the committed table (gen_migration_table.problems() at validation/v2/gen_migration_table.py:492-493 rejects unknown rows, and the committed table holds only the 3 real modes → test_p6_orchestration.py:535 gets a clean SeamRefusal 'no row'). Fail-closed but untyped if a caller passes a hand-built/corrupt table.  
  *Repro/test:* seam.resolve('X','X',{'rows':[{'v1_mode':'X','subject_absence':None,'reason':'UNKNOWN_V1_MODE','obligation_role':None,'polarity':None}]}, None) raises ValueError, not SeamRefusal.
- **[P3]** `aisef/cli/parser.py:416` — V1/V2 lifecycle interop gap (needs owner design-intent confirmation): only run/bundle/demo route to V2, while plan/gate/qa/report/pre-deploy/evidence/closure stay V1 and read V1 `_bmad-output/` evidence, but `aisef run` (V2) writes its own journal and no `_bmad-output/`.  
  *Impact:* A wave executed via `aisef run` (V2) does not populate the V1 evidence store, so a subsequent `aisef report` / `aisef pre-deploy` / `aisef evidence` (V1) can read empty/stale results if a user mixes the two. By-design per charter (V2 is authoritative run with its own verify), but the shared CLI namespace invites the mix.  
  *Repro/test:* Confirmed aisef2 writes no `_bmad-output/` (grep empty) while V1 cmd_report/cmd_predeploy (implement.py) read _artifact_root=_bmad-output. End-to-end: `aisef run` then `aisef report` → V1 report lacks V2 story evidence. Needs owner confirmation of intended post-run path.

Other invariant checkers: v1_evidence_guard (WP-0.4; preventive audit hook :232 armed at test import, detective git-blob baseline :149); except_boundaries (WP-5.5; InvariantError uncontainable, audits every try/suppress in aisef2); refmodel_independence (WP-3.5); kernel_static_checks (11 rules); probe_static_checks (6 rules, imports aisef2.probe.catalog); cohort_static_checks (4 rules); authority_limits (B-03c; 4 rules over migrate/verify closure in aisef2/app). checker_calibration.py calibrates all nine (known-bad fixtures).

CI ARMING (measured by running the tests, log <scratch>/gap_auth_run.txt: 30 ran, 11 skipped):
- destructive_authority live check: NOT ARMED in `unit`. tests/v2/p0/test_destructive_authority.py:45 and tests/v2/p5/test_invariants.py:993 are @sealing (GATED tests/v2/sealing_gate.py:30,32) → skipped. The `sealing` job runs only on master/tags (tests.yml:76) and is red by design (master run 44845163565 @c9a3cbb6: sealing=failure). So on release/v2.3-big and PRs it never runs. Live tree currently PASSes when run by hand.
- checker_calibration (non-vacuity of all 9): NOT ARMED. Whole class @sealing (tests/v2/p0/test_checker_calibration.py:26; GATED :29).
- cleanup_authority: armed at runtime by construction (only signal path); its unit tests are not gated.
- refmodel_independence: ARMED (tests/v2/p3/test_reference_models.py:155-156).
- v1_evidence_guard: ARMED. Preventive at import (tests/v2/test_v1_evidence_guard.py:35); detective CI step (tests.yml:64) + Detective.test_repository_matches_the_baseline.
- except_boundaries: ARMED (tests/v2/p5/test_invariants.py:479).
- kernel_static_checks: ARMED (tests/v2/test_p6_orchestration.py:977 all rules; per-rule elsewhere).
- probe_static_checks: ARMED (tests/v2/test_cycle2_wp201.py:82).
- cohort_static_checks: ARMED (tests/v2/test_c2_p10_cohort_static.py:28); only its record test is gated (sealing_gate.py:37).
- authority_limits: ARMED (tests/v2/test_v21_authority_limits.py:52).
The probes behind the defects below are under <scratch>/gap_auth/ (probe_da.py, scope_da.py, probe_eb.py, scan_eb.py, probe_ri.py, probe_ks_ps.py, probe_al.py). They are read-only against the repo and use throwaway temp trees.

**Trust boundaries.** 1) Implementation-under-test or mutant output → host destructive authority. Code under mutation can only REPORT pids or groups. cleanup_authority re-proves each one from its own ps/PowerShell reading (ancestry + birth + group leadership) and treats anything it cannot prove as RESIDUAL_OWNERSHIP_UNKNOWN. This holds on POSIX. On Windows the reader fails open to an empty table (D6), and ppid is never re-parented, which leaves a narrow reuse gap (D13). 2) Developer- or agent-written code → static ledger. destructive_authority is the mechanical guard that every destructive call has a declared authority source. It is currently not run in branch/PR CI (D1). Its derivation check passes when the authority only exists somewhere in the module, not on the code path (D2). Its discovery fails open on aliases and shells (D3), and it does not cover validation/v21 or validation/qualification (D4). 3) Tests → frozen evidence. Only V1 evidence (closure-evidence/ minus v2/) is guarded. v2.x released records, v2.2.0 included, have no mechanical immutability check, and CI does not check the working tree after the suite (D7). 4) Kernel errors → containment. InvariantError derives from BaseException. except_boundaries audits try handlers and suppress(). Tuple aliases, early-return handlers, finally-return and custom __exit__ are not modelled (D8); there is no live instance in aisef2. 5) Checker self-trust. probe_static_checks and except_boundaries import the aisef2 code they check. Their non-vacuity rests on checker_calibration, which is gated out of `unit` (D1).

**Module map.** LINEAGE (lineage.py): claims() :146 lists generations (CONTINUED-BY.json, .N.json; _look :458 turns a listing refusal into LineageRefused). child_evidence() :175 → BEGUN/ENDED_UNBEGUN/UNBEGUN/UNREAD (_in_the_way :208, _without_a_journal :229). claim() :290 reads the newest claim, decides supersession from child state + _gone() :278 (claimant identity = host, pid, `ps -o lstart=` text from process_started() :259), then O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW 0o444 create :335 + write/fsync :346-349. Called once from run.execute at run.py:549 (stage "claim"), BEFORE clone/admission/venv/confinement/preflight; the journal first exists at run.begin() run.py:630, so the child is UNBEGUN for that whole window. reconcile() :389 (bundle_cli.py:995) matches unjournaled main commits/worktree heads to proof/verified candidates. Spend: account_of() :486 (ledger + sessions via client.Budget, in-flight requests :538-548, _journal_mismatch :554, _evidence_gone :604 with RUN.json witness _recorded_spend :573); lineage_account() :708 walks _predecessor() :665 (RUN.started.json then RUN.json `continues.path`) + superseded siblings from claims; consumed by run.py:552-576 (refuse on unread, refuse unaccounted unless --accept-unaccounted), verify.py:84-94, bundle_cli.py:1027. usage_lineage() :787 (run.py:724). write_anchor/read_anchor :833-851 (unsigned; already CODE-AUDIT #10/A2). COHORT: preregistration.py: SHAPES/FROZEN_INPUTS :83-97, DEVELOPMENT_REGRESSION (LedgerLock) :102, runspec_of :120 (re-derive profile), policy_of :136, problems/validate :146-184, frozen_inputs/workload_identity/shared :191-204. lifecycle.py: EvaluationCohort frozen dataclass :47 with __post_init__ consistency checks :57-83; seal :114 (caller-supplied prior), advance :133 (NEXT one step), record_run :149, read_results :159, observe_inputs :165 (rules 2/3, caller supplies current hashes), generalization :184 (_complete :178). No kernel importer; consumers only tests + validation/qualification/c2_p10_cohort.py; run.py:713 hard-codes generalization_claim "NONE".

**Trust boundaries.** (1) Predecessor directory is the trusted writer domain (claims 0o444 but directory writable; chain/anchor unsigned = A2, CODE-AUDIT #10, not re-reported). (2) Process identity boundary: "claimant provably gone" is decided by comparing a recorded `ps lstart` TEXT with a fresh one from a DIFFERENT process/invocation; that text depends on the caller's TZ and LC_* (measured: same pid prints 'Sat Nov 21 09:28:10 2026' default, '02:28:10' under TZ=UTC, 'sam. 21 nov.' under LC_ALL=fr_FR). Assumed-but-unstated invariant: recorder and checker share TZ/locale and hostname uniqueness. (3) Evidence-gone rule (owner decision 2026-10-15, V21-77/V21-78): absent evidence must not read as zero spend; enforced for sessions/stream/ledger and for a named run whose directory is missing, NOT for a named run whose directory exists but was emptied. (4) One-continuation-per-run is enforced per predecessor directory only; nothing checks that a --parent is not itself a superseded child. (5) Cohort: all state (cohort objects, prior set, current input hashes, run ids) is caller-held, in-memory and unauthenticated; the one-way and anti-p-hacking rules hold only along the advance()/seal(prior) API path, not as properties of the value. Preregistration content validation itself is robust (malformed profiles/policies give typed PREREGISTRATION_INVALID: probed, no escape).

#### GAP-FILL quality/anti-gaming: aisef2/quality/{vacuity,relevance,adequacy,test_execution}.py + aisef2/orchestrate/qualit…

*Files inspected (17):* `aisef2/quality/__init__.py`, `aisef2/quality/vacuity.py`, `aisef2/quality/relevance.py`, `aisef2/quality/adequacy.py`, `aisef2/quality/test_execution.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/quality.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/story_runner.py`, `aisef2/orchestrate/workspace.py`, `aisef2/app/run.py`, `aisef2/control/owner.py`, `aisef2/errors.py`, `tests/v2/p5/test_adequacy.py`, `tests/v2/p5/test_vacuity.py`, `docs/v2.3/CODE-AUDIT.md`…

**Module map.** Live path: app/run.py:672 TestsPolicy(True) (blocking) + run.py:681-685 UNITTEST runner, Dependencies(environment=frozenset(), project=product_roots) -> story_runner.py:296-305 assess() at the verifier checkout; q.failure stops the attempt before review and merge. orchestrate/quality.assess (quality.py:49-69) runs, in order: WP-5.1 te.execute on the story tests (quality.py:53-54); WP-5.2 relevance.measure(story_diff(patch)) (:57); WP-5.3 vacuity.evaluate, a second run in a scratch copy with the story's product hunks reversed (:58-59); the regressions via te.execute (:60-61, run even when the story run was UNRUNNABLE); then WP-5.4 adequacy.assemble (:62), the journal row tests/adequacy (:63), and failure TESTS_UNRUNNABLE (outcome None) or TESTS_INADEQUATE (may_block and blocking) (:64-68). test_execution.classify (:195-227) is the only report->TestExecution mapping. _UNITTEST_RUNNER (:257-385) is an in-process stdlib runner: it imports the story test modules, traces lines/arcs with sys.settrace, records file access with an audit hook, and writes its result set to a file. _read_unittest/_read_junit are the adapters. relevance.story_diff (:85-125) and vacuity.file_patches (:76-140) are two independent unified-diff parsers of the same patch. adequacy._defects/_gaps/_adequate (:51-94) give precedence UNRUNNABLE > INADEQUATE > INCOMPLETE > ADEQUATE, and EngineeringTestAdequacy.__post_init__ re-checks it.

**Trust boundaries.** The developer (LLM) is untrusted (CODE-AUDIT.md:263). Story test files and candidate product code are developer-authored. The real boundary is this: test_execution.py:21-23 says the RunnerReport is "harness-produced", but its result_set is written by the same interpreter that imports and runs the untrusted test modules (test_execution.py _UNITTEST_RUNNER), so the subject can forge or withhold it (#80). The probe subsystem MACs protocol lines against exactly this threat (CODE-AUDIT.md:297); the test runner has no equivalent. Only the harness-side fields are trustworthy: launched, exit code, signalled, timed_out (execute). Other boundaries: (1) the patch comes from controller git (workspace.py:156-160, git diff with default rename detection and errors="replace"), so it is trusted for paths but carries U+FFFD for non-UTF-8 text. (2) Vacuity's counterfactual run is not controlled against the candidate run (vacuity.py neutralise/evaluate; #82, detail withheld until the W3 fix). (3) InvariantError is a BaseException (errors.py:8). Subject-shaped data that reaches Case/CollectionError constructors escapes every `except Exception` up to run.py:697. (4) Rule 4 resolves collection failures it cannot attribute to INTEGRATION, which charges nobody and never blocks (adequacy.py:19-22, pinned by tests/v2/p5/test_adequacy.py:118-125).

calibrations.py: 19 generated rows (probe_id, probe_digest, class, fixture paths, demonstrated_at). Read by run.calibrations() (run.py:103-107) and admit (run.py:596; static_admission.py:267 check 9).

orchestrate/security.py: ToolCall in the owned range :39-45, typed report read :48, gate rows :51-54. The shipped scanners are RuffScanner (informational, blocking=False) and NoScanner (app/adapters.py:372-404), so SECURITY_FINDING cannot be reached in production. This is by design and documented.

errors.py: InvariantError(BaseException) carries the raising module through frame 1. No subclass defines __init__ (registry.py:45, routing.py:40, calibration.py:73), so the attribution is correct. No defect.

**Trust boundaries.** (1) Oracle → developer prompt. Holds by construction: the prompt is built only from public facts plus typed failure codes. The tripwire re-scans prompt+env before every session (client.py:324-328). REVIEW rows enter the prompt only when passed is False (feedback.py:86-87), so non-blocking anchor reasons are excluded. Note this depends on a filter that exists for a different purpose (D6).
(2) Oracle → developer-reachable disk. Not enforced unless confinement is on (settings.py:110 defaults to None). The developer cwd is <out>/work/ws/wt-<story> (run.py:631-634). <out>/probe-observations (PM-8) and <out>/review-findings plus the journal sit three levels up. This is the V21-02/D1 class (#21).
(3) Serve-surface tripwire. Substring match on UTF-8 text ≤2 MiB of the working tree. The .git gap is #19. Further fail-open gaps found here: oversize files, non-UTF-8 encodings, format-sensitive composite needles (D1, D2).
(4) Reviewer → verifier checkout. Integrity is enforced by app/adapters.py tree_of/_unchanged (:146, :151, :171), workspace.move checkout --force + clean -ffdxq (workspace.py:149-153) and bwrap read-only when confined (run.py:664). It is not enforced by ReadOnlyScope chmod (D8).
(5) Secrets. Session streams are redacted before read_session (client.py:364-365). Review gate details are redacted (review_verify.detail :436). The diagnostics sink redacts, including JSON-escaped forms (diagnostics.py:141-144). retry_feedback does no redaction of its own but its inputs are already redacted or typed.
(6) Settings. Every value that spends or routes is explicit and checked fail-closed. The key-in-session and egress surfaces are #9 (D3) and #20.

All flagged modules confirmed live (imported on the run path) — not dead code.

A defect here = independence leak or a bad plan admitted.

== CONTRADICTIONS / STRUCTURAL OVERLAPS IN THE PARTITION (double-home or orphan risk) ==
A. "security" spans two packages: aisef2/app/confine.py + app/tripwire.py (the security/confine lane) AND aisef2/orchestrate/security.py (security.py:1-5 — the SECURITY-budget scanner gate). If "orchestrate" claims all of aisef2/orchestrate/, security.py is claimed by both lanes or by neither — verify it was read once.
B. "adapters" double-homes: aisef2/app/adapters.py (adapters.py:1-7) and aisef2/orchestrate/adapters.py (adapters.py:1-4). "adapters/grade" vs "orchestrate" (owns aisef2/orchestrate/) — orchestrate/adapters.py is the overlap.
C. "verify" triple-homes: app/verify.py, app/review_verify.py (a named PRODUCT_TRUTH authority per authority_limits.py), orchestrate/review.py — split across "verify/authenticity", "control/reviewer", "orchestrate". review_verify.py is the one most likely to fall in the seam.
D. "calibration" naming collision across 3 lanes: aisef2/probe/calibration.py (probe capability), app/calibrations.py (shipped data), validation/v2/checker_calibration.py (Q0 checker), validation/qualification/{c2_cli,c2_effect,release}_calibration.py. A map claiming "calibration" is ambiguous about which; confirm each file has exactly one owner.
E. "proof": aisef2/orchestrate/proof.py (candidate+independent verification, proof.py:1-8) vs the "verify/authenticity" lane — the actual two-checkout verifier-disagreement logic lives in orchestrate/, which the "orchestrate" lane may treat as already covered.

== OUT OF MY SCOPE but unlaned (note only; task scoped me to aisef2//validation/) ==
framework/ (15 .py, 2,444 LOC: bench/cost_decomp, memory_compare, memory_rank, pins) and bin/aisef are production-adjacent and named by no lane; framework/pins is referenced by PINS.md conventions. validation/experiments/ (campaign.py, harness.py — paid-run campaign machinery) sits between "mutation/efficiency" and "qualification/release"; confirm one owns it.

Bottom line: the five highest-value unaudited trust surfaces are, in order, aisef2/plan/ (admission+independence), the validation/v2 authority-checker family (self-policing), aisef2/quality/ (test anti-gaming), aisef2/app/lineage.py (continuation authority), aisef2/cohort/ (preregistration seal). None maps cleanly to a named lane; all are live on the run path.
